
=== 2026-06-13 (session 2 — smoke test results + tuning analysis) ===

Smoke test 23751546 completed (50 stations, 2 epochs, all fixes applied).

Root cause of silent logs — conda run stdout buffering:
  Every previous job showed only 3 lines (bash echo) then silence until job exit.
  Root cause: conda run internally pipes child stdout and batches it until process
    exits. All 20+ Python/torchrun/worker processes wrote to the same pipe;
    conda run only forwarded to the SLURM log file at exit.
  PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1 only unbuffers Python's internal buffer — not the kernel pipe.
  Fix: conda run --no-capture-output (-s flag). Streams stdout live. All future
    jobs will show real-time output.
  Also bumped --mem=600G → 700G: full-run DataLoader IPC Shmem could push counted
    memory close to 600G. 700G gives ~170G headroom at no SBU cost (GPU-billed).

Smoke test results:
  L12 preload: 842 stations preloaded in 1247s (21 min) before DDP init.
  RAM at training start: 251.9 GB / 792.3 GB (32% of node).
  Peak VRAM: 24.2 GB / 100 GB per GPU — 76% unused headroom.
  Trainable parameters: 50,052,679.
  Train loss: epoch 1 = -2.1497 → epoch 2 = -2.5059 (NLL, decreasing = learning).
  Val loss:   epoch 1 = -1.0594 → epoch 2 = -0.2848.
  Validation metrics (epoch 2, 10 stations):
    0–10 cm:   ubRMSE=0.061  MAE=0.084  bias=+0.002
    10–30 cm:  ubRMSE=0.050  MAE=0.069  bias=-0.017
    30–100 cm: ubRMSE=0.054  MAE=0.106  bias=+0.071
  Surface ubRMSE 0.061 already below 0.07 target after just 2 epochs / 48 stations.

Step time analysis (GPFS stall signature):
  Batch 1: 27s  — cold GPFS read, all 32 workers hammer metadata servers simultaneously
  Batch 2: 3.9s — partially page-cached
  Batch 3+: 283ms — OS page cache warm, effectively RAM speed
  Stalls at batches 17 (3s), 25 (8s), 33 (8s): DataLoader queue starvation.
    Pattern: 8 workers × prefetch_factor=2 = 16 queued batches. When slowest worker
    hits a new un-cached zarr chunk on GPFS, queue drains in 8×285ms = 2.3s, then
    GPU stalls waiting. Stalls every 8 batches until all chunks page-cached (~batch 34).
  After batch 34: clean 283ms for remainder of epoch 1 and all of epoch 2.
  GPU SM utilisation during compute: 97–100% at 460W. Duty cycle ~60% in steady state
    (GPU active ~150–180ms per 283ms step; remaining is DataLoader + PCIe transfer).

Memory architecture confirmed:
  /dev/shm is 378 GB total (50% of 792 GB node RAM) — not 1 TB as assumed.
  L12 preload uses 182 GB → only 192 GB free. L3+L6+L9 ≈ 546 GB → cannot preload.
  Page cache handles L3/L6/L9: after epoch 1 warmup, zarr reads hit RAM at same speed
    as memmap. Converting zarr → GPFS memmaps provides no benefit (page cache already
    does this). Current architecture is correct and complete for hardware available.
  GPFS memmaps are only beneficial on local storage (NVMe); Snellius H100 nodes have
    no local NVMe — scratch is GPFS network storage.

Worker / prefetch tuning analysis (decided NOT to change):
  Each queued batch costs ~2.5 GB in DataLoader IPC Shmem.
  Current (8w × pf=2): 16 queued batches × 4 ranks = 160 GB IPC Shmem.
  Proposed 10w × pf=3: 240 GB IPC (+80 GB), 108 GB headroom — technically safe.
  Proposed 8w × pf=4: 320 GB IPC (+160 GB), only 28 GB headroom — risky on full run.
  Proposed 12w × pf=8: 960 GB IPC → OOM.
  Decision: keep 8w × pf=2. GPFS stalls in epoch 1 = ~60s total overhead across a
    700-min full run = 0.1% of training time. Not worth RAM risk or added complexity.

Memmap warning fix:
  torch.from_numpy() on mode='r' /dev/shm memmaps triggered PyTorch non-writable
    warning, printing source line 215 of dataset.py 39× in log output.
  Fix: warnings.filterwarnings("ignore", message=".*non-writeable.*") in dataset.py.
    Warning is benign: tok is immediately copied into pre-allocated l12 buffer.

Full run estimate (baseline_nll, ~500 train stations):
  ~2,880 batches/epoch × 283ms = 814s = 14 min/epoch steady state.
  Epoch 1 (cold): ~25–30 min. Epochs 2+: ~14 min each.
  50 epochs estimated → ~12–16 hours total. Well within 120h limit.

Why the original OOM crash (job 23733182) cannot recur:
  Old behaviour: _l12_cache populated lazily per DataLoader worker. Each of 4 ranks
    independently loaded L12 for every station it sampled → progressive AnonPages growth
    until batch ~600 when all stations had been sampled. 600 stations × 300 MB × 4 ranks
    = ~728 GB L12 in AnonPages alone → hit 384G cgroup limit at batch 603.
  New behaviour: rank 0 preloads ALL stations' L12 to /dev/shm before DDP init.
    All 4 ranks read via np.memmap(mode='r') → OS shares one physical copy in Shmem.
    L12 cost: 182 GB Shmem (counted once, not 4×). No lazy loading, no progressive growth.
  L3/L6/L9 zarr reads during training land in OS page cache (reclaimable, not counted
    by SLURM cgroup). RAM profile is flat from batch 1 onward.
  Current full-run memory budget:
    L12 Shmem:         182 GB
    DataLoader IPC:    160 GB
    AnonPages:        ~130 GB
    Total counted:    ~472 GB / 700 GB → 228 GB headroom, no growth expected.

Changes committed: 1310807 (--no-capture-output), b3554db (--mem=700G), e3d512b (warning fix + this log).

Training plan and research priorities:

Phase 1 — Baselines:
  baseline_nll   (job 23752836): NLL log-var loss, uncertainty-aware. Primary model. Running.
  baseline_huber (pending):      Huber loss, point estimate. Submit only after NLL converges
                                 cleanly. Required for paper — reviewers need the comparison.
  Both must run to full convergence (~50 epochs, ~17h each) for a fair comparison.
  If NLL ubRMSE < Huber: "uncertainty improves accuracy."
  If NLL ubRMSE ≈ Huber: "uncertainty at zero accuracy cost."
  Either outcome is publishable; without Huber the NLL contribution is unproven.

Phase 2 — Ablations (after both baselines converge):
  - Skip connection ablation: remove L3/L6/L9 intermediate layers, keep L12 only
  - Frozen decoder: freeze TerraMind ViT, train fusion head only
  - Modality ablation: S2-only vs S2+S1 vs full multimodal

Research questions to answer in priority order:
  1. Does the model learn?          → epoch 1 val ubRMSE < 0.07  (answer in minutes)
  2. Does it converge?              → epochs 20-30, ubRMSE still improving?
  3. Is NLL better than Huber?      → compare converged final metrics
  4. What drives accuracy?          → ablations: skips, S1, ERA5 contribution
  5. Does it generalise?            → test stations, by climate zone, seasonal breakdown

Next: watch epoch 1 val metrics. Everything else depends on that answer.

=== 2026-06-13 ===
Problem: Three consecutive training job crashes before completing a single epoch.

Crash 1 — Job 23733182 (OOM):
  Symptom: SLURM cgroup killed the job at batch 603 after ~2h. Node RAM hit 421GB.
  Root cause: 4 DDP rank processes each loaded a full copy of the dataset. Workers dirtied
    CoW pages progressively (zarr chunk reads), causing burst-style RAM growth from 48GB
    to 421GB, exceeding the 384G cgroup limit.
  Fix: --mem=384G → --mem=600G in slurm/train.sh.

Crash 2 — Job 23749254 (empty log):
  Symptom: Log file had only the job header then silence. Job cancelled immediately.
  Root cause: Python stdout is block-buffered inside `conda run` subprocess; buffer never
    flushed before SIGTERM.
  Fix: export PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1 in slurm/train.sh.

Crash 3 — Job 23750234 (BFloat16 numpy):
  Symptom: TypeError: Got unsupported ScalarType BFloat16 in evaluate() at line 252.
    Job ran 277 batches successfully (2.4× faster) then crashed at the first val epoch.
  Root cause: mu/var tensors from torch.autocast("cuda", dtype=torch.bfloat16) remain
    bfloat16; numpy does not support bfloat16.
  Fix: .float() cast before .numpy() on mu and var in evaluate().

Performance fixes applied (from tensor core utilisation analysis):
  - Added torch.autocast("cuda", dtype=torch.bfloat16) to train_one_epoch and evaluate.
    Before: tensor core utilisation 0.4%, batch time ~724ms.
    After (job 23750234): batch time 283-300ms (2.4×), stalls 6-7× shorter (3-9s vs 30-68s).
  - Removed 4 explicit .float() casts in model.py that upcast fp16 tensors before linears,
    defeating autocast.
  - Re-enabled L12 RAM cache (removed DISABLE_L12_CACHE); added static cache for
    DEM/LULC/soil/token_masks in dataset.py — eliminates per-sample zarr reads for all
    static-per-station data.

RAM fix — /dev/shm shared L12 cache (job 23751109, pending):
  Problem: Full run estimated 660GB peak (614GB data + 46GB prefetch/CUDA overhead),
    exceeding the 600G cgroup limit. Root cause: torchrun spawns 4 independent OS processes,
    each holding its own copy of the L12 cache — no shared memory by default.
  Fix: _preload_l12_to_shm() in train.py — rank 0 writes all L12 arrays to /dev/shm tmpfs
    as numpy memmaps before dataset construction. All 4 ranks open the same files with
    np.memmap(mode='r'); OS shares one physical copy (~400GB saved, peak ~258GB).
    /dev/shm on Snellius H100 nodes ≈ 1TB; we need ~134GB.
  Also added NCCL_TIMEOUT=3600 to prevent NCCL allreduce timeout during zarr stalls.

Files modified: train.py, dataset.py, model.py, slurm/train.sh
Commit: 1aabd3a — pushed to main.

  23751109: smoke_bf16, 50 stations, 2 epochs — verifies all fixes end-to-end.

  1. Confirm job 23751109 completes both epochs + val without error.
  2. Check log for "[SHM] L12 preloaded for ~48 stations" and startup RAM ~115GB.
  3. Submit full baseline_nll: sbatch slurm/train.sh --run-name baseline_nll
  4. Monitor W&B: target val/0-10/ubRMSE < 0.07 m³/m³.

--- Code review fixes (same session, continued) ---

Job 23751109 failed — TCPStore timeout after 600s:
  Root cause: rank 0 preloaded ~120GB to /dev/shm while ranks 1-3 sat inside
    dist.barrier() waiting for rank 0 to store its NCCL unique ID. TCPStore times
    out after 600s independently of NCCL_TIMEOUT.
  Fix: moved preloading to BEFORE dist.init_process_group(). Ranks 1-3 now spin
    on a sentinel file (.done) with sleep(2) — no NCCL involved, no timeout risk.

Additional fixes from structured code review:
  1. NLL loss — replaced softplus+1e-6 variance with log-variance parameterization.
     NLL = 0.5*log_var + 0.5*(y-mu)^2*exp(-log_var). Avoids division by near-zero
     variance (gradient ~1e9 under softplus) → stable gradients in bfloat16.
     Decoder bias init -3.7 unchanged: exp(-3.7)≈0.025, σ≈0.16 m³/m³.
  2. log_var bounded via tanh(raw/10)*10 — soft clamp to (-10,10), σ∈(0.007,148).
     Hard clamp rejected: kills gradient at boundary (dead neuron). tanh preserves
     non-zero gradient everywhere.
  3. S2 NaN guardrail — load_s2_rolling_zarr was missing the isnan check that S1
     already had. Swath-edge corruptions would silently corrupt batch embeddings.
  4. All-cloud NaN gradient — spatial_pyramid_pool: -inf → -1e4 in attention mask.
     -inf + softmax = NaN; autograd cannot backprop through NaN in bfloat16.
  5. Worker seed offset — worker_init_fn: seed += worker_id for explicit per-worker
     numpy/random divergence.
  6. ulimit -n 65536 — slurm/train.sh: 612 stations × 3 memmap keys × 8 workers
     ≈14k FDs; default SLURM ulimit 4096 would crash dataset init on full run.
  7. non_blocking=True — all .to(device) calls in train loop and evaluate. With
     pin_memory=True already set, overlaps PCIe transfer with GPU compute.
  8. ubRMSE deflation guard — compute_metrics: skip stations with <2 samples.
     Single-sample station has zero anomaly → deflates metric; guard now explicit.
  9. Architecture note (no change): 14×14 U-Net skip connections provide semantic
     depth but not high-frequency spatial edges. Acceptable for soil moisture;
     revisit for Phase 2 flux estimation where spatial sharpness matters.

Commits: 0347379, 39ead73, 2845faa, 5db3d1c — all pushed to main.

Active job:
  23751546: smoke_bf16, 50 stations, 2 epochs — resubmitted with all fixes.

=== 2026-06-02 ===
Session summary (late afternoon/evening) — satellite TIF-to-Zarr migration:

Storage audit (before migration):
- Scratch (/gpfs/scratch1/shared/pkhanal/satellite/): 411 GB, 998 stations, ~765k TIF files
- Project quota: 5 TB total, 826 GB used → 4.2 TB free; scratch purge policy 14-30 days (TIFs from 2026-05-26 at risk)

Design decisions:
- Zarr v2 format with Blosc+zstd-3 compression; S1RTC cast to float16 (~87 GB saving, negligible precision loss)
- zarr==2.18.7 + numcodecs installed in terramind conda env
- Resume-safe via .complete sentinel file per station store
- Zarr structure per station: s2/ (N,12,224,224 int16), s1_asc/ + s1_desc/ (N,2,224,224 float16), dem/ (1,224,224 float32), lulc/ (N_years,224,224 uint8); dates stored as |S8 arrays; station metadata in root .zattrs

Scripts created:
- convert_satellite_to_zarr.py  — multiprocessing.Pool(4); reads TIFs from scratch, writes Zarr to /gpfs/work3/0/prjs1968/satellite_zarr/; --execute flag (dry-run default)
- verify_zarr.py                — 16-worker verification; checks .complete sentinel + tile count match + spot-reads; outputs csvs/zarr_verification.csv
- slurm/migrate_to_zarr.sh     — 100-task array, rome partition, 5 CPUs, 4 workers, 2h; email on FAIL only
- slurm/verify_zarr.sh         — single job, 16 CPUs, 30 min

Jobs completed:

- Verification job 23383413: 998/998 stores passed, 0 failures
- Final Zarr store size: 247 GB (down from 411 GB — 40% reduction)

=== 2026-06-03 ===
Session: Full training pipeline implementation, bug fixes, and first training job submission.

## Pipeline implementation (dataset.py, model.py, train.py)
- dataset.py: complete refactor — split-based station loading, ERA5/SIF/TWSA/label numpy
  caches loaded in __init__ (no xarray in __getitem__), _resolve_pt_paths() pre-resolves
  all .pt file paths to eliminate GPFS glob() calls in hot loop
- model.py: added hist_modality_emb, era5_modality_emb, sif_modality_emb, twsa_modality_emb,
  sif_mlp, twsa_mlp; predict_uncertainty flag; UNetDecoder returns (mu, log_var) tuple
- train.py: AdamW with weight-decay separation, ReduceLROnPlateau (patience=10, factor=0.5),
  early stopping (patience=20), W&B logging, argparse CLI, checkpoint resume, batch_size=64
- compute_era5_stats.py: Welford online mean/std over all sm_only train stations
  → csvs/era5_stats.json (587 stations, 1.78M station-days, log1p for tp_sum)
- slurm/train.sh: A100, 48h, 8 CPUs, 64GB RAM
- demo_plot.py: loads best.pt, plots predicted vs observed SM + ±1σ band + spatial map

## Bugs fixed (in order found)
1. SM_DEPTHS: was ["0-10","10-20","20-40","40-100"] (4 depths) → fixed to ["0-10","10-30","30-100"] (3)
2. Cloud mask: was (cm==0).all() treating water/snow as invalid → fixed to bad_frac<=0.01
   for classes {3,4,5,255} (thin cloud, thick cloud, shadow, nodata)
3. Missing modality embeddings: ERA5, S2-hist, S1-hist tokens had no type embeddings —
   transformer couldn't distinguish them → added hist_modality_emb, era5_modality_emb
4. RNG duplication across DataLoader workers: self._rng.Random() duplicated on fork →
   replaced with torch.rand(1).item() + worker_init_fn seeding
5. xarray in __getitem__: ERA5/SIF/TWSA/labels opened per sample → moved to __init__ numpy caches
6. Label xr.Dataset cache: open file handles across fork → numpy arrays, close immediately
7. _in_window leap year bug: DOY comparison included 366 days for leap-year boundaries →
   replaced with _window_datetimes() using timedelta(364) arithmetic
8. _window_ints leap year bug: same root cause → now delegates to _window_datetimes()
9. S1 glob() in __getitem__: GPFS metadata query per sample → _resolve_pt_paths() in __init__
10. log_var clamping: clamp only in loss function created dead neurons (zero gradient outside
    [-6,6]) → moved clamp to UNetDecoder.forward before returning
11. _rel_pos leap year overflow: DOY formula could return values outside [0,364], crashing
    nn.Embedding(365) → replaced with 364-(target_dt-acq_dt).days datetime subtraction
12. ERA5 padding mask: slot-index compared with calendar DOY → model was blind to recent
    weather (target_doy=100 masked 265 most-recent ERA5 slots including target day itself) →
    fixed to era5_pad = (era5_doys == 0), masking only zero-padded slots
13. Spatial anchor selection: _get_target_spatial_tokens used DOY.max() to find most-recent
    acquisition — breaks at year boundary (Dec DOY~350 > Jun DOY~180 even if Jun is newer) →
    replaced with rel_pos.max() which is monotonically increasing within rolling window
14. Unused target_doy: removed from dataset batch dict after ERA5 masking fix

## 2026-06-03 — ERA5-Land NaN fix for 3 coastal stations

### Root cause
check_dataset job 23421285 found 26 ERA5 NaN errors across train/val splits.
All from 3 stations: ISMN_SCAN_Combate (val), ISMN_SNOTEL_PortGraham (train),
ISMN_USCRN_Cape-Charles-5-ENE (oos).
Pattern: 18/19 ERA5 variables entirely NaN, only tp_sum valid.

Root cause: these are coastal/island stations whose coordinates fall on ocean pixels
in the ERA5-Land 0.1° grid. ERA5-Land masks ocean cells. The old CDS download script
used two separate datasets — Group A (derived-era5-land-daily-statistics) returned
all-NaN for ocean pixels, while Group B (reanalysis-era5-land-timeseries) happened
to return valid tp values, creating a mixed result.

### Fix
Rewrote download_era5land_gee.py with a 3-strategy fallback chain per station:
  1. ERA5-Land point query (0.1°) — default
  2. ERA5-Land 25 km buffer + mean of valid nearby land pixels
  3. ERA5 reanalysis (0.25°, global, no land mask)
  4. Flag for exclusion if all strategies return NaN

All 3 stations resolved via Strategy 2 (buffer 25km) — nearby land pixels had valid
data. Strategy 3 was not needed.

Re-downloaded via: download_era5land_gee.py --stations ISMN_SCAN_Combate,
ISMN_SNOTEL_PortGraham,ISMN_USCRN_Cape-Charles-5-ENE --consolidate --era5-only

Consolidated files overwritten (0 NaN in all variables):
  sm_only/ISMN_SCAN_Combate/ERA5Land/meteo_20190101_20241231.nc        (2192 days)
  sm_only/ISMN_SNOTEL_PortGraham/ERA5Land/meteo_20140101_20221231.nc   (3287 days)
  sm_only/ISMN_USCRN_Cape-Charles-5-ENE/ERA5Land/meteo_20150101_20211231.nc (2557 days)

### Next
Re-run check_dataset to confirm clean, then submit training.

## 2026-06-03 — Zarr data pipeline decision

### Problem identified
WandB system metrics confirmed 93.6% GPU idle time with 4 workers (21% with 16 workers).
Root cause: 7-10 random GPFS file opens per sample across individual .pt files.
H100 computes a batch in ~75ms, waits ~400ms for GPFS I/O → 21% effective GPU util.

### Decision: everything → zarr
Single zarr store per station containing ALL modalities:
  tokens (S2/S1 L3/L6/L9/L12), CloudMask, DEM, LULC, ERA5, SIF, TWSA, labels
Zarr = single format for training AND publication (open standard, Zenodo-compatible).
Original .pt + .nc files deleted after zarr is verified.

### Storage architecture
- work3: zarr stores (permanent, publication-ready)
  /gpfs/work3/0/prjs1968/data/zarr/{category}/{station}/
- scratch1: rsynced copy (fast training cache, ~20 min rsync at job start)
  /gpfs/scratch1/shared/pkhanal/zarr/{category}/{station}/

### Scratch1 cleanup (926GB freed)
- satellite/ 411GB, token_bundles/ 505GB, token_zarr/ 10GB → all deleted

### Expected improvement
  GPU util: 21% → 65-75%  |  Epoch time: 110 min → 25-35 min  |  SBUs/epoch: 370 → 85
  work3 storage: 841GB → ~350GB after deleting originals

### Zarr schema (complete package — replaces ALL original files)
Each station zarr store contains:
  s2/{l12,l9,l6,l3,dates}        S2L2A tokens (N,196,768) fp16
  s1_asc/{l12,l9,l6,l3,dates}   S1RTC ASC tokens + orbit attr
  s1_desc/{...}                   S1RTC DESC tokens (if exists)
  cm/{masks,dates}                CloudMask (N,224,224) uint8
  dem, lulc                       Static (196,768) fp16
  soil                            Soil patch (21,74,74) float32
  era5/{values,date_ints,doys}    ERA5-Land daily (N,19)
  sif/{values,date_ints,doys}     SIF sparse
  twsa/{lwe,lwe_uncertainty,...}  TWSA monthly
  labels/{sm,qc,depths,dates}     ISMN soil moisture labels
  .complete                       Sentinel (written last)
All modalities verified present. zarr = publication-ready (Zenodo/HuggingFace).
After check_dataset passes: delete all .pt, .nc, .tif originals.

### Architecture fix: staleness embedding for spatial anchor tokens (model.py)
_get_target_spatial_tokens() now adds rel_pos_emb(anchor_rel_pos) to the 196
bottleneck tokens. Previously, U-Net decoder had no idea how old the anchor
satellite image was — now explicitly knows staleness via the same embedding
used for satellite history tokens.

### Model architecture fixes (model.py, commit 893e2c1)
1. Removed absolute DOY sinusoidal PE from satellite history tokens
   sat_tok: pyr + pe + rp → pyr + rp (rel_pos only)
   Reason: ERA5 is the seasonal anchor; satellites only need staleness signal.
   ERA5/SIF/TWSA keep both pe+rp (division of labour).
2. Staleness embedding added to spatial anchor tokens in _get_target_spatial_tokens()
   U-Net decoder now knows how old the anchor image is via rel_pos_emb(anchor_rel_pos).

### Zarr conversion — final job: 23439114 (rome, 64 workers, 16h, started ~19:05)
Previous jobs (23437926, 23438505, 23438765, 23439097) cancelled due to iterative
fixes: pre-flight layer check, station naming fix, soil_patch addition, PE cleanup.
Final schema verified on 2 stations before submitting full run.
Converts all 993 stations to /gpfs/work3/0/prjs1968/data/zarr/.
Expected: ~4-6h with 64 workers on rome.

### Next session checklist
1. Check zarr job 23439114 (find zarr -name ".complete" | wc -l → target 993)
2. Submit check_dataset SLURM job to verify zarr reading path
3. Submit training: sbatch slurm/train.sh --run-name baseline_nll
4. Once training epoch 1 clean: delete originals (~841GB freed)

### See text/zarr_pipeline_plan.txt for full technical spec

## Notes for architecture.md
Current implementation diverges from architecture.md on several points:
- Uses spatial pyramid pooling (not Latent Cross-Attention bottleneck)
- 3 depth bins (not 4)
- log1p + z-score ERA5 norm (not Yeo-Johnson)
- ~1035 tokens in sequence (not 680-730)
- No staleness gate implemented yet
- ERA5 future masking: right-aligned array with era5_doys==0 mask (not calendar masking)
architecture.md update deferred to after Phase 1 training completes.

=== 2026-06-04 ===

### Problem identified: zarr inode/quota crisis
- Job 23439114 (creating zarr stores on work3) hit Errno 122 (disk quota exceeded) at 163/993 stations
- Root cause: chunks=(1, 196, 768) — one chunk file per satellite acquisition per layer
- Station ISMN_RSMN_Bacles: 7,589 inodes (373 S2 + 686 S1_asc + 677 S1_desc acquisitions × 4 layers each)
- At this rate: ~7.5M inodes for 993 stations — well beyond project quota
- Secondary problem: dataset.py was reading from wrong path (token_zarr/ instead of zarr/)
  and old scratch data at /gpfs/scratch1/shared/pkhanal/token_zarr/ used T=1 chunks (also wrong)

### Decisions: rechunk strategy
- S2/S1 token arrays: chunks=(32, 196, 768) → 9.6 MB/chunk uncompressed; reduces 60 reads/window to 2
- Cloud masks: chunks=(128, 224, 224) → 6.4 MB/chunk (GPFS 10-100 MB target)
- Tabular (ERA5, SIF, TWSA, labels): full array = 1 chunk (tiny, preloaded to RAM)
- Expected: ~341 inodes/station (13× reduction for worst-case stations)
- Output path corrected: /gpfs/scratch1/shared/pkhanal/zarr/ (matches dataset.py ZARR_ROOT_FAST)
- Per-station single zarr store retained (modality split saves only ~22 inodes; chunk files dominate)

### L12 RAM cache design (dataset.py)
- Preload S2/S1 L12 arrays into RAM at __init__: ~51 GB for 584 train stations, shared CoW across 16 workers
- Reduces disk reads per training sample from ~103 to 3 (only L3/L6/L9 skip features read from disk)

### write_labels fix (create_token_zarr.py)
- Old code looked for labels.nc inside satellite token dirs — only 204 of 890 SM stations had it
- Fix: read from /projects/prjs1968/raw_soil_moisture/{network}_{station_name}_*.nc
  and clip to station's start_date/end_date from station_splits.csv
- Non-ISMN (AmeriFlux/ICOS) stations: empty labels arrays (no raw SM file)

### Depth coverage analysis (842 active SM stations)
- L1 (0-10cm): 842 stations, 6,904 station-years
- L2 (10-30cm): 656 stations, 5,534 station-years
- L3 (30-100cm): 534 stations, 4,564 station-years
- All 3 layers: 501 stations (59%); surface only: 153 (18%); L1+L2: 155 (18%); L1+L3: 33 (4%)

### Files modified
- create_token_zarr.py: ZARR_ROOT → /gpfs/scratch1/shared/pkhanal/zarr/, T_TOKENS=32, T_CM=128,
  write_labels() rewritten to read from RAW_SM_DIR, zarr.consolidate_metadata() added,
  convert_station() extended with raw_sm_key/start_date/end_date args, default workers=64
- dataset.py: _open_zarr() uses zarr.open_consolidated(); added _l12_cache dict populated at __init__;
  load_s2_rolling_zarr()/load_s1_rolling_zarr() accept optional l12_np arrays for zero-disk reads;
  __getitem__ passes _l12_cache to rolling loaders
- slurm/create_token_zarr.sh: output path updated to scratch

### Pending (NOT yet done — requires user action)
1. Delete old T=1 scratch data: rm -rf /gpfs/scratch1/shared/pkhanal/token_zarr
   /gpfs/scratch1/shared/pkhanal/token_zarr_test  (496 GB — needs confirmation)
2. Submit: sbatch slurm/create_token_zarr.sh
3. After all 993 .complete files confirmed: delete work3/data/zarr/ and .pt bundles in projects/prjs1968/data/

### Next session
- Confirm scratch deletion, then submit zarr job
- Verify depth coverage in new zarr matches raw_soil_moisture counts (842 SM stations)
- Start training once zarr complete

  (boolean check). Investigated dataset.py and confirmed
  fill_soil_nans() (nearest-neighbour distance_transform_edt) already
  unconditionally fills any amount of NaN at load time, and the 17 stations
  with very high NaN% (0.58-1.00) are already excluded via
  soil_patch_ok=False. Replaced boolean flag with soil_nan_pct, only CRITICAL
  if >50%.

  44.4%, 0 stations >50%). Audit closed clean again.

  slurm/audit_comprehensive.sh, .gitignore (+wandb/), and the accumulated
  backlog from prior sessions (tokenization gap fixes, satellite_zarr cm-trim,
  2016 start_date clamp, Prades S1 recovery, slurm scripts/logs). Removed
  superseded audit_zarr_complete.py / check_satellite_alignment.py /
  check_label_continuity.py and their old outputs.
- Architecture Q&A (no code changes):
  - Soil data: OpenLandMap 30m, patch = 74x74px = 2.22km x 2.22km, 21 ch
    (3 depths x 7 properties).
  - TerraMind tile = 224x224px@10m = 2.24km, patches = 16x16px -> 14x14=196
    tokens. Soil patch (2.22km) ~= TerraMind tile (2.24km) -> ~99% spatial
    overlap, not a meaningful misalignment. "Soil patch size in TerraMind
    patch units" ~= one full 14x14 token grid (i.e. soil ~ whole-tile scale,
    much coarser than a single 16x16px token).
  - Soil -> CNN (SoilEncoder, model.py:205): depthwise-separable conv +
    4-scale spatial pyramid -> 4x768 tokens, ~211K params.
  - Feasible to also feed soil into UNetDecoder (model.py:128) skip
    connections via resize+conv at 56x56/112x112 stages -- discussed but
    not implemented.
  - Soil currently has NO modality dropout (unlike SIF/TWSA 50%, S1/S2
    20%/10%) -- flagged as a possible gap; user wants ablation tests for
    soil map too -- not yet scoped/implemented.
  - fill_soil_nans(): no percentage threshold, ALWAYS nearest-neighbour
    fills remaining NaN at load time; the 50%/58% thresholds only govern
    whether a station is excluded from the dataset entirely
    (soil_patch_ok=False), not whether the fill runs.
- Reviewed dataset.py loading architecture (__init__ lines 728-895,
  __getitem__ 894+): per-station eager caching at construction time for ALL
  993 stations (era5/sif/twsa/labels/L12 token arrays into RAM, zarr group
  opened once); __getitem__ assembles one full multimodal sample per
  (station, year, doy) from these caches + a few small per-call zarr reads
  (soil, dem/lulc, L3/L6/L9 skip features). Noted fill_soil_nans() reruns
  every __getitem__ call even though soil is static per station -- possible
  future optimization (precompute once per station), not implemented.

=== 2026-06-12 ===
Investigation:
- Dug into the H100 smoke-test OOM (job 23676471, 2026-06-11): root cause
  was SoilMoistureDataset.__init__ eagerly caching L12 token arrays for
  ALL stations in a split into RAM, ignoring --max-stations entirely.
- Decided to empirically test eager (RAM-cached L12) vs lazy (on-demand
  zarr chunk reads) before committing to a --mem setting for the full run,
  since this had never been measured directly.

Fixes (dataset.py, train.py, slurm/smoke_h100.sh):
- dataset.py: added max_stations param with an early-exit in the splits
  loop (stop opening new station zarr groups once max_stations reached),
  and a DISABLE_L12_CACHE env toggle that skips the eager L12 RAM cache
  and forces the existing lazy zarr-chunk-read fallback.
- train.py: replaced the old post-hoc Subset-based --max-stations limiting
  with passing max_stations directly into SoilMoistureDataset; added
  per-batch step timing to train_one_epoch; added --num-workers /
  --prefetch-factor CLI overrides + CONFIG["prefetch_factor"].
- Verified via CPU sanity test (max_stations=1) that both eager and lazy
  paths produce correct samples (s2_l12 shape [60,196,768]).

New OOM discovered mid-experiment (jobs 23704119/23704120, both hung at
0% GPU util, killed by OOM-killer per dmesg):
- Independent of eager/lazy: DataLoader num_workers=16 x prefetch_factor=4
  = 64 queued batches x ~2GB/batch (31MB/sample x batch_size=64) ~= 125GB,
  nearly exhausting --mem=128G on its own.
- Fix: reduced smoke_h100.sh to --num-workers 4 --prefetch-factor 2
  (8 queued batches ~= 16GB). Cancelled hung jobs.

Eager vs lazy comparison (re-run with fixed settings, jobs 23704637 eager
vs 23704638 lazy, --max-stations 3, 2 epochs):
- Eager: completed cleanly in ~5.5 min. avg GPU util 41.2%, max VRAM
  737 MiB, steady ~720ms/batch (periodic ~3.5-4s stalls for worker
  refill). val_loss improved -0.75 -> -0.80.
- Lazy: cancelled after 10 min, only ~2-3 batches done (~2-2.5 min/batch
  vs eager's 0.7s -- would take 130-165 min/epoch). CPU RSS stable
  (~26GB), no OOM -- purely a throughput problem.
- Root cause of lazy slowdown: load_s2_rolling_zarr/load_s1_rolling_zarr
  (dataset.py:304,352) call tokens_z[src_i] once per timestep over a
  60-step history window. Plain zarr.Array has no chunk cache, and chunk
  size is 32 along the time axis, so each chunk gets re-read +
  re-decompressed ~30x per sample, x3 modalities (s2/s1_asc/s1_desc),
  x batch_size 64 -- ~1500x redundant decompression.

Decisions:
- Eager L12 RAM caching is the right approach for the full run; the lazy
  fallback is not viable without a rewrite (would need either a chunk
  cache or reading the full window slice in one call).
- For slurm/train.sh (full baseline_nll run): eager L12 caches need
  ~180GB (train 157.72G + val 21.63G, measured) + ~24GB worker overhead
  + ~16-32GB DataLoader prefetch queues ~= 220-235GB total. 256G would
  leave only ~8-14% headroom for a long multi-day run -- recommended
  bumping to 320G instead. Also worth testing num_workers=8 (prefetch
  ~32GB) since the smoke test showed GPU util only 41% at num_workers=4,
  suggesting the CPU-side batch prep is the bottleneck on the 16-cpu
  allocation.

### Next steps (carried over)
1. Update slurm/train.sh: --mem=256G -> 320G, set --num-workers/
   --prefetch-factor (test 8x2 vs 4x2 for GPU utilization), decide
   whether to keep DISABLE_L12_CACHE as a documented escape hatch or
   remove the lazy fallback code entirely.
2. Commit accumulated changes: dataset.py, train.py, slurm/smoke_h100.sh,
   text/logs.txt, plus new log files under logs/.
3. Prepare for model run: sbatch slurm/check_dataset.sh, then
   sbatch slurm/train.sh --run-name baseline_nll (ensure
   csvs/era5_stats.json exists first).

   soil ablation tests; possible soil-into-decoder skip connections.
5. (Optional, low priority) Decide whether to also clean up
   /projects/prjs1968/data/excluded_stations/ and logs/ (~1.7G,
   currently preserved).

## 2026-06-12

### Summary
Debugged and fixed the S1 NaN token overwrite job (zarr vindex vs oindex API mismatch), then built a
full nodata-mask pipeline for S1, DEM, and LULC modalities. Propagated these masks through dataset.py
and model.py, adding quality-filtered anchor selection, per-orbit spatial embeddings, and
ContextFormer-style random masking during training.

### Discussions & Decisions
- zarr 2.18.7 `vindex` does not support a plain 1-D integer array as an index into a 3-D array; `oindex`
  (outer/orthogonal indexing) does — switched `arr.vindex[idxs]` to `arr.oindex[idxs]` in
  fix_bad_s1_tokens.py. This is the canonical zarr 2.x way to do arbitrary-row selection.
- Nodata fraction threshold for patch masking set to FILLABLE_THRESH=0.01, matching the convention
  already used in precompute_terramind.py (patches with >1% nodata pixels are flagged invalid).
- Anchor selection strategy: prefer the most recent fully-clear S2 image (all 196 patches valid);
  fall back to most recent image regardless of cloud cover. This replaces the previous
  `load_recent_skip_features_zarr` approach which ignored cloud quality.
- `anchor_orbit` encoding changed from bool (is_s1: True/False) to 3-class int (0=S2, 1=S1_asc,
  2=S1_desc) so the spatial modality embedding can distinguish the two orbit directions.
- ContextFormer-style random masking (p=0.5, training only) AND-ed with real nodata masks before
  pyramid pooling — forces the model to learn spatial redundancy robustly.
- DEM/LULC are static per station, so their token_mask is written once as a (14,14) bool array rather
  than per-acquisition.

### Code & Files Changed
- **fix_bad_s1_tokens.py** — `arr.vindex[idxs]` replaced with `arr.oindex[idxs]`; job 23708627
  rerun and verified: 571 stale fully-NaN S1 tokens overwritten, 0 remaining NaN acquisitions.
- **compute_s1_dem_lulc_token_masks.py** (new) — CPU scan (Pool(64)) over all 993 stations; reads
  raw satellite_zarr, computes nodata_frac per 16×16 patch, writes `s1_asc/token_mask` (N,14,14),
  `s1_desc/token_mask` (N,14,14), `dem_token_mask` (14,14), `lulc_token_mask` (14,14) into token zarr.
- **slurm/compute_token_masks.sh** (new) — SLURM submission for above; rome node, Pool(64),
  --cpus-per-task=64, ~3m16s wall time. Job 23709150: COMPLETED, 374,992 total masked patches written,
  0 errors.
- **dataset.py** — multiple changes:
  - `load_s1_rolling_zarr`: now returns 5-tuple; added `s1_token_mask` (per-acquisition, per-orbit);
    tracks orbit_key per entry so the correct orbit's token_mask is fetched from zarr.
  - `load_recent_skip_features_zarr` removed; replaced with `select_anchor_zarr` (quality-filtered
    anchor selection with L3/L6/L9/L12 load + l12_cache zero-I/O path).
  - `__getitem__`: adds `s1_token_mask` (MAX_S1,14,14), `dem_token_mask` (14,14), `lulc_token_mask`
    (14,14); replaces skip_l3/l6/l9/recent_is_s1 keys with anchor_l3/l6/l9/l12/rel_pos/orbit keys.
- **model.py** — multiple changes:
  - `_static_pyramid`: accepts `token_valid` (14,14 bool reshaped to 196) for DEM/LULC nodata masking.
  - `spatial_modality_emb`: Embedding(2→3, d_model) — S2, S1-asc, S1-desc.
  - `_get_target_spatial_tokens`: simplified to read directly from `batch["anchor_l12"]` and
    `batch["anchor_orbit"]`; removed argmax over rel_pos.
  - `_get_skip_connections`: reads anchor_l3/l6/l9 keys.
  - `forward()`: wires all 4 token masks; applies ContextFormer-style random masking (p=0.5,
    training only) AND-ed with real nodata masks for S2, S1, DEM, LULC before pyramid pooling.
- **check_dataset.py** — EXPECTED shapes updated for all new dict keys.

### Key Outcomes
- All 571 stale fully-NaN S1 tokens in the token zarr are fixed and verified clean.
- Nodata token masks computed and stored for S1 (both orbits), DEM, and LULC across all 993 stations
  (374,992 total masked patches written).
- dataset.py and model.py are consistent with the new 4-mask / 3-class orbit / anchor-selection design.

## 2026-06-12 — Architecture & Code Critique Q&A

### Question (external review)

A detailed technical and architectural critique was raised covering the following points:

I. Technical (Code Mechanics)
  1. RAM Limits and the _l12_cache: eager-loading the full L12 history (S2 + S1-ASC + S1-DESC) for
     every station into RAM during __init__ risks OOM at scale. Suggested fix: use numpy mmap_mode='r'
     instead of zg["s2/l12"][:] eager copies.
  2. DOY Positional Encoding Discontinuity: sinusoidal_pe feeds absolute DOY (1-365) into a standard
     Transformer PE, which is not periodic. DOY 365 and DOY 1 get very different embeddings even though
     they are temporally adjacent. Suggested fix: circular encoding sin/cos(2π·DOY/365.25).
  3. Hardcoded Magic Numbers in Pyramid Pooling: spatial_pyramid_pool uses explicit indices
     _pool(6,8,6,8) etc., rigidly coupling the code to a 14×14 grid. Suggested fix:
     torch.nn.AdaptiveAvgPool2d.
  4. Uncertainty Clamping (log_var): .clamp(-6.0, 6.0) kills gradients outside bounds (dead neurons).
     Suggested fix: Softplus activation to ensure positivity with gradient flow everywhere.

II. Architectural (Model Design)
  1. Point-to-Pixel Supervision on a UNet: UNet generates a 224×224 map but losses only compute
     gradients at the single center pixel (row=col=112). No pressure for spatial coherence in the
     other 50,175 output pixels.
  2. Temporal-to-Spatial Bottleneck: 196 target spatial tokens must absorb 365-day ERA5 history and
     satellite history via self-attention; high-frequency historical data may be diluted before
     reaching the UNet bottleneck.
  3. Skip Connections Bypass Temporal Context: UNet skip connections (L3/L6/L9) come from the static
     anchor acquisition and completely bypass the Temporal Transformer, so high-res decoder layers
     have no knowledge of recent weather or historical soil state.

Final question: given that the UNet output is only supervised at the center pixel, how is spatial
coherence across the 224×224 map validated?

---

### Answer

**I.1 RAM / L12 cache — Partially valid, but already partially addressed**

The critique is right that eager L12 loading is dangerous at scale. However the code already has two
mitigations not acknowledged by the critique:
- _load_pt (dataset.py:103) already uses mmap=True (PyTorch-level memory mapping, not eager loading).
- DISABLE_L12_CACHE=1 env var exists as an explicit escape hatch.
The real risk is whether __init__ does zg["s2/l12"][:] (a full array copy into RAM) rather than keeping
a lazy zarr reference. That specific path in load_s2_rolling_zarr:296 correctly avoids the copy when
l12_np is None. The mmap suggestion is already implemented for the .pt path.

**I.2 DOY PE discontinuity — Valid, but only applies to ERA5/SIF/TWSA**

sinusoidal_pe (model.py:42) does treat DOY as a linear scalar, creating a real discontinuity at year
boundaries. However, satellite history tokens do NOT use sinusoidal_pe — they use rel_pos_emb (a
learned relative-position embedding, model.py:494,504). The discontinuity only affects ERA5
(model.py:519), SIF (model.py:545), and TWSA (model.py:563). The circular encoding fix is correct and
straightforward. This is a real bug for training across year boundaries and worth fixing:

  # Replace sinusoidal_pe for ERA5/SIF/TWSA with:
  sin_enc = torch.sin(2 * math.pi * doys.float() / 365.25)
  cos_enc = torch.cos(2 * math.pi * doys.float() / 365.25)
  # then project [sin, cos] -> d_model via a small MLP

**I.3 Hardcoded pyramid indices — Valid concern, wrong fix**

The indices at model.py:103-106 are hardcoded for 14×14 and are a real brittleness. But the suggested
fix (AdaptiveAvgPool2d) cannot replace the existing implementation because _pool uses learned attention
weights from self.s2_pyramid_attn (nn.Linear(768,1)). AdaptiveAvgPool2d only does uniform pooling. The
right fix is to parameterize the grid size while preserving attention-weighted pooling:

  G = int(tokens.shape[1] ** 0.5)  # 14 for 196-token grid
  c = G // 2
  # compute scale bounds from G dynamically

**I.4 log_var clamping — Critique partially misses what the code already does**

The comment at model.py:195-198 already diagnoses the "dead neuron via loss-side clamping" problem and
explicitly moved the clamp to the model output to ensure gradients flow through the network. The
Softplus suggestion is mathematically cleaner (no zero-gradient regions anywhere), but changes the
parametrization (would learn variance directly rather than log-variance). Given the bounds [-6,6]
correspond to σ² ∈ [0.0025, 403] — a very wide range for soil moisture — this is a low-priority fix.

**II.1 Point supervision on a UNet — The most valid critique, the most important**

Confirmed: masked_huber_loss and masked_nll_loss both extract a single pixel (row=112, col=112) before
computing loss. The entire UNet decoder (~4M parameters across 4 upsampling stages) receives gradient
from exactly one pixel per sample. This means the model can satisfy the loss with a spatially uniform
sheet at the station value. Practical options in order of cost:
  1. Total Variation regularization: penalize |∇SM_map|. Low cost, physically justified (SM is
     spatially coherent).
  2. LULC/DEM consistency loss: pixels with identical LULC class and similar elevation should have
     similar SM. Uses data already in the pipeline.
  3. FiLM-modulated skip connections (see II.3 below).

**II.2 Temporal-to-Spatial bottleneck — Less concerning than stated**

The architecture is well-suited for cross-modal fusion: the 196 spatial tokens sit inside the
bidirectional Transformer alongside all 365 ERA5 tokens and satellite history tokens, able to attend
to any of them (model.py:644). This is closer to a ViT fusion pattern than a bottleneck. Whether 6
layers is sufficient is a hyperparameter question, not a fundamental design flaw.

**II.3 Skip connections bypass temporal context — Valid and tractable to fix**

_get_skip_connections (model.py:429) loads L3/L6/L9 from the static anchor acquisition with no ERA5
conditioning. The high-resolution UNet layers are driven entirely by "what did the satellite see on the
anchor day" with no knowledge of recent weather. FiLM modulation is the right fix: take a summary
vector from the transformer context (e.g., mean-pooled ERA5 output tokens), project to (scale, shift)
via a small linear layer, and apply to each skip before concatenation. ~100 lines of code.

**On spatial validation**

Since the loss only supervises the center pixel, the map outputs are currently unvalidated beyond that
point. Practical approaches:
  1. Cross-station tiling: some ISMN stations are within a few km of each other. If two stations share
     overlapping tile footprints, the model's predicted SM at each station's pixel can be compared to
     the observed SM at that station (without ever training on that relationship).
  2. Visual audits against LULC: generated SM maps should show lower values over urban/bare soil and
     higher over vegetated pixels in the same tile.
  3. Lag-correlation with precipitation: SM at any pixel should show a positive response to ERA5 tp_sum
     within a few days. Computable for every pixel in held-out tiles.
  None of these replace dense labels, but they would falsify the "flat sheet" failure mode.

**Priority ranking of fixes**
  High: II.1 (point supervision) — add TV or LULC/DEM consistency loss before scaling up training.
  High: I.2 (circular DOY encoding) — real bug for cross-year samples, easy to fix.
  Medium: II.3 (FiLM-modulated skip connections) — ~100 lines, directly improves temporal context in
          high-res decoder layers.
  Low: I.3 (parameterize pyramid grid size) — brittleness, not a current bug.
  Low: I.4 (Softplus for log_var) — current clamping is defensible; Softplus is cleaner but non-urgent.

All 5 fixes implemented in commit bce7fd5 (model.py, train.py):
  - circular_doy_pe: ERA5/SIF/TWSA tokens now use sin/cos(k*2π*DOY/365.25) harmonics
  - Softplus log_var: UNetDecoder outputs log(softplus(x)+ε); clamp removed from loss
  - FiLMLayer: temporal context (mean-pooled non-spatial transformer output) injected
    into L9/L6/L3 skip connections via learned scale+shift
  - total_variation_loss: λ_tv=0.001 default, tunable via --lambda-tv
  - spatial_pyramid_pool: grid side G=int(N_tok**0.5), works for any square token grid

=== 2026-06-12 — Second architecture critique and follow-up fixes ===

Second critique raised 5 further issues. Assessment and fixes:

Q1 (Softplus variance init): VALID — zero bias init → σ≈0.83 m³/m³ initial std, physically
  massive for ISMN SM (range 0-0.6, within-station σ≈0.08-0.12). Fix: bias for variance
  channels set to -3.7 → softplus(-3.7)+ε≈0.025, σ≈0.16 m³/m³. Matches realistic prior.

Q2 (TV lambda too small): VALID — adjacent-pixel SM differences ~0.01-0.05; λ=0.001 gives
  TV contribution ~2e-5, invisible against NLL ~1.0. Fix: default bumped to λ=0.1; raw
  (unweighted) TV now logged to W&B as train/tv each epoch for empirical tuning.

Q3 (log→exp redundancy in NLL): VALID — decoder computed log(softplus(x)+ε) and loss did
  .exp() to recover variance. Fix: decoder now outputs var=softplus(x)+ε directly; loss
  uses torch.log(var) and divides by var. Interface renamed log_var→var throughout.

Q4 (RAM cap for L12 cache): Already handled — max_stations + 320G SLURM allocation.

Q5 (temporal holdouts): Valid methodological concern for Phase 2. Deferred — Phase 1 goal
  is to verify the model learns anything. Saved to memory for evaluation design later.

ISMN SM units confirmed: m³/m³ (volumetric water content), range ~[0.02, 0.55],
  within-station temporal σ ~0.08-0.12.

Code changes: model.py, train.py — committed and pushed.
Next: smoke test (slurm/smoke_h100.sh), then full baseline_nll run.

=== 2026-06-12 (continued session) — DDP bug fixes, smoke tests, and full baseline_nll submitted ===

Context:
  DDP (4-GPU) training was implemented in the previous sub-session. This session began with the
  decision to smoke-test the DDP code on H100 before committing to the 120h baseline_nll run.

DDP bug fixes (committed to GitHub):

  1. train_loss reduction (commit 665a8bb):
     Added dist.reduce(t_loss, dst=0, op=dist.ReduceOp.AVG) so rank 0 logs the global mean
     train loss, not just its local shard (which would be ~4× lower than the true loss).

  2. Early stopping broadcast (commit 665a8bb):
     Fixed a DDP deadlock: without dist.broadcast(stop_flag, src=0) before break, rank 0
     would exit the training loop while ranks 1-3 hung waiting at the next collective
     (dist.barrier / val_loss broadcast). Fixed with a stop_flag tensor broadcast to all ranks.

  3. train_tv reduction (commit 2bd7cc2):
     Same dist.reduce(AVG) pattern applied to train_tv (total variation loss) for accurate
     W&B logging of the TV term.

  4. gradient_as_bucket_view=True (commit 6304afb):
     Added to DDP constructor call to eliminate "grad strides mismatch" warning and reduce
     peak VRAM by reusing gradient buffers in-place.

  5. PYTORCH_CUDA_ALLOC_CONF=expandable_segments:True (commit 6304afb):
     Added to slurm/train.sh to reduce CUDA memory fragmentation during long multi-epoch runs.

  6. RAM/VRAM memory reporting (commit 83a73d2):
     Added a memory snapshot before epoch 1: RAM usage via /proc/meminfo (MemTotal, MemFree,
     MemAvailable), per-GPU VRAM allocated/reserved/total. Peak VRAM per epoch printed
     alongside the loss summary line.

  7. psutil replaced with /proc/meminfo (commit 1c6988b):
     psutil is not installed in the terramind conda env. Replaced all psutil calls with
     direct /proc/meminfo parsing, which is always available on Linux.

Smoke test journey (multiple jobs before passing):

=== 2026-06-13 (session 3 — NCCL timeout hardening + checkpoint-before-val) ===

Context: job 23752836 (baseline_nll, epoch 1) crashed after training completed.
  Epoch 1 training: 4101/4101 batches done. No crash during training.
  Val ran on rank 0 only (~127,742 cold GPFS samples; never page-cached since training
    only reads train split). Rank 0 took >10 min to evaluate cold val set.
  Ranks 1-3 waited at dist.broadcast(val_loss) which never came.
  PyTorch ProcessGroup default timeout = 600s → ranks 1-3 timed out at BROADCAST SeqNum=41013.
  NCCL_TIMEOUT=3600 env var was set but it controls NCCL library timeout, NOT PyTorch PG timeout.
  These are two separate timeout systems.
  No checkpoint was saved (crash happened before save). Epoch 1 training ~20 min lost.

Fix 1 — PyTorch PG timeout (already in previous commit 405a91e):
  dist.init_process_group(backend="nccl") →
  dist.init_process_group(backend="nccl", timeout=timedelta(seconds=3600))

Fix 2 — PyTorch PG timeout increased further (this session):
  timedelta(seconds=3600) → timedelta(seconds=7200)   # 2 hours
  NCCL_TIMEOUT env var: 3600 → 7200 in slurm/train.sh
  Reasoning: 127k val samples × cold GPFS; at 2s/batch = ~66 min > 60 min worst case.
  2 hours gives comfortable margin even if GPFS is degraded.

Fix 3 — Checkpoint saved before validation (this session, user's suggestion):
  Previously: epoch trains → evaluate() → save last.pt. If val crashes, epoch is lost.
  Now:        epoch trains → save last.pt (val_pending=True) → evaluate() → overwrite last.pt (val_pending=False).
  If val crashes at any point, last.pt exists with trained weights for that epoch.

Resume logic for val_pending:
  On resume, if ckpt["val_pending"] is True:
    - Load model/optimizer/scheduler weights from checkpoint
    - Set val_pending_epoch = ckpt["epoch"] and start_epoch = that epoch
    - In the training loop: skip train_one_epoch() for that epoch, reuse saved train_loss/train_tv
    - Skip dist.reduce() for train_loss (training didn't happen; no ranks to average)
    - Run evaluate() on rank 0, broadcast val_loss, save full checkpoint with val_pending=False
    - Continue to next epoch normally
  This means a val crash only costs the time to re-run validation (~15-30 min), not re-training.

Tensor pipeline usage analysis (low GPU utilization):
  GPU duty cycle ~60% in steady state: GPU active ~150-180ms per 283ms step.
  Remaining 40% is DataLoader waiting for next batch.
  Root cause: GPFS cold-read latency during first epoch. Workers hit un-cached zarr chunks
    and stall; DataLoader queue drains; GPU idles.
  This is a hardware constraint, not a code bug. Page cache warms after epoch 1 → epoch 2+
    runs at near 100% duty cycle.
  No code change made — increasing prefetch_factor risks RAM OOM; stalls = 0.1% of total runtime.

Jobs:
  23752836: FAILED — NCCL timeout during epoch 1 val (600s PG timeout, val took >10 min)
  23758139: submitted with 3600s fix, then cancelled (2 min in, still preloading) to apply
            further hardening
  23758952: ACTIVE — baseline_nll with all fixes (7200s timeout + checkpoint-before-val)

Files modified: train.py, slurm/train.sh

Active job as of 2026-06-13:
  23758952: baseline_nll, node gcn120, all fixes applied.
  Status at 06:30 runtime: rank 0 preloading L12 to /dev/shm (~21 min total).
    Only 3 lines in log (job header) — normal, preload is silent.
  Expected timeline:
    ~21 min: preload done, DDP init, datasets built, training starts
    ~21 + 20 min: epoch 1 training done → last.pt saved with val_pending=True
    ~21 + 20 + 15-30 min: epoch 1 val done → last.pt overwritten with val_pending=False
  If job cancelled/crashed during val: resume with same sbatch command.
    last.pt will have val_pending=True → resumes at val only, skips re-training.
  If job cancelled during training: no last.pt yet → starts fresh from epoch 1.
  Fixes in this job vs previous crashes:
    - PyTorch PG timeout: 7200s (was 600s default → caused 23752836 crash)
    - NCCL_TIMEOUT: 7200s env var
    - Checkpoint saved before val (val_pending=True) → epoch training not lost if val crashes
    - All previous fixes: /dev/shm L12 preload, bf16, no-capture-output, 700G RAM

=== 2026-06-14 (session 4 — baseline_nll analysis, huber switch, diagnostics) ===

--- What happened with baseline_nll ---

Job 23764793 (baseline_nll) ran on gcn131, 4×H100.

Results:
  Epoch 1: train_loss=5.1284  val_loss=1574.6  | 0-10 ubRMSE=1.9783  MAE=0.0930
  Epoch 2: train_loss=0.8748  val_loss=435.4   | 0-10 ubRMSE=1.9782  MAE=0.0855
  Epoch 3: train_loss=-2.0056 val_loss=656.8   | 0-10 ubRMSE=1.9781  MAE=0.0890

Key observations:
  - 0-10 cm ubRMSE completely frozen at 1.9782 across all 3 epochs — model not improving
  - val_loss spiked to 1574 in epoch 1 because NLL loss explodes when σ is small but μ is wrong
  - Labels audited (job 23794318): all 74 val stations have SM in [0, 0.6] — labels are clean
  - Root cause of frozen ubRMSE: model output unbounded (no sigmoid/clamp) → some val samples
    get predictions of ±2-3 m³/m³ which are physically impossible and dominate the MSE
  - NLL loss allowed model to "game" by shrinking σ rather than improving μ
  - mid-epoch checkpointing working correctly (mid_epoch.pt saved every 500 batches, deleted after epoch)
  - per-station worst-5 printing added (commit 130ef1f) but old code still running — no per-station
    data available for NLL epochs

Code changes this session (all committed and pushed):
  32baa5a — mid-epoch checkpointing every 500 batches + skip_batches resume logic
  130ef1f — sigma clamping (sigma_min=0.01, log_var_min≈-9.21) + per-station val metrics
  85ce2e3 — eval_stations.py standalone script for checkpoint analysis

--- Switch to baseline_huber ---

Decision: NLL is masking learning signal. Huber loss directly penalises prediction error,
no σ to game. Also more robust to outliers (delta=0.05 m³/m³).

Job 23790427 (baseline_huber) started fresh on gcn131.
Epoch 1 training: Huber loss converged to ~0.0006-0.0007 by batch 3750.
  Small training Huber loss could indicate either:
  (a) Model learning well (typical error ≈ 0.037 m³/m³)
  (b) Trivial solution: predicting station climatological mean (no temporal dynamics)
  ubRMSE will discriminate — val results pending.

--- Architecture encodings clarified ---

SoilMoistureModel token assembly per ERA5 timestep:
  era5_tok   — ERA5 feature values projected to 768-dim (weather content)
  era5_pe    — circular_doy_pe: sin/cos(k × 2π × DOY/365.25), k=1..384
               Explicitly periodic at 365.25 days — no year-boundary discontinuity
               Tells model "what season it is" (absolute calendar position)
  era5_rp    — rel_pos_emb: learned embedding of days-before-target (staleness)
               Tells model "how long ago" (relative temporal proximity)
  era5_mod   — learned modality token distinguishing ERA5 from S2/S1/SIF/TWSA

Discussion: advisor suggested stripping era5_pe to avoid "year-boundary discontinuity".
  This argument is WRONG for our circular_doy_pe (designed to be periodic at 365.25 days —
  DOY 365 and DOY 1 are nearly identical in embedding space). Stripping era5_pe would
  remove seasonality information with no benefit.
  Decision: keep era5_pe.

--- Data audit results ---

Label audit (job 23794318): 0 stations with SM values outside [0, 0.6] across all val stations.
Zarr completeness (job 23775422): 890 stations fully complete, 103 flux_only have no SM labels
  (by design — they're flux towers). All 993 zarr stores exist, all have S2/ERA5/L12.

  - If 0-10 ubRMSE drops significantly (< 0.5): Huber is learning, continue for 5-10 epochs
  - If 0-10 ubRMSE still flat (~1.978): proceed to Phase 1 diagnostic below

Step 2 (if needed): Phase 1 diagnostic run
  - Zero skip connections (s9, s6, s3 × 0.0 in UNetDecoder forward)
  - Keep era5_pe (circular, no discontinuity)
  - Run: sbatch slurm/train.sh --run-name diag_phase1 --loss-fn huber --lambda-tv 0
  - Goal: isolate whether temporal transformer alone can learn SM temporal dynamics
  - Success criterion: 0-10 ubRMSE < 0.10 within 5 epochs

Step 3 (after Phase 1 converges):
  - Re-enable skip connections
  - Add NLL with sigma clamping (sigma_min=0.01 already coded)
  - Add per-station worst-5 reporting (already coded)

  GPU VRAM impact: ~50 GB / 80 GB at batch 128 — within H100 limits.

Session 6 — 2026-06-14
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

Training status (job 23796729, baseline_huber, bs=128, lr=2e-4):
  Epoch 1 completed (from previous job, train_23790427.out):
    val_loss=0.0036 | peak_vram=24.7GB
    0-10 cm:  MSE=3.95, MAE=0.087, ubRMSE=1.98 (SNOTEL_UpperRioGrande=15.4)
    10-30 cm: MSE=0.066, MAE=0.069, ubRMSE=0.247
    30-100 cm: MSE=0.062, MAE=0.090, ubRMSE=0.229
  Epoch 2: still running (resumed from mid-epoch batch 501, currently ~batch 653/2050)

IO bottleneck analysis:
  Root cause: GPFS stalls from L3/L6/L9 skip connection reads.
  L12 is already preloaded to /dev/shm (182GB, 24-min overhead) → no L12 stalls.
  L3+L6+L9 are read from GPFS per batch → 39% stall rate, avg spike ~9s.
  Current epoch wall time: ~2.2 hrs. Without stalls: ~19 min (7× speedup).

  Options considered and ruled out:
    - Rechunking zarr: N/A — data is in consolidated .pt files, not zarr
    - Per-date .pt files (chunk_size=1 equivalent): tried before consolidation,
      hit HPC inode quota limit (millions of files not allowed)
    - Preload L3/L6/L9 to /dev/shm: too large (~700GB), doesn't fit
    - Local NVMe copy: unknown if Snellius GPU nodes have local NVMe

Two-phase training proposal (deferred — revisit after convergence check):
  Phase 1 — no UNet decoder, no L3/L6/L9 skip reads:
    Full transformer sequence unchanged (DEM, LULC, Soil, anchor spatial×196,
    S2/S1 history, ERA5, SIF, TWSA). L12 still preloaded to /dev/shm.
    Output: PointHead MLP → (B, n_depths) point prediction directly.
    No GPFS stalls → ~19 min/epoch. Clean ablation baseline for paper.

  Phase 2 — add UNet decoder (full model, same as current architecture):
    Load Phase 1 transformer checkpoint.
    Restore UNet decoder + L3/L6/L9 loading.
    Training signal: same point loss at center pixel + TV regularization.
    Spatial tokens (196) from transformer become (B, 768, 14×14) bottleneck.
    Strategy: freeze transformer 3-5 epochs → unfreeze end-to-end.
    IO stalls return (L3/L6/L9 back) → same ~2.2 hrs/epoch.

  Decision criterion: implement two-phase ONLY IF full model loss stagnates
  after 10+ epochs. If loss keeps improving, current approach is fine.
  Revisit after reviewing multi-epoch convergence curves.

Session 7 — 2026-06-15
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

Context: Previous full run (job 23864068) OOM'd. Last smoke test (job 23868316,
run "smoke_gather") showed catastrophic gpu_util=4% due to a bad §3c architectural
change (workers return indices only, main process gathers L12 from /dev/shm). That
change was reverted. This session focused on diagnosing and fixing the OOM root
cause and a separate DDP rank imbalance issue.

--- Working tree reverted to last commit (cdd8f42) ---

The §3c indices-only IPC approach caused data=875s compute=36s gpu_util=4%.
Reverted to the parallel multi-worker IPC approach (workers copy full L12 tensors
into shared memory, prefetch_factor controls queue depth).

--- OOM root cause and fix ---

Runbook memory budget was built on estimated /dev/shm=91 GB, but actual measured
value on the H100 gcn node is 145 GB (+54 GB). With prefetch_factor=3, the
boundary peak was:
  /dev/shm L12:  145 GB
  rank heaps:    159 GB
  train IPC:     440 GB  (32 workers × pf=3 × batch)
  val IPC:        61 GB
  Boundary peak: 805 GB > 790G SLURM limit → OOM

Fix 1: prefetch_factor dropped 3 → 2
  Train IPC drops from 440 GB → 242 GB.
  New boundary peak: 607 GB → 183 GB headroom vs 790G.

Fix 2: gc.freeze() inserted in train.py after both datasets are built, before
  DataLoader forks workers.
  Mechanism: gc.freeze() moves all existing Python objects into a permanent GC
  generation that the collector never scans. Without it, the GC traversal touches
  refcount fields on CoW-shared pages → kernel gives each worker a private RSS copy
  → RAM blowup across 32 worker processes (observed in earlier sessions).
  With gc.freeze(): refcount fields are never touched after fork → CoW pages stay
  shared → ~159 GB rank heap stays as one physical copy.

Runbook (text/training_runbook.md) §2 and §4 updated with actual measured numbers:
  /dev/shm = 145 GB (not 91 GB estimated), pf=2 budget, dual-limit table for
  720G shared vs 790G gcn nodes.

--- DDP rank imbalance fix (model.py) ---

Symptom: G0 = 100% GPU util, G3 = 30% at DDP gradient barrier.
Root cause: _pyramid_from_l12() in model.py used .nonzero() to find valid
  (non-padded) acquisitions. nonzero() output size depends on data → PyTorch
  must sync CPU↔GPU to determine output shape → different ranks stall for
  different durations depending on how many valid acquisitions they have →
  imbalance at the DDP allreduce barrier.

Fix: removed .nonzero() and all gather/scatter logic (~22 lines deleted).
  New approach: run spatial_pyramid_pool on ALL rows (padded l12=zeros →
  zero output from pool), then multiply by flat_valid[:, None, None] to zero
  out padded slots. No GPU sync required. All ranks process the same number
  of rows (MAX_S2 + MAX_S1) regardless of how many are valid.
  Zero-init invariant documented in model.py comment: padded l12 slots must
  be initialised to zero (guaranteed by dataset.py pre-allocation).

--- Code review (multi-agent ultra review) ---

Four additional fixes found and applied:
  1. val_loader had prefetch_factor=2 hard-coded; now reads CONFIG["prefetch_factor"].
  2. val_loader had no guard for val_num_workers=0 (persistent_workers and
     prefetch_factor are now conditionally set only when val_num_workers > 0).
  3. slurm/train.sh had redundant --num-workers 8 before $@ (removed; $@ already
     passes --num-workers from the sbatch command line).
  4. model.py _pyramid_from_l12 zero-init invariant documented in comment.

--- Commits pushed ---

  7a18f77 — Fix OOM risk: pf 3→2, gc.freeze() before fork, update budget with
             measured numbers
  dcf888c — Remove .nonzero() GPU sync from _pyramid_from_l12 to fix DDP rank
             imbalance
  03dbc82 — Fix val_loader guards, remove redundant --num-workers, document
             padding invariant

--- Memory budget for full run (confirmed) ---

  /dev/shm L12:   145 GB
  Rank heaps:     159 GB
  Train IPC:      242 GB  (32 workers × pf=2)
  Val IPC:         61 GB
  Boundary peak:  607 GB  → 183 GB headroom vs 790G ✓

--- prefetch_factor vs CudaPrefetcher clarified ---

  prefetch_factor: DataLoader workers stay ahead of main process (CPU→CPU,
    IPC shared memory buffers). Controls queue depth of assembled batches.
  CudaPrefetcher: overlaps H2D transfer of batch N+1 with GPU compute of
    batch N (CPU→GPU, async CUDA stream). These two mechanisms stack
    independently.

=== 2026-06-17 (Session 11) ===

--- Context ---

Job 23888806 (baseline_huber) OOM'd at epoch 4 on gcn140. Root cause diagnosed this
session: DataLoader IPC carried full L12 tensors (30 MB/sample), creating 437 GB of
IPC traffic combined with 688 GB persistent worker base RAM, exceeding the 720 GB
cgroup limit.

--- OOM root cause ---

Full memory accounting at OOM boundary:
  /dev/shm L12 preload:  145 GB  (unchanged — workers still read L12 to pool it)
  Rank heaps:            159 GB
  Train IPC:             242 GB  (8w × pf2 × bs128 × 30 MB × 4r)
  Val→Train boundary:    +61 GB  (val IPC overlaps with train worker startup)
  TOTAL at boundary:     607 GB  → should be safe under 720 GB
  ACTUAL at epoch 4:     >720 GB → OOM
  Discrepancy: persistent DataLoader workers accumulate RSS over epochs; epoch 4
    showed 688 GB already used before val phase, leaving no headroom for 61 GB val IPC.

--- Fix: move all pyramid pooling from GPU to CPU (in DataLoader workers) ---

Key insight: the 30 MB/sample IPC cost comes from transferring full L12 token arrays
(s2_l12 [MAX_S2,196,768] + s1_l12 [MAX_S1,196,768]) through shared memory. If pyramid
pooling is done in the worker before IPC, each sample sends only a pooled tensor
(MAX_S2,4,768) + (MAX_S1,4,768), reducing per-sample IPC from 30 MB → ~2 MB (15×).

New function: _cpu_pyramid_pool(l12, token_mask) → (M, 4, 768)
  Static masked-mean over 4 nested spatial windows (widths [1,3,5,7]) on the 14×14
  token grid. Runs in DataLoader worker on CPU before IPC, no GPU involved.

S2/S1 random token dropout (ContextFormer augmentation):
  Training only: 50% random dropout applied to token_mask before pooling for S2 and S1.
  Simulates cloud cover and orbit gaps. Controlled by training: bool=False param in
  load_s2_rolling_zarr / load_s1_rolling_zarr. DEM/LULC: no dropout (always available).

Batch key changes:
  BEFORE: s2_l12 (MAX_S2,196,768) + s2_token_mask (MAX_S2,196) — 30 MB total
  AFTER:  s2_pyr (MAX_S2,4,768) + s1_pyr (MAX_S1,4,768) + dem_pyr (4,768) +
          lulc_pyr (4,768) — ~2 MB total

model.py changes:
  - Removed s2/s1/dem/lulc_pyramid_attn from __init__ (learned attention weights)
  - forward() reads pre-pooled tensors directly from batch
  - spatial_pyramid_pool, _pyramid_from_l12, _static_pyramid marked NOT USED

Architecture decision — learned attention dropped:
  Previous: nn.Linear(768,1) attention weights per spatial window per modality.
  New: simple masked mean (all valid patches weighted equally).
  Rationale: minimal expressiveness difference vs transformer cross-attention already
  present; avoids GPU-side computation that prevented CPU pre-pooling.

--- Memory impact ---

  Per-sample IPC:     30 MB → ~2 MB        (15× reduction)
  Train IPC:         242 GB → ~16 GB
  Val→Train boundary: 607 GB → 324 GB      (396 GB headroom vs 720G cgroup limit)
  /dev/shm L12 preload: 145 GB unchanged   (workers still read L12 to pool it)

test_pyramid_equiv.py: 5 checks all passed
  - shape: (M, 4, 768) correct for all modalities
  - zero-slots: padded acquisitions produce zero output rows
  - determinism: same input → same output
  - finite: no NaN or Inf in output
  - scale consistency: pooled values in expected range

Agent code review (multi-agent ultra): no critical issues found.

SESSION 12 — 2026-06-16
================================================================================

--- Context ---

Job 23921632 (baseline_huber, pyramid pooling fix, 661 stations) was submitted at the
end of Session 11. This session focused on two code review agents running in parallel,
fixing the memmap load bug discovered by those agents, and comparing zarr vs memmap
data-loading throughput via smoke tests.

--- Agent-based code review (two parallel agents) ---

Bug review agent findings:
  - Tautological ternary in dataset.py: `best_orbit if best_orbit == "s2" else best_orbit`
    always evaluates to best_orbit regardless of branch — dead code.
  - Silent zarr fallback: when use_mmap=True but no .npy files are found for a station,
    the code silently falls back to zarr with no warning — hard to diagnose in production.
  - All other correctness checks passed: index alignment, fork safety, key format,
    use_mmap plumbing to val dataset.

Performance review agent findings:
  - Memmap fix is theoretically sound: zarr page cache stores compressed bytes; .npy mmap
    is zero-copy after warmup, giving a real throughput advantage.
  - Red flags: page cache margin tight (~95 GB shortfall for 525 GB of L3/L6/L9 data on
    an 811 GB node), GPFS+fork mmap behaviour uncertain.
  - step_time growth across epochs (27 → 30 s/batch) is a secondary co-cause of
    data_time degradation, independent of the memmap fix. Root cause: sequence length
    grows in the TemporalTransformer as more acquisitions accumulate across epochs (more
    unique dates sampled → longer attention sequences).

--- Bug fixes committed ---

Commit 67f1635 — Two fixes in dataset.py:
  1. Tautological ternary removed: `best_orbit if best_orbit == "s2" else best_orbit`
     replaced with just `best_orbit`.
  2. Init-time warning added: when use_mmap=True but no .npy files are found for a
     station, a UserWarning is emitted (was previously silent zarr fallback).

Commit 229148f — Critical memmap load bug fixed:
  convert_l369_to_npy.py creates raw binary files via np.memmap (no .npy header),
  but dataset.py was calling np.load(..., mmap_mode="r") which expects a .npy header.
  This caused an immediate crash on any station that had .npy files.
  Fix: read the array shape from the .json sidecar file and use
  np.memmap(dtype="float16", mode="r", shape=shape) directly, bypassing np.load
  entirely.

--- Job history ---

Job 23921632 (baseline_huber, zarr, no memmap): ran 9+ hours, reached epoch 3.
  Epoch 3 data_time=5200s, gpu_util=7% — data loading dominated and GPU nearly idle.
  Cancelled to avoid wasting SBUs.

Job 23888806 (baseline_huber, previous): OOM-killed at 1h33m (pre-pyramid-pooling-fix).

SBU used so far: ~8,400 on L2 GPU budget (out of 800,000 total).

--- Smoke tests comparing zarr vs memmap ---

Smoke test zarr (job 23936317, 20 stations, 3 epochs):
  Completed cleanly.
  data=22s/epoch, compute=3-6s, gpu_util=12-21%.

Smoke test memmap — first attempt (job 23936316):
  Crashed immediately on the np.load mmap_mode bug (commit 229148f fixes this).

Smoke test memmap fixed (job 23936647, 20 stations, 3 epochs):
  Completed cleanly.
  data=10-11s/epoch, compute=3-6s, gpu_util=23-35%.
  Result: 2× faster data loading confirmed vs zarr smoke.

--- Key insight discussed ---

GPU util dropping (22% → 7%) and data_time growing are the same problem viewed
differently — both reflect the GPU waiting for data. However step_time also grew
across epochs (27 → 30 s/batch) in job 23921632, suggesting sequence length growth
in the TemporalTransformer is a secondary co-cause that memmap alone will not fix.

--- Context ---

Job 23936932 (baseline_huber, memmap fix) is running on gcn128 with epoch 14

at 0.0001 (21× gap). VRAM is stable at 38.6 GB (empty_cache() fix confirmed

--- Job status ---

  Job: 23936932  run-name: baseline_huber  node: gcn128
  Epoch 14:
    train_loss=0.0001  val_loss=0.0021  (21× gap — overfitting)
    0-10 cm:   ubRMSE=0.0530
    10-30 cm:  ubRMSE=0.0486
    30-100 cm: ubRMSE=0.0570
  VRAM: 38.6 GB (stable — no creep between epochs)
  Early stopping: fires at epoch 22 (~2-3 h from session end)

--- Overfitting audit (agent-verified) ---

An external critique of the training curves prompted a full audit of all
regularisation levers. Findings:

  Spatial/temporal split — CLEAN. Zero location-group overlap between train
    and val splits. Spatial leakage is not the cause of the train/val gap.

  Stochastic depth (drop path) — ABSENT. Identified as the highest-priority
    missing regulariser. Not in model.py at all; timm's DropPath was the
    natural choice but not yet added.

  UNetDecoder dropout — ABSENT. Transformer had Dropout(0.1) but the
    UNetDecoder _ConvBlock had zero dropout.

  ERA5 temporal masking — ABSENT. All 365 days of ERA5 always visible to the
    model during training; easy data augmentation that forces robustness.

  Weight decay — ALREADY at 0.05 (sufficient; no need to increase).

--- Decision: combine all fixes into cls_perdepth_huber ---

Rather than incremental runs (perdepth_huber → cls_perdepth_huber), all
regularisation and architecture improvements are folded into a single new run:
  - Per-depth loss: equal-weight Huber loss per depth (not mean across depths)
  - Stochastic depth: drop_path_rate=0.1, linear schedule across 6 transformer layers
  - Dropout in UNetDecoder _ConvBlock: nn.Dropout2d(0.15) after second ReLU
  - Dropout in ERA5/SIF/TWSA MLPs: nn.Dropout(0.1) between linear layers
  - ERA5 temporal masking: 15% of valid timesteps zeroed during training only
  - CLS depth tokens: 3 learnable tokens (one per depth), prepended to transformer
    sequence; per-depth FiLM + heads in UNetDecoder

--- Code changes implemented ---

model.py:
  - Added DropPath + DropPathTransformerLayer classes (no timm dependency)
  - _ConvBlock: added nn.Dropout2d(0.15) after second ReLU
  - era5_mlp, sif_mlp, twsa_mlp: added nn.Dropout(0.1) between linear layers
  - SoilMoistureModel: new params drop_path_rate=0.1, use_cls_depth=False;
    replaced nn.TransformerEncoder with nn.ModuleList of DropPathTransformerLayer
    + nn.LayerNorm; added self.depth_tokens when use_cls_depth=True
  - UNetDecoder: use_cls_depth param; pre_head_drop; per-depth depth_film + heads
    when use_cls_depth=True
  - forward(): prepends depth tokens, manual transformer loop, extracts depth_ctx,
    passes to decoder
  - Param delta with CLS enabled: +297,600 params (50.05M → 50.35M)

train.py:
  - CONFIG: added drop_path_rate: 0.1, use_cls_depth: False
  - evaluate(): per_depth=False param wired through
  - Model instantiation: passes drop_path_rate and use_cls_depth
  - argparse: --per-depth-loss and --use-cls-depth flags
  - Main loop: per_depth=CONFIG["per_depth_loss"] in both train_one_epoch and
    evaluate calls

dataset.py:
  - ERA5 temporal masking: 15% of valid timesteps zeroed (training only)

text/training_runbook.md:
  - Added §12.3 overfitting audit table (verdict on all 6 external suggestions)
  - Added §12.4 all fixes for cls_perdepth_huber (4 implementation details)
  - Added §12.5 updated experiment sequence (2-run table)

SESSION 14 — 2026-06-17
================================================================================

--- Context ---

Job 23936932 (baseline_huber, memmap, 661 stations) was cancelled at epoch 14.
val_loss had been flat at 0.0021 since epoch 2; best checkpoint was epoch 3.
This session diagnosed why checkpoints appeared stuck at epoch 3 despite 7 "New
best val_loss" log lines, fixed the root cause, audited the train/val/oos split,
and passed a smoke test with the CLS per-depth architecture.

--- Checkpoint stalling root cause: GPFS write buffering ---

Symptom: last.pt and best.pt both reported epoch=3 even though the training log
showed "New best val_loss — checkpoint saved" at epochs 1, 2, 3, 4, 9, 10, 11.
Also: val_station_metrics.csv (58 KB) stopped updating after epoch 3.

Ruled out:
  - GPFS login-node page cache: direct I/O (dd iflag=direct) still read epoch=3.
  - Preemption/requeue: log showed "No checkpoint found — starting fresh" and no
    SIGTERM events.

Root cause confirmed: GPFS client-side write buffering. torch.save writes ~600 MB
synchronously from Python's perspective (returns immediately), but the GPFS client
on gcn128 buffers the data in its local page cache and flushes to storage servers
asynchronously. Epoch 3's write happened to flush due to cold-write cache pressure;
epochs 4–14 were accumulating in the buffer and were lost when scancel terminated
the job. Evidence: val_station_metrics.csv (written in the same is_main block)
also froze at epoch 3 — confirming that the entire is_main checkpoint/CSV block
stopped producing visible filesystem side effects after epoch 3.

--- Fix: _fsync_save() helper in train.py (~line 262) ---

Added _fsync_save(obj, path) that wraps torch.save() + os.fsync(fd) to force the
GPFS client to flush from its buffer to the storage servers before returning.
Overhead: ~6 s per 600 MB file, ~18 s/epoch total (<4% overhead at ~7 min/epoch).

All 4 checkpoint save sites replaced:
  - Mid-epoch save (every 500 batches)
  - Pre-val save (val_pending=True)
  - Post-val last.pt
  - Post-val best.pt

--- Train/val/oos split audit ---

Phase 1 (category_filter: ["sm_only"]):
  587 train / 74 val / 181 oos stations

Distributions balanced: climate zones and IGBP land cover proportional across
all three splits.

Spatial stratification confirmed clean: all 783 location groups fall entirely
within one split — zero spatial autocorrelation leakage between train and val.

OOS (181 stations) is reserved for paper metrics and spatial maps.
Val is internal-only (early stopping signal). Neither set is touched during training.

--- Smoke test (job 23956770, smoke_cls) ---

Config: --max-stations 5, --max-epochs 3, --per-depth-loss, --use-cls-depth
Result: PASSED
  - 3 epochs completed cleanly, no NaN, finite loss

SESSION 15 — 2026-08-01
================================================================================

--- Goal ---

Remove the TV (total-variation) regularizer from the loss and launch a run.
Rationale (user): TV smooths the 224x224 output map. This matches the Tier-1
verdict (2026-07-01) and the Step-0 capacity check (job 24352962, 2026-07-02,
"CAPACITY CONFIRMED"): the decoder CAN paint structure under dense supervision,
so the flat maps are a SUPERVISION problem, and TV actively rewards flatness.

--- Change made ---

train.py:211  lambda_tv default 0.1 -> 0.0  (TV disabled by default).
  _compute_loss already gates on `if lambda_tv > 0.0`, so loss is now pure
  masked_huber_loss (+ lambda_boundary=0.1 clamp, unchanged). The --lambda-tv
  CLI flag still lets any run re-enable it. Loss is Huber-only going forward.

Caveat recorded (Tier-1 verdict): removing TV stops the map being pushed flat
but does NOT by itself create structure — only pixel (112,112) is supervised.
The real fix is the Step-1 proxy-consistency structure loss (S1 VV/VH or TWI),
per training_runbook §16.4. Deferred; user chose "just remove TV and run for now".

--- Smoke-test saga + queue workaround ---

gpu_h100 was 100% saturated (84->76 nodes allocated over the session, 0 idle);
the H100 smoke (job 25136830) sat PENDING >1h on (Resources). gpu_a100 had 26
idle nodes, so cancelled the H100 smoke and resubmitted on A100:
  - New script slurm/train_a100_smoke.sh (partition=gpu_a100, mem=240G).
  - A100 = 40 GB VRAM (vs H100 80 GB), so batch cut to 32 (--batch-size 32).
  - Job 25138313 started instantly on gcn29.

--- ROOT CAUSE: scratch ZARR_ROOT was purged ---

A100 smoke COMPLETED in 62 s but was INVALID: "Dataset: 0 samples from 0
stations", "[SHM] L12 preloaded for 0 stations", peak_vram=0.4 GB, val_loss=0.0.
Nothing trained. Investigation (this is NOT a TV/A100/batch issue):
  - _preload_l12_to_shm (train.py:139) skips any store lacking a .complete marker.
  - ZARR_ROOT = /gpfs/scratch1/shared/pkhanal/zarr (scratch, dataset.py:39).
  - Scratch: 842 sm_only dirs present but 0 .complete markers; each station is a
    5 KB empty skeleton (chunk data gone). Scratch was purged since Jun 17.
  - Backup /projects/prjs1968/zarr_tokens INTACT: 842/842 .complete, ~352 MB/station.

Consolidated store layout confirmed — ONE per-station zarr holds all three:
  tokens  : s2(+l3/l6/l9), s1_asc/s1_desc(+l3/l6/l9), dem, lulc, *_token_mask
  inputs  : era5, sif, twsa, soil, cm (cloud mask)
  labels  : labels/{sm,dates,depths,qc}
So the purge wiped tokens + inputs + labels together for every station; the
/projects backup is the only surviving complete copy.

Caveat: scratch era5/labels dirs were dated Jul 19 (newer than backup's Jun 29).
If era5/labels were regenerated Jul 19, those edits were purged and are NOT in
the backup (unrecoverable). Backup's Jun 29 version is what gets restored.
