        e1     e3     e4     e5     e6 |   e1      e3      e4      e5      e6
  0-10  .0540  .0531  .0529  .0531  .0527 | -.0161  -.0019  +.0070  +.0030  +.0065
 10-30  .0488  .0486  .0486  .0494  .0489 | -.0066  +.0028  +.0107  +.0076  +.0137
30-100  .0541  .0559  .0561  .0568  .0566 | +.0185  +.0146  +.0190  +.0234  +.0245

0-10 and 10-30 are flat. 30-100 drifts WORSE from epoch 3 on (ubRMSE .0559 ->
.0566) and its wet bias grows monotonically (+.0146 -> +.0245). The depth-
averaged val_loss completely masked this — the scalar looked "converged" while
one depth was actively degrading.

Val station coverage per depth (val_station_metrics.csv, epoch 6):
    0-10   74 stations  127,625 samples
   10-30   51 stations   92,911 samples
   30-100  43 stations   74,384 samples
So deep coverage is ~58% of surface — thinner, but not catastrophically sparse.

Also note pooled vs station-mean ubRMSE for 30-100 at epoch 6: pooled .0566 vs
station-mean .0491, median .0474. Large gap => strong heterogeneity; a minority
of stations carry most of the deep error.

--- ROOT-CAUSE FINDING: only 195 parameters are depth-specific ---

train.py CONFIG never sets use_cls_depth, so it falls through to the default
False (model.py:204). That selects the else-branch of the decoder head:

    model.py:241    self.head = nn.Conv2d(c[3], n_depths, 1)
    with dec_ch = (512, 256, 128, 64)  ->  Conv2d(64, 3, 1)

    depth-specific params:        195   (192 weights + 3 biases)
    shared by all three depths: 50,050,749
    => 99.9996% of the network receives ONE summed gradient.

All three depths are three linear read-outs of the SAME shared 64-channel
feature map. A depth difference can only be expressed as a different linear
projection of one representation.

Corroborated independently by the DDP warning already in the job log:
    "grad.sizes() = [3, 64, 1, 1]"
which is exactly this head.

Interpretation: train_loss falling to 0.0004 while 30-100 val DEGRADES is not
an underfitting signature. The shared trunk is being fit to the abundant
surface labels and is dragging the deep read-out along with it. That is what
195 depth-specific parameters would predict.

--- Why per-depth LOSS cannot fix this (settled this session) ---

per_depth_loss=True (train.py:210) computes Huber separately per depth then
takes the mean (model.py:735-748). That gives each depth EQUAL WEIGHT in one
shared gradient. It does NOT produce separate updates per depth.

"Train each depth separately" is not actually available here:
  - L0.backward(); L1.backward(); L2.backward(); step()
    PyTorch accumulates grads, so this computes grad(L0+L1+L2) — mathematically
    IDENTICAL to the current mean, up to the 1/3 scale. Just slower.
  - Stepping between each backward makes the result order-dependent and triples
    the effective LR. Strictly worse, and still moves all 50M shared params.
Separate training requires separate PARAMETERS. The lever is architecture, not
the loss function.

--- Planned changes (DESIGNED, NOT YET IMPLEMENTED) ---

1. Per-depth train + val loss reporting.
   masked_huber_loss (model.py:725) gains return_breakdown=False -> returns
   (loss, depth_loss, depth_count), both (n_depths,), detached and kept
   on-device (counts[d] = mask_d.sum(), no .item(), so no new GPU syncs; the
   existing `if mask_d.any():` already syncs once per depth per batch).
   _compute_loss (train.py:356) forwards them; train_one_epoch (L434) and
   evaluate (L520) accumulate sample-weighted sums and return the RAW SUMS so
   the DDP reduction is correct (all_reduce SUM, then divide).
   Sample-weighting, not mean-of-batch-means: deep coverage is sparse, so batch
   -mean would over-weight batches holding two deep samples.
   CONSEQUENCE to document in code: the mean of the new per-depth losses will
   NOT exactly equal val/loss (val_loss is mean-of-batch-means-of-depth-means).
   val_loss must stay byte-identical for baseline comparability.
   EDGE CASE: the DDP reduce block (train.py:936) is guarded by
   `epoch != val_pending_epoch`, and the val-pending path (L857) skips training
   entirely — initialise the vectors so that path cannot NameError.
   New W&B keys: train/{depth}/loss, val/{depth}/loss, val/{depth}/MSE (printed
   today but never logged), val/worst_depth_loss.

2. use_cls_depth=True — the real lever, and it is ALREADY WRITTEN, just never
   switched on. It gives each depth its own learned CLS token into the
   transformer (model.py:411, 689, 703) plus its own FiLM modulation and its
   own head (model.py:237-239, 264-269):

       depth_tokens    3 x 768              =   2,304
       depth_film      3 x Linear(768,128)  = 295,296
       heads           3 x Conv2d(64,1,1)   =     195
       depth-specific     195 -> 297,795   (1,527x)
       total model  50,050,944 -> 50,348,544  (+0.59%)

   KEY SAFETY PROPERTY: FiLMLayer is identity-initialised (proj.weight zeroed,
   scale bias=1, shift bias=0 — model.py:158-160), so at step 0 x_d == x for
   every depth and the model is behaviourally IDENTICAL to the current
   baseline. It cannot regress at init; it can only learn to differentiate.

   ONE FIX TO MAKE ALONGSIDE: depth_tokens is torch.zeros(3, 768)
   (model.py:411) — all three tokens identical, so they are symmetric and must
   break symmetry purely through gradient differences. Use
   nn.init.trunc_normal_(std=0.02) (standard ViT CLS init). One line.

   Requires a FRESH RUN: the parameter set changes, so last.pt will not load.

--- Open caveat: information ceiling vs capacity limit ---

More depth capacity only helps if the deep signal is present but unextracted.
Physically:
  - S2 optical  -> surface reflectance, nothing below ~1 cm
  - S1 SAR      -> C-band, a few cm at most
  - 30-100 cm information can ONLY come from ERA5-Land, soil texture, and
    temporal memory.
No head architecture can pull deep moisture out of satellite tokens. If the
ERA5/soil/temporal pathway is the bottleneck, use_cls_depth buys a better
read-out of information the trunk never encoded, and it will plateau again.

Per-depth TRAIN loss is the diagnostic that separates the two:
   train 30-100 high & flat + val high  -> underfitting: capacity or info ceiling
   train 30-100 low        + val high   -> label scarcity / generalisation
These need opposite fixes, and today we cannot tell which we have.

If use_cls_depth does NOT move it, the next lever is physically motivated
rather than more parameters: CASCADE the depths — predict 10-30 as a residual
on 0-10, and 30-100 as a residual on 10-30 — so deep predictions inherit the
well-constrained surface representation instead of learning independently.
Premature until the diagnostic says capacity was the problem.

--- Addendum: the use_cls_depth path was already smoke-tested ---

Found in prior session notes after the above was written: the combination
"per-depth loss + CLS depth tokens" was already code-complete and SMOKE-PASSED
as run name cls_perdepth_huber (smoke job 23956770, 2026-06-17). It was never
launched as a full run. So use_cls_depth=True is not new/untested code — it is
a switched-off path that has already run end-to-end. This materially de-risks
the next run.

Related caveat on comparing runs: the previous full run baseline_huber_memmap
(job 23936932) reached best_val ~0.00209 at epoch 11 with use_cls_depth=False.
That number is NOT comparable to this session's 0.002182 — that run POOLED all
(sample x depth) pairs into one Huber mean, whereas per_depth_loss=True means
per-depth Huber then averages. Different quantities. Compare per-depth
ubRMSE/MAE/bias (m3/m3) across runs instead, never the loss scalar.

--- Goal ---

Get the bundled run cls_depth_star_reg launched. It had been paused since S16
to combine three changes: per-depth loss reporting, use_cls_depth=True + star
residual, and heavier regularisation. Ended with the run live, the code
reviewed, committed and merged to main.

--- What happened, in order ---

1. Five smoke runs on H100 x4 to validate the bundled changes end to end:
     25234282  smoke_cls_star_reg    20 stations, 2 epochs  best_val 0.0038
     25234340  smoke_cls_star_reg2    6 stations, 1 epoch   best_val 0.0042
     25234719  smoke_cls_star_reg3    6 stations, 1 epoch   best_val 0.0043
     25235250  smoke_paramgroups      6 stations, 1 epoch   best_val 0.0041
     25235660  smoke_reviewfixes      6 stations, 2 epochs  best_val 0.0042
   Jobs 25234370 and 25234753 were cancelled during SHM preload to fold in
   fixes before they burned compute. No checkpoints written, nothing wasted.

2. Adversarial review of train.py, model.py and dataset.py BEFORE committing
   H100 hours. Nine real defects surfaced — two introduced by this branch,
   seven pre-existing and shipping in every run to date. Written up as
   runbook §19.12; the two that mattered most:

   - nan poisoned the per-depth diagnostic (model.py:783). `elem * valid`, and
     nan*False is nan, not 0. A non-finite prediction at an UNLABELLED depth
     cannot affect training (the scalar loss only ever sees pred[mask]) but it
     made depth_sum nan, survived all_reduce(SUM) to all four ranks, and
     silently nan'd train/{depth}/loss, huber_pooled and huber_depth_mean —
     while train_loss still printed a healthy number. That is precisely the
     signal §19.1 added the breakdown to obtain. This defect was introduced by
     c66eb52, i.e. it broke the branch's own headline feature.

   - Every resume reset the learning rate (train.py:943). ReduceLROnPlateau
     .load_state_dict is a bare __dict__.update and never writes back to
     param_groups, so the decayed LR lives only in the optimizer — and an
     unconditional `pg["lr"] = CONFIG["lr"]` loop overwrote it. train.sh sets
     --requeue and --time=120:00:00, so any preemption after the first LR drop
     jumped LR from e.g. 5e-5 back to 2e-4 while the restored scheduler.best
     and num_bad_epochs still believed the decay had happened. No crash, no log
     line — the run simply re-diverges, days in. This was the single most
     likely way this run could have been silently corrupted.

   Also fixed: non-atomic 600 MB checkpoint writes over the live last.pt
   (SIGKILL 30 s after SIGTERM leaves a truncated file that torch.load rejects
   on all four ranks -> crash-loop on requeue); weight decay hitting 10 decoder
   BatchNorm scales because the split was name-based and nn.Sequential names
   them positionally (decoder.conv1.net.1.weight — no "norm" substring), which
   §19.5 had just doubled the effect of; --weight-decay/--lr-patience silently
   reverting on resume; a bare ValueError crash-loop from the changed group
   sizes; mid-epoch resume reverting the LR; and endswith(".bias") dropping
   protection from six MultiheadAttention in_proj_bias tensors — the exact
   mirror of the BatchNorm bug, caught while fixing it.

   The mechanism diagnostic was also pointed at the wrong tensor:
   diag/depth_token_cos measured the learned INPUT parameters, but
   use_cls_depth going inert manifests in the transformer OUTPUTS, which can
   collapse while the inputs stay near-orthogonal. forward() now stashes
   batch-mean depth_ctx and training logs diag/depth_ctx_cos_*.

3. dataset.py came back with NO defects — verified against the data on disk,
   not just read: depth strings across all 993 stores, the 513-station QC
   realign (surplus proved entirely leading), 0 labels >1.0 at qc==0 out of
   5,192,395, ERA5 right-alignment across 1,598,711 observed label days, all
   8,667 memmap slots. Two guards added anyway, both against burning
   120 h x 4 H100s: _load_l12_shm now requires both .bin and .meta.json, and
   the SHM sentinel wait is bounded at 3 h instead of spinning for the full
   walltime if rank 0 died during preload.

4. test_per_depth_loss.py gained section 8 (optimiser param groups), including
   a regression witness that demonstrates the old name-based filter leaking 10
   norm scales. Full suite re-run in the terramind env: all pass.

5. Committed, pushed, merged.
     b81faf7  Pre-launch bug hunt: fix 9 defects across the three files
     5a5c271  Runbook §19.12: record the pre-launch bug hunt
   origin/main fast-forwarded ef31bcc -> 5a5c271. Done as
   `git push origin feat/per-depth-loss-reporting:main` + `git branch -f main`
   rather than checkout+merge, deliberately: job 25235976 was already live and
   re-reads train.py/dataset.py from this checkout on requeue, so a checkout to
   main would have exposed a window where those files held pre-fix code.

  --weight-decay 0.1 --drop-path-rate 0.2 --early-stop-patience 6 --lr-patience 3

Job 25235976, node gcn89, H100 x4, 120 h limit. Fresh run name — no checkpoint
dir existed, and one was required regardless because use_cls_depth changes the
parameter set. Expect Trainable parameters: 50,348,544 (was 50,050,944).
At sign-off the job was RUNNING, still in SHM preload (~145 GB, up to ~30 min),
no epoch line yet.

--- What to watch, in priority order ---

1. diag/depth_ctx_cos_* — NOT depth_token_cos. Both smokes that logged it read
   0.999, which is the inert-use_cls_depth signature. Not conclusive at 6
   stations x 2 epochs, but if it is still ~0.999 by epoch 3-4 at full scale
   then the CLS mechanism is not differentiating and the whole premise of the
   run has failed. The parameter cosine (0.087 / -0.039) will keep looking
   healthy throughout — ignore it.
2. train/30-100/loss — high and flat means capacity or information ceiling;
   falling while val/30-100/loss rises means label scarcity. Opposite fixes.
3. val/30-100/ubRMSE and bias — should stop the monotonic wet drift that was
   the S16 failure mode (+.0146 -> +.0245 across e3->e6).

Stated confound, accepted to save a run cycle: this run changes architecture
AND regularisation together. Mitigation is the per-depth TRAIN loss —
regularisation lifts all three depths together, architecture moves them apart.

If depth_ctx_cos says the CLS mechanism is inert, the next lever is a CASCADE
(10-30 as a residual on 0-10, 30-100 on 10-30), NOT more parameters.

--- Known and accepted, not fixed (§19.12.7) ---

  * evaluate() omits lambda_boundary, so val_loss optimises a slightly
    different objective than train_loss. Left unchanged to preserve
    comparability with pg7mw3xb; huber_pooled is boundary-free on both sides
    and is the honest cross-run metric. Flagged for a deliberate decision
    rather than changed silently.
  * One epoch of per-depth TRAIN loss logs nan after a val-crash resume.
  * depth_film[1:] receives zero gradient at step 0, self-resolving after one
    AdamW step — worth knowing if the first ~100 batches look flat.

--- Untested at scale ---

The full SHM preload path (~145 GB, worst observed 1901 s) including its new
3 h timeout guard. --max-stations makes the preloader and the train dataset
select different station subsets, so no smoke run exercises it. First real
exercise is this launch.

--- Comparison baseline ---

baseline_huber_notv_perdepth (W&B pg7mw3xb), compared on per-depth ubRMSE /
MAE / bias in m3/m3. Never compare the val_loss scalar across runs with
different loss definitions.

--- Still deferred, unchanged ---

Dense spatial supervision / Step-1 proxy-consistency structure loss
(runbook §16.4); distributed validation; skip-connection ablation.

--- Run cls_depth_star_reg (job 25235976) — still running at sign-off ---

  epoch  train      val        30-100 ubRMSE  30-100 bias
    1    0.001601   0.002512   0.0553         0.0194

Best = e6. Train fell 7x, val 11%. Gap 8x. All three startup checks passed
(Trainable parameters 50,348,544; Optimiser groups 70/94; SHM preload 1732.9s;
zero zarr [WARN]). Per-depth loss reporting works — three finite depths, no nan.

--- THE FINDING: two separate failures, not one ---

compute_metrics removes each station's own temporal mean before ubRMSE, so
within a station the anomaly error and the offset error are orthogonal:
    MSE ~ ubRMSE^2 + (per-station offset)^2

Measured DIRECTLY from val_station_metrics.csv (not derived) at e5:

    depth    RMS bias   mean bias   range            |b|>0.05
    0-10     0.0618     -0.0019     -0.156..+0.127   29/74
    10-30    0.0611     +0.0053     -0.216..+0.117   17/51
    30-100   0.0875     +0.0079     -0.219..+0.244   21/43

Mean bias ~0 while individual stations reach +/-0.15 -> the offsets CANCEL.
Global bias is meaningless per-site. Always take RMS of the per-station column.

So: (1) absolute level is wrong by 0.062/0.061/0.088, and (2) dynamics are
stuck at ubRMSE 0.054/0.050/0.055. Two deficits, they add in quadrature,
total RMSE ~0.082 at the surface. Fixing the offset does NOT improve ubRMSE —
it cancels out of the anomaly by construction.

--- §20.14 station-mean probe (job 25246010, ~2 min, CPU) ---

Question: is per-station MEAN SM predictable from what the model already sees?
Ridge chosen because it is WEAK (~600 rows under real L2 cannot memorise, so a
positive result cannot be an artefact); GBM as the nonlinearity control.
GroupKFold on location_group_id to match the split's zero train/val overlap.

Inputs verified complete: 661 sm_only train+val (587/74), zero dropped, all
with soil/era5/labels. Val 74 matches val_station_metrics.csv. 403 of 661 need
the qc/sm realign that _load_zarr_labels already handles.

RESULT (best RMSE on 74 held-out stations):
              probe    null    network
    0-10      0.0597   0.0752   0.0618
    10-30     0.0588   0.0763   0.0611
    30-100    0.0815   0.0877   0.0875

  * Level is only WEAKLY predictable — best beats null by 21/23/7%. Largely not
    determined by available inputs -> §20.7 Branch A.
  * 0-10 and 10-30: the network ALREADY MATCHES the probe. No untapped signal.
    Decoder-vs-transformer is moot there; do not spend a GPU job on it.
  * 30-100 is the exception and the only live Branch-B pocket: the network sits
    EXACTLY at null (0.0875 vs 0.0877), extracting nothing about deep level,
    while the probe reaches 0.0815.
  * GBM beat ridge at the surface (0.0597 vs 0.0647) -> the relation is
    NONLINEAR; my ridge-only preliminary run understated it by ~8%. Never run
    this ladder ridge-only.
  * lat/lon added ~0 -> the signal is physical, not geographic interpolation.

Honesty note recorded in the runbook: the pre-registered VERDICT rule
("Branch B if best < 0.90 x network") printed "network already extracts what
exists" for 30-100, where the ratio is 0.932 — misleading, because the rule
never compares network vs null and the network IS at null there. Rule needs
fixing; that line must not be quoted as printed.

--- §20.12.1 FiLM check — CLOSED, my premise was wrong ---

I had written that FiLM is the only route by which transformer context reaches
the decoder. It is not. The 196 target-day spatial tokens come from the
transformer OUTPUT (model.py:732-733) and go straight into decoder.bottle_proj,
so context reaches the decoder spatially, unconditionally and FiLM-free. FiLM
modulates only the frozen TerraMind skip branch — a side channel.

Measured anyway: FiLM HAS trained (element RMS ~1.3e-2, off zero), so the
zero-init-trap hypothesis is dead. But that is ~790x below AdamW's decay
equilibrium 1/lambda=10 and ~0.5% of the ballistic bound, so the gradient
reaching FiLM has been near-sign-random. Weight decay is not the explanation —
the undecayed biases barely moved either. One real finding: depth_film.0 is
2.5x smaller than .1/.2, and depth 0 is the star-residual base that predicts
absolute SM rather than an offset.

Also ruled out with evidence, do not re-attempt: more regularisation (§19.5
doubled wd and drop_path, memorisation curve unchanged); more depth capacity /
the cascade (30-100 has the LOWEST train loss); raising Huber delta (train
residuals are 0.020-0.027, already INSIDE delta=0.05, so delta is inactive
exactly where gradients are produced — and it targets the offset, which ubRMSE
removes by construction).

--- §21 SMAP + ECOSTRESS — ceiling tests before any build ---

User proposal: SMAP for absolute level, ECOSTRESS LST 70m as a dense spatial
supervision target. Critique recorded in §21.1:

  * SMAP is categorically different from anything §20.14 tested — soil/terrain/
    landcover/ERA5 are PROXIES for wetness, SMAP L-band MEASURES it. §20.14's
    negative was scoped to "not learnable from what we have"; SMAP is the thing
    we don't have. The finding motivates the idea.
  * BUT 36 km is ~1300 km2 and gives the REGIONAL mean, while our error is the
    station's deviation FROM its region — sub-pixel heterogeneity is exactly
    what SMAP averages away.
  * Prior from data in hand: lat/lon added ~0 to the ladder, so station means
    are not strongly spatially organised. Lowers the prior.
  * The proposal's emphasis is backwards: SMAP's strong suit is LEVEL, not
    ubRMSE — ERA5-Land already carries regional dynamics at 9 km, FINER than
    SMAP.
  * It changes the thesis from retrieval to SMAP downscaling, and hardens the
    baseline from "predict the global mean" to "resample SMAP to the station".

ECOSTRESS deferred by user decision. Banked: CMR confirms ECO_L2T_LSTE v002/003
run 2018-07-09 -> ongoing, so it covers at most 5 of the 7 training years and
ZERO of 2016-2017.

--- GEE access ---

Credentials had expired (Jun 3, invalid_grant). Default `earthengine
authenticate` failed — it wants gcloud, not installed. notebook auth_mode needs
an interactive paste-back, which a FIFO could not deliver (the write blocked).
Solved by splitting the flow across two processes using ee.oauth.Flow: process
1 builds the URL and persists flow.code_verifier to disk; process 2 calls
oauth._obtain_and_write_token(code, verifier, scopes). Worth remembering — this
is the reliable headless-HPC pattern for EE auth.

Catalog probe (2026-08-05):
  NASA/SMAP/SPL3SMP_E/006   n=4060    2015-03-31 -> 2026-08-03   PRESENT
  NASA/SMAP/SPL3SMP (36 km)                                      ABSENT
  NASA/SMAP/SPL4SMGP/008    n=33134   2015-03-31 -> 2026-08-02   PRESENT
  NASA/SMAP/SPL1CTB                                              ABSENT

  * The standard 36 km L3 is NOT in GEE. Only the 9 km Enhanced, which is the
    SAME 36 km footprints Backus-Gilbert interpolated — same information, finer
    posting, and finer helps against the sub-pixel objection.
  * L1C_TB is not a separate collection, but tb_h_corrected / tb_v_corrected
    are BANDS inside SPL3SMP_E. So brightness temperature comes free.
  * L4 has sm_rootzone (0-100 cm) and sm_profile — the only thing covering the
    10-30 and 30-100 bins, since L3 is surface-only. User chose L3 ONLY, to keep
    the independence claim (L4 is a land-model assimilation). Consequence
    accepted: 0-10 is SMAP's BEST case, so a failure there kills the idea
    without needing L4.

--- SESSION 20 addendum: SMAP result (evening) ---

§21.2 ceiling test COMPLETE. GEE auth recovered (see the auth pattern above),
NASA/SMAP/SPL3SMP_E/006 extracted for all 661 stations, 2016-2022, per-year
means then averaged. 651/661 with retrieval, 0 missing for brightness
temperature. Job: interactive, ~25 min. Ladder re-run = job 25250245.

RESULT (best RMSE on 74 held-out stations, ridge/GBM):

  block            0-10             10-30            30-100
  B6 +latlon       0.0647/0.0597    0.0599/0.0604    0.0836/0.0819
  B7 +smap_sm      0.0631/0.0607    0.0597/0.0604    0.0812/0.0782
  B8 +smap_tb      0.0627/0.0576    0.0603/0.0576    0.0806/0.0795
  network                  0.0618           0.0611           0.0875
  null                     0.0752           0.0763           0.0877

  1. SMAP does NOT break the wall. Gains 3.5%/2.0%/4.5% over the SMAP-free
     best. Level still only 23/25/11% over null. Worth ~3% on total RMSE — not
     worth a new acquisition pipeline plus reframing the thesis as downscaling.
     VERDICT: drop the SMAP integration.
  2. B8 > B7 CONFIRMED at both shallow depths — TB-derived features beat SMAP's
     own retrieval by 5.1% (0-10) and 4.6% (10-30). Raw brightness temperature
     normalised by ERA5 skin temperature predicts station mean better than the
     L3 product does. That is evidence the retrieval's climatological tau-omega
     assumptions (vegetation optical depth, albedo, roughness, effective soil
     temperature) discard information the observable retains. Holds regardless
     of whether SMAP is ever ingested — worth writing up independently.
  3. 30-100 flipped to Branch B on the pre-registered rule, correctly this time:
     best 0.0782 vs network 0.0875 vs null 0.0877. The network is AT NULL there
     and misses 10.6% that is demonstrably extractable. Interestingly the
     RETRIEVAL beats TB at depth (0.0782 vs 0.0795) — the reverse of the
     surface — consistent with vegetation/roughness corrections mattering more
     where the surface signal is weakest.
  4. Coefficients confirm SMAP contributes rather than rides along: smap_sm_am
     is the LARGEST single coefficient at 10-30 and third-largest at the other
     two depths.

  Practical note recorded: TB_h ranges down to 76.8 K, implausible for land
  (typical L-band land TB is 200-280 K). Those are water-contaminated pixels —
  coastal/lake cells. Noise in B8, not a blocker, but do not treat raw TB
  extremes as physical.

--- Run cls_depth_star_reg continued to e15 ---

  e11 0.002212 NEW BEST   e12 0.002208 NEW BEST   e13 0.002187 NEW BEST
  e14 0.002207            e15 0.002213

  I called this run converged at e5 and again at e9. WRONG BOTH TIMES — it
  found three consecutive new bests at e11-e13 after four flat epochs. Do not
  predict this run's stopping point from a short plateau.

  At e12 the network's implied per-station offset reached ~0.0569 (0-10) and
  ~0.0586 (10-30) — BETTER than the SMAP-augmented probe's 0.0576. So at the
  two shallow depths the network has now caught up with and passed everything
  the probe could extract. Strongest form of the negative result, and it only
  became visible because the run kept improving.

  Overfitting is now 21x (train 0.000103 vs val 0.002213) and val turned at
  e14-e15. But this is station memorisation, structural — §19.5 proved
  regularisation cannot touch it. The fix is anomaly retargeting (§20.7-A1),
  which removes the memorisable quantity from the target.

  10-30 crossed below ubRMSE 0.05 for the first time (0.0497 at e12) and is now
  the BEST depth, better than 0-10. Counterintuitive since satellites sense the
  surface — likely physical: 0-10 responds sharply to rain events and dries in
  days (high temporal variance), 10-30 is damped. The depth easiest to predict
  is not the one best observed.

--- GEE auth: the pattern that worked (headless HPC) ---

  Default `earthengine authenticate` wants gcloud (absent). notebook auth_mode
  needs interactive paste-back, which a FIFO cannot deliver (the write blocks).
  Solution: split the flow across two processes using ee.oauth.Flow —
    p1: flow = oauth.Flow(auth_mode="notebook"); persist flow.code_verifier
    p2: oauth._obtain_and_write_token(code, verifier, scopes)
  Also: EE point extraction fails on globally-scattered chunks with "User memory
  limit exceeded". Sort stations by coarse lat/lon cell first so each chunk is
  spatially compact. And do NOT ask EE for collection.mean() before sampling —
  reducing ~4000 images server-side stalls; sample per year and average locally.
  getInfo on a FeatureCollection aborts past 5000 elements.

--- GATE PASSED ---

Reproduced THREE independent quantities from train.py to 4 dp: pooled ubRMSE
0.0539/0.0500/0.0552, station-equal 0.0490/0.0459/0.0490, RMS offset
0.0585/0.0584/0.0851. The metric code agrees with train.py:compute_metrics.

--- Headline (station-equal) ---

  OOS  (180 novel stations, 2016-22): ubRMSE 0.0507 / 0.0489 / 0.0499
  OOT  (399 seen stations, 2023):     ubRMSE 0.0397 / 0.0357 / 0.0369
  OOST (98 novel stations, 2023):     ubRMSE 0.0542 / 0.0465 / 0.0569

Dynamics generalise to unseen stations almost for free — OOS ubRMSE is only
2-7% worse than val.

--- MAIN RESULT: the level failure is TRANSFER, not representation ---

OOT and OOST are both 2023, so year is controlled. RMS offset:

  OOT  (2023, SEEN stations)   0.0303 / 0.0289 / 0.0427
  OOST (2023, NOVEL stations)  0.0665 / 0.0713 / 0.0906
  OOS  (2016-22, novel)        0.0639 / 0.0646 / 0.0865

Level error is 2.2x larger on novel stations IN THE SAME YEAR. OOST sits with
OOS, not OOT — so 2023 is not an easy year; station-seenness is the whole
effect. The network CAN represent per-station level (0.030 for seen stations);
it cannot INFER it for novel ones. Confirms §21.8's attribution of the 21x
train/val gap to station-level memorisation, and strengthens §21.9 item 1
(anomaly retargeting removes exactly the memorised quantity).

--- Other findings ---

  * SNOTEL dominates the headline. ubRMSE 0-10 non-SNOTEL vs SNOTEL:
    OOS 0.0433 vs 0.0616 (+42%), OOT 0.0350 vs 0.0427, OOST 0.0455 vs 0.0602.
    SNOTEL is 41-60% of every split. EXCLUDING THEM, OOS 0-10 = 0.0433 —
    below the 0.05-0.06 typical ISMN range and approaching the 0.04 SMAP bar.
    8 of the 10 worst OOST stations are SNOTEL (up to 0.124). Report stratified.
  * 30-100 does not work. NSE_anom is negative on every held-out split
    (OOS -1.76): even after removing level error it is worse than predicting
    the station mean. Do not report 30-100 as functional.
  * NSE vs r-squared: at OOS 0-10, r2=0.611 but NSE=-0.76 and NSE_anom=+0.35.
    A single "R2" would have hidden either the skill or the failure.
  * §22.7 diagnostic fired: OOT error rises through 2023 while the OOST control
    traces a different shape -> consistent with memorised input context.
    Suggestive only; the decisive masking ablation is NOT yet run.

--- Pre-registered checks: 5 of 6 pass ---

  FAIL: OOST >= OOS at 10-30 (0.0465 vs 0.0489). Survives a like-for-like recut
  on the 98 common stations (0.0465 vs 0.0481) — a real small improvement in
  DYNAMICS. Level error still degrades in 2023 at every depth. Temporal
  extrapolation costs absolute level, not tracking.

  Check 1 ("OOT ~ val") is BADLY WRITTEN: two-sided at 25% tolerance, and OOT
  came in 19-25% BETTER, not merely close. Rewrite as a signed test.

--- Operational (measured, do not rediscover) ---

  * OOT needs ~156 GB of L12 in RAM (val 18, oos/oost 39). evaluate_meeting.sh's
    --mem=120G would OOM. Must del + gc.collect() between splits or oos+oot are
    resident together (~195 GB peak).
  * DISABLE_L12_CACHE=1 is NOT the fix: 3.9x slower (1:32 -> 5:59 on 8 stations)
    for ~0.13 GB/station saved. Would bust the 4 h wall.
  * The cost is INIT, not inference: OOT = ~66 min loading vs ~15 min compute
    at ~179 samples/s. Chunk it with --csv-start-idx/--csv-end-idx/--tag.
  * RAM and lazy paths give BIT-IDENTICAL predictions (verified).
  * Use ckpt_utils.load_checkpoint only — eval_stations.py and demo_plot.py
    build the model without use_cls_depth and crash on this checkpoint.

--- Addendum: §22.7 DOY diagnostic quantified (same day) ---

Refit the OOT error-vs-day-of-year trend properly, adding OOS 2016-22 (7 years x
180 novel stations) as the strong seasonality control. Trend in |error| anomaly
per 100 DOY, 95% CI from a station-clustered bootstrap:

              0-10                10-30               30-100
  OOT  2023  +0.0033 [.0023,.0041] +0.0045 [.0032,.0055] +0.0032 [.0011,.0048]
  OOST 2023  -0.0039 [-.0074,-.0002] -0.0016 (ns)        +0.0022 (ns)
  OOS 16-22  -0.0025 [-.0038,-.0012] -0.0013 (ns)        -0.0007 (ns)

OOT rises at all three depths; BOTH novel-station controls go the other way,
non-overlapping at 0-10 and 10-30. Seasonality would move all rows together —
it does not. SEASONALITY EXCLUDED; this is the memory-decay signature.

Magnitude ~+0.012 m3/m3 across the year = ~30% of OOT's 0.0397 ubRMSE. Part of
OOT's apparent advantage over val is borrowed from seen inputs and decays.
DO NOT quote OOT as clean temporal generalisation.

Residual confound not yet checked: if OOT records end mid-2023 more often than
OOST's, late-year samples have sparser tokens, mimicking decay. The masking
ablation is immune to it and is now justified rather than speculative.

--- CORRECTIONS to the §22 write-up (same day) ---

Two claims I made earlier today were WRONG. Both are fixed in §22.9.

1. "30-100 does not work — worse than predicting the station mean."
   WRONG. I quoted the MEAN NSE_anom (-1.76). NSE is unbounded below and a few
   catastrophic stations dominate the mean. Medians:

     OOS      mean     median   %stations>0   p10
     0-10    +0.347    +0.555      88.9%     -0.06
     10-30   -0.073    +0.476      82.4%     -0.26
     30-100  -1.761    +0.214      58.5%     -2.49

   The MEDIAN station at 30-100 beats its own climatology and 58.5% of stations
   do. 30-100 is the WEAKEST depth with a HEAVY FAILURE TAIL, not a broken one.
   Same pattern on OOST (0-10 mean -0.676 vs median +0.516).

   RULE: never quote mean NSE. Use median + fraction beating climatology + p10.
   (I added the median columns for exactly this reason and then didn't use them.)

2. "Exclude SNOTEL; OOS 0-10 is really 0.0433."
   WRONG, and it would have been cherry-picking in reverse. SNOTEL ubRMSE is
   33-42% higher, but their observed SD is 44-57% higher. Normalised:

     OOS 0-10        non-SNOTEL   SNOTEL
     ubRMSE            0.0433     0.0616  (+42%)
     observed SD       0.0623     0.0898  (+44%)
     ubRMSE/SD         0.682      0.630   <- SNOTEL BETTER
     median NSE_anom   +0.535     +0.602  <- SNOTEL BETTER

   The model handles SNOTEL RELATIVELY BEST at all three depths. Those sites just
   swing harder (snowmelt) and ubRMSE is absolute. Report a variance-normalised
   metric alongside ubRMSE; do not exclude stations.

   I also blamed the SNOTEL gap on frozen soil without testing it. ERA5 skt is in
   the zarr in Kelvin (100% join), so the SMAP-style flag skt_mean < 273.15 is
   free. It flags 23-32% of OOS rows but frozen days are only 1.05-1.11x worse
   -> masking moves overall MAE ~2.5%. The SNOTEL gap is a VARIANCE effect, not
   a freezing effect.

Net effect on the headline: the model looks BETTER than this morning's write-up,
not worse. 89% / 82% / 59% of unseen stations beat their own climatology on
dynamics at the three depths.

--- Failure-tail diagnosis + tomorrow's starting point ---

Diagnosed the NSE_anom < 0 tail on OOS. NOT a data bug. It is an AMPLITUDE
calibration problem.

  OOS 0-10        pred SD   obs SD   ratio
  NSE_anom < 0     0.0759   0.0456   1.67
  NSE_anom >= 0    0.0730   0.0757   0.98

Failing stations have 1.7-2.6x LOWER observed variability (84-90% below median),
but their correlation is fine (R ~0.62-0.65) — the shape is right, the amplitude
is wrong. The model emits nearly the SAME spread at every station regardless of
how variable that station is (0.0759 vs 0.0730 at 0-10 — essentially identical).
At 30-100 observed SD differs 2.6x between groups while the model's output
differs only 1.24x.

=> The network learned ONE typical soil-moisture rhythm and applies it
   everywhere. Same class of defect as the level failure — inability to infer a
   per-station property — but the second moment (sigma) instead of the first (mu).

Secondary, weaker: SCAN fails 18% at 0-10 / 40% at 30-100; Shrub-Savanna 31% at
0-10. Variance explains most of it; don't chase the network signal first.

NEXT SESSION — start here:

  1. GATE: is sigma_station predictable from soil/ERA5/terrain?
     Adapt station_mean_probe.py (already does this for mu) to target sigma.
     CPU-only, ~an afternoon, no GPU.
       - predictable    -> fix amplitude directly, no leakage, most of the win
       - not predictable-> anomaly retargeting is a REFRAME not a fix
  2. Only then decide on anomaly retargeting z=(theta-mu)/sigma. Implementation
     is modest (per-station mu/sigma from TRAINING YEARS ONLY in dataset.py,
     loss unchanged) but the back-conversion needs mu and sigma, which a novel
     station does not have. Computing them from the test period is leakage.
     If sigma is not predictable, the honest framing is "given a site's
     climatology, predict its anomalies" — what drought monitoring and data
     assimilation actually consume. The paper must say which claim it makes.
  3. Still not run: spatial maps (§22 Phase 4); §22.7 masking ablation.

FIGURES / TOOLING BUILT (all CPU unless noted)

§22.10  LEVEL CLAIM VERIFIED — AND MY EARLIER PHRASING CORRECTED

  Station-equal, novel stations: median |bias| 0.041-0.061 vs ubRMSE 0.044-0.059.
  |bias| ~ ubRMSE, so bias^2 is ~50% of MSE. "Level DOMINATES" was too strong;
  the two terms are the SAME SIZE. (57-70% was the observation-weighted figure.)

  What is specific to the level is TRANSFER.  seen -> novel:
      |bias|  x3.06 / x2.91 / x3.56
      ubRMSE  x1.23 / x1.39 / x1.47
      sd_obs  x1.06 / x1.08 / x1.02   <- novel stations are not more variable
  Level skill vs climatology (1 - RMS_model/RMS_null), 95% CI over stations:
      OOT  +63 / +67 / +55 %      (station seen in training)
      OOS  +22 / +22 / +12 %
      OOST +17 / +21 /  +8 % [-0.06,+0.20]   <- NOT distinguishable from climatology
  ...while median daily r stays 0.70-0.85 at those same stations.
  Offset fitted on the 1st half of a record removes 45-64% of 2nd-half MSE on OOS
  (oracle ceiling 60-73%): the offset is real, persistent and transferable.
  SECOND FAILURE, previously under-weighted: anomaly NSE on novel stations is
  0.56 / 0.48 / 0.22 by depth. Deep dynamics barely transfer either. A perfect
  level buys only -29% RMSE at the surface.

§22.11/22.12  "FOREST IS WORSE" IS ACTUALLY "SNOTEL IS WORSE" — AND NOT FROZEN SOIL

  Within Forest: SNOTEL 0.0572 vs non-SNOTEL 0.0429 (OOS 0-10). Within SNOTEL,
  land cover makes no difference (Forest 0.0572 vs Grass-Crop 0.0581).
  Frozen/unfrozen split by ERA5 skin temperature: frozen days are EASIER, and
  removing them WIDENS the SNOTEL gap (OOST 30-100: +0.0210 -> +0.0328).
  => warm-season property.  dataset.ERA5_VARS has NO snow variable at all
  (no snow depth, SWE or melt), so snowmelt — the dominant control at half the
  evaluation pool — is invisible to the model. Concrete, testable input gap.

§23  SPATIAL MAPS: THE §23.0 PREDICTION WAS WRONG, AND THE TRUTH IS WORSE

  Verified first: centre pixel of the rendered map == predictions_oos.parquet to
  max |d| = 4.88e-04 (tol 2e-3, bf16/batch-size), anchors exact, selftest pass.

  norm-std 0.093-0.524 vs the §16.1 baseline of 0.0065 — 14-80x higher, several
  panels above the §16.4 dense-supervision ceiling of 0.2504.  The maps are NOT
  flat; §16.1's magnitude is obsolete (its decoder-side MECHANISM still stands).

  THE CONTROL STATION INVERTED THE RELATIONSHIP:
      Crossroads  1 LULC class,  9.5 m relief -> n~ 0.317-0.524   (MOST structure)
      Iasi        5 LULC classes, 75 m relief -> n~ 0.133-0.282
      Tucson      2 LULC classes, 249 m relief-> n~ 0.093-0.201   (LEAST)
  r(anomaly,DEM) -0.04..+0.21, r(anomaly,NDVI) -0.10..+0.06, r(season~season)
  +0.34..+0.84 (independent fields give +0.03), variance retained at 14x14 = 64-83%
  => effective resolution ~160 m, not 10 m.  Structure painted is ANTI-correlated
  with structure present. Unconstrained is not empty: the decoder fills the
  50,175 unsupervised pixels with confident, physically meaningless structure.
  => Phase 2 must CONSTRAIN existing structure, not CREATE missing structure.
  => Do not present this model as producing 10 m soil-moisture maps.

§24  BUILT AND LAUNCHED — IS THE SATELLITE BRANCH DOING ANYTHING?

  No no-satellite ablation had ever been run. Three results say it may be doing
  little: (a) §20.14's imagery-free probe TIES the network at 0-10/10-30 and BEATS
  it at 30-100; (b) §22.10's novel-station level skill (8-22%) ~ the static probe's
  23%; (c) §23's non-physical maps.

  ablation.py: swap ONE modality between samples (shuffle, never zero — zeroing
  tests off-distribution shock, not use). cross_station = different site, same
  season (kills site identity); within_station = same site, different season.
  A modality moves as a WHOLE (tokens + masks + rel_pos + orbit) or you test
  incoherence. Donor map built from metadata, NOT a batch-dim permute — the eval
  loader is unshuffled, so batch-permuting would silently give a within-station
  shuffle labelled cross_station.

2026-08-12  Session 25 — why is the within-tile prediction flat?
================================================================================
Trigger: figures/tile_context/ISMN_TxSON_CR200-18.png — six stations in one 2.24 km
tile, observed mean SM 0.137-0.287 (spread 0.0601), predicted spread 0.0113 = 19% of
observed, r(pred level, obs level) = -0.175.

MEASURED FIRST (rules out the easy explanations)
  * Not a readout artefact: reading each station from its OWN centred patch still
    collapses — sd_pred 0.0158 vs sd_obs 0.0633, r = -0.11 over 18 OOS TxSON stations.
  * Not a general failure: level skill by scale, depth 0-10, predictions_oos.parquet —
    between networks r=+0.85 (ratio 0.91), within SCAN +0.78, SNOTEL +0.31,
    COSMOS-UK -0.05, TxSON -0.11 (ratio 0.25), one tile -0.18 (ratio 0.19).
    Skill decays monotonically with separation and vanishes below ~10 km.

§27a  TOKEN PCA->RGB MAPS  (plot_token_pca.py, figures/token_pca/)
  * Embeddings ARE spatially heterogeneous at 160 m, at every layer, and track the
    landscape — DEM PCA->RGB traces the drainage, LULC reproduces the tree patches.
    Sink-excluded PCA ratios high-rank throughout: S2 L3 .13 / L6 .10 / L9 .08 / L12 .09.
  * Pooling destroys it: variance kept 3% (S2 L3/L6/L9), 2% (L12), 1.5% DEM, 2.6% LULC.
  * Correction on record: the ANCHOR is NOT pooled (196 tokens, 4 layers, model.py:497-530).
    Only the history and DEM/LULC are. Earlier blanket "320 m" claim was wrong.
  * Dropped Moran's I and the per-token norm panel — magnitude statistics that a single
    register coordinate dominates by construction.

§27a.3-.6  MASSIVE-ACTIVATION REGISTERS  (three audit scripts + csvs/)
  * A DEM token at norm 1682 vs median 123 — but the raw raster under it is pristine
    (452.88-454.55 m, 254 unique values, no NaN). The spike is ONE coordinate at -1671.
  * Systematic: 970/993 stations (97.7%), median ratio 13x, median variance share 66.7%,
    ZERO masked by dem_token_mask.
  * Encoder-wide, not DEM/LULC-specific: dim 126 in every modality and layer, 87/328 at
    L9/L12. High-norm TOKEN is an L12 phenomenon (S2 1.5x at L9 -> 7.2x at L12; S1 never).
  * Dilution proven: dims 87/126 hold 88.4% of DEM's mean magnitude but 13.9% of the
    across-station variance; zeroing 2 of 768 dims drops median inter-station cosine
    0.783 -> 0.157. Input redundancy is only a weak modulator (r(DEM relief) = -0.217).
  * Fix available and cheap: per-dimension standardisation, exactly as ERA5 already gets.

§27b  DO EMBEDDINGS PREDICT MEAN SM?  (probe_token_sm_structure.py, 842 sm_only stations)
  * Global k-NN skill 9.8% (s2/l12) against 23.4% for the B8 tabular stack — ~2.4x worse.
    CAVEAT: k-NN is weaker than the ladder's ridge/GBM, so 9.8% is a FLOOR. B9 block not run.
  * Within-network 0-4.2%, FOUR of fourteen combinations negative.
  * Layer ordering flips vs §27a: L12/L9 beat L3/L6 here — global station-mean SM is a
    climate task and L12 is where climate lives.
  * UMAP (unsupervised fit, SM only for colour): Koppen classes separate cleanly, soil
    moisture is near-uniform scatter. The embedding organises by climate, not wetness.
  * Data quality: 4 stations with non-finite S1 tokens, ALL sm_and_flux (AmeriFlux_US-Ton,
    US-Var, ICOS_DE-HoH, FI-Var). sm_only is clean.
  * umap-learn 0.5.12 installed into the terramind env; add to environment.yml.

DESIGNED, NOT BUILT
  §28  Replace the U-Net decoder with a shared per-token head -> honest 160 m map. Cost
       goes DOWN (the decoder is the expensive part). Two independent defects: pooling
       (information never arrives) and the decoder (information arrives, 1 of 50,176
       pixels supervised). Position leakage is the trap; token-space translation crops +
       multi-station supervision are the mitigations.
  §29  LST over TxSON. Decisive difference is day/night, not revisit: Landsat C2 L2 ST is
       DAYTIME ONLY so it can never give a diurnal range; ECOSTRESS gives day and night
       (ISS orbit precesses). 70 m is fine here — stations are 6-13 pixels apart.
       Step 0 is a catalogue query to measure actual overpass-hour distribution.

NEXT, IN ORDER
  1. §27b.4 B9 +terramind ridge/GBM block (few CPU-hours) — turns the k-NN floor into a claim.
  2. §29 Step 0 catalogue query (minutes) — settles the sensor choice with evidence.

2026-08-13  SESSION 26 — §30 designed: per-location processor. No code, no GPU.
================================================================================

Whole session was diagnosis + design + write-up. Nothing was trained, nothing on the
GPU. §30 is in the runbook (line ~6181) with two architecture figures.

THE DIAGNOSIS THAT CHANGED THE PLAN
  * Verified at model.py:736-743 (read the source, did not trust a summary): `context`
    is the masked mean of EVERY valid non-CLS, non-spatial, non-pad token, applied
    through FiLM as a uniform per-channel scale/shift.
    -> ALL of the time-series signal (ERA5 365 d, S2/S1 history, SIF, TWSA) reaches the
       decoder through ONE SPATIALLY CONSTANT VECTOR. Two stations 500 m apart cannot
       have different RESPONSE FUNCTIONS, only different means. That is exactly the
       observed signature: temporal SD 0.051 vs 0.051 perfect, between-station SD 15-19%.
  * CORRECTION on record: I first wrote "100% of the temporal signal". Wrong — the anchor
    contributes one date's spatial snapshot with a staleness embedding, so the bottleneck
    IS date-dependent. The pattern can change between samples; the response to weather
    cannot vary across the tile. Also: that mean includes the pooled STATIC tokens
    (idx 0-11), which the code comment "temporal context for FiLM" glosses over.
  * The current model already HAS an S-like object (196 spatial tokens, model.py:731-734)
    and a g-like object (context). What is missing is per-location PROCESSING. They meet
    only inside FiLM, where the temporal half is broadcast uniformly.

WHY §27b DOES NOT CLOSE THE QUESTION (revised view)
  Time-averaging the embedding deletes the wetness signal and leaves a landscape
  descriptor — hence the Koppen-organised UMAP. Station-mean SM at global scale is a
  climate/texture/sensor-depth quantity, not the within-tile anomaly. Within-network is
  ~100 km, not 500 m. And it tested TERRAMIND EMBEDDINGS, NOT TERRAIN DERIVATIVES —
  independent claims, only the first was tested.
  Dropped the probe_variogram gate: the terrain probe is benchmarked against the model's
  own 15-19% / r=-0.18 on the SAME stations, so R^2_max is a thesis-writing number, not a
  build-decision number.

DATA FINDING — nothing needs downloading
  /projects/prjs1968/satellite_zarr has 993/993 stations of RAW 224x224 @10 m rasters:
  dem/data (1,224,224) float32 REAL ELEVATION 0 NaN, lulc (4,224,224), s1_asc (N,2,...),
  s2 (N,12,...). The full-resolution carrier §17.7 ranked as constraint #2 was on disk the
  whole time. I initially and wrongly said scratch purge had lost it — project_data_backup
  memory already said otherwise. Only MERIT Hydro (upa/hnd for TWI/HAND) needs fetching;
  that also kills the "TWI not computable from an isolated tile" rejection, since MERIT
  publishes routed flow accumulation globally.

MEASURED — sub-token variance fraction (§30.3a, 4 dense TxSON tiles)
  Share of each channel's variance surviving removal of its 16x16 token mean, i.e. the
  part a 160 m token CANNOT carry:
    curv_lap 0.96 | curv_plan 0.95 | tpi_100m 0.79 | S1 VV 0.76   <- tier 1
    socd 0.54 | soc 0.50 | northness 0.51 | S2 B08 0.47 | bd 0.44 | slope 0.42 | LULC 0.40
    ph 0.36 | clay/sand/silt 0.29/0.27/0.26  <- context encoder only
    ELEVATION 0.05  <- DROP from the per-pixel head, the token already has all of it
  THE METRIC MEASURES NON-REDUNDANCY, NOT USEFULNESS. Curvature scores ~0.95 almost
  tautologically (second derivative = high-pass, token means ~0). S1's 0.76 includes
  speckle. 4 semi-arid Texas tiles are not globally representative — elevation 0.05 and
  curvature 0.95 are structural, S1/LULC are landscape-dependent. Sufficiency still needs
  a regression of observed within-tile SM contrast on these channels. NOT YET RUN.
  Corrects subkm_design.tex "soil is noise" (3% clay across 6 stations): the full-tile
  range is 11 wt% clay / 22.75 wt% sand.

COLOCATION GEOMETRY (recomputed, correct station keys + %Y%m%d date parsing)
  Pairs with >=365 d overlap: 13 at 0-50 m (nugget, 5 networks) | 4 at 50-160 m |
  16 at 160-500 m | 29 at 500-1120 m. Signal pairs span 8 networks (ARM, AmeriFlux, ICOS,
  NGARI, SCAN, SMOSMANIA, SNOTEL, TxSON) — so a terrain probe is not confined to
  semi-arid Texas. Train split has only 12 off-centre pairs on 12 tiles, and
  location_group_id never spans splits (0 of 906) — multi-station supervision cannot
  carry a run.

§30 ARCHITECTURE (Contextformer-style, Benson et al. CVPR 2024)
  B1 context encoder, once/tile   -> S (196,768) per-location + g tile summary
  B2 weather encoder, once, SHARED -> W; ERA5 is 9 km, identical over the tile, never replicated
  B3 processor per location k, weights shared: [S_k, g, s2_hist_k, s1_hist_k] ~102 tokens,
     self-attn then cross-attn into W.  train 1 location / infer 196
  B4 per-pixel head, NO UPSAMPLING: nearest GATHER S[i//16,j//16] + measured 10 m rasters
     -> (B,3,224,224).  train 1 pixel / infer 50,176
  * Terrain enters at B1, not at the output — only then can it change the RESPONSE
    FUNCTION (a hollow drains slower), not just the mean.
  * Contextformer's unpatchify head does NOT transfer: it works because every pixel has an
    NDVI label. We have 1 point sensor in 50,176 pixels, so unpatchify would invent
    sub-token structure — §17.7's DiVAE objection.
  * Blockiness from the nearest gather is a DIAGNOSTIC, not a defect. Do not smooth it.
  * Un-pooling REDUCES payload: one history column (60,768)=92 KB vs the 4-scale pyramid
    (60,4,768)=184 KB. Training should get CHEAPER — UNetDecoder deleted.
  * NOT doing a water-movement loss: it asserts the relationship instead of measuring it.
    Honest version is TWI/HAND as INPUTS.
  * Expected gain is LOW and stated before the GPU is spent (§27b.8: own-token
    within-network skill 0-4.2%). Fallback stays §29 LST.

GIT / ROLLBACK (new standing preference)
  Tag `pre-s30-architecture` at 61c1773 = rollback point for cls_depth_star_reg.
  Three rollback layers: code (git), the trained model (checkpoints/, NOT in git),
  and --head token|pixel|unet keeping the old architecture runnable in the new codebase.
  Commits 61c1773, 14dcbed, 02f4bc9 pushed to origin/feat/per-depth-loss-reporting.

NEXT, IN ORDER (nothing started)
  0. Branch feat/per-location-processor.
  1. Grid check (~20 min, 3-4 tiles): curvature via dilated stencil vs native-derived,
     look for a spectral peak at the source-cell spacing. download_dem_cdse.py:202 fetched
     COPERNICUS_30 resampled to 10 m bilinear, so the array is 10 m but the information is
     30 m — derive on a 3-px dilated stencil, never a 10 m one. Decides a GLO-30 re-fetch.
  2. download_merit_hydro_gee.py — upa/hnd, 993 stations (soilmoisture env, GEE).
  3. build_static_stack.py + compute_static_stats.py -> static_hr (C<=24, 224x224) fp16.
  4. dataset.py emissions + translation crop + anchor_valid (dataset.py:483-486 silently
     returns all-zero anchors with orbit_id=0, indistinguishable from a real S2 anchor —
     latent bug, fix regardless).
  5. model.py block split + per-pixel gather head + pixel_idx loss.
  6. Regression gates -> smoke (20 stations, 3 epochs, measure data= and peak RAM against
     the 540 GB baseline) -> full run ~42 GPU-h.
  Untouched from earlier: §27b.4 B9 block; §29 Step 0; §26.3's mosaic_txson.py gate.

  AMENDMENT (same day, after §29 Phase A ran): §30.6's fallback said LST is "tied to
  wetness by evaporative cooling". §29.13 tested that and it is FALSE within-tile —
  daytime Landsat LST vs SM is -0.077 under station fixed effects; the +0.167 pooled
  figure is a Simpson's-paradox artefact of station identity. The §30 fallback now rests
  on §29.9 (LST as a DENSE AUXILIARY TARGET: 5776 px/tile vs 1 — supervision density, not
  correlation). Phase B (ECOSTRESS night + diurnal range) is the only untested arm that
  could still give a direct LST<->SM link. §30.6 amended in place.

PRIMARY OUTPUT
  text/architecture_per_location.txt — 1286 lines, restructured to be read cold,
  front to back: Part 1 summary → Part 2 symbols/grids → Part 3 flowcharts →
  Parts 4-15 detail.  figures/architecture_proposed.{png,pdf} regenerated to match.
  training_runbook.md §31 (~line 6772) is the OLD pre-DEM version and now carries
  a SUPERSEDED header pointing at the .txt.  DO NOT read §31 as current.

FOUR DECISIONS REVERSED OR CHANGED FROM §30, each argued rather than assumed

  1. OUTPUT IS 70x70 @ 32 m, not 224x224 @ 10 m.
     30 m does not divide the 2240 m tile — 74.67 cells, ragged 5.33 per token.
     32 m gives exactly 70 cells and exactly 5x5 per token, so the gather is an
     integer divide with no remainder.  6.7% coarser than 30 m, immaterial when
     the thermal band is 100 m.  Makes the resolution claim need no asterisk:
     every channel is at or below 32 m except the token and LST.

  2. BLOCK 4 DROPPED, THEN REINSTATED.
     Dropped when terrain was MERIT-only at 90 m — with no real sub-160 m carrier
     a 224 output is a 9x-upsampled 90 m field, i.e. §17.7's DiVAE objection.
     Reinstated once (a) terrain is computed at 30 m ourselves and (b) it was
     noticed S1/S2 at 10 m were on disk all along.  §30's criterion was never
     resolution, it was whether sub-token detail is MEASURED or INVENTED.  C=17.

  3. MERIT BECOMES VALIDATOR/CALIBRATOR, NOT PRODUCT.
     upa inside the tile  -> does this tile contain a channel? (the risk criterion)
     upa at the station   -> how wide the window must be
     ours vs MERIT's      -> per-station boundary-capture PASS/FAIL, ground truth
     hnd ~ 0              -> calibrates our stream threshold instead of guessing
     TWI/HAND from our own conditioned wide DEM at native 30 m.  10 km window =
     333^2 cells ~ 0.44 MB/station, ~440 MB for all 993.  Compute was NEVER the
     constraint — I said "multi-day build" early on and that conflated code effort
     with resources.  Corrected.

  4. LST IS NOW A COMPONENT, not merely the §29 fallback.
     Side branch off S — NOT off h_k, which would need Block 3 run at all 196
     locations and destroy the reason training is cheap.  S already exists.
     Linear(768->1) over 196 vectors -> (14,14) predicted anomaly.
     TARGET = Landsat ST per token MINUS THE TILE MEAN.  Subtracting the mean is
     load-bearing: absolute LST is season and time-of-day, readable off ERA5, and
     removing it kills exactly the Simpson's-paradox artefact §29 found.
     Nothing downstream reads it; it only makes gradient.
     Plus the time-averaged anomaly map as a Block-4 INPUT channel regardless —
     the cheap, risk-free half.

MEASURED THIS SESSION

  * LOWEST CELL LIES ON THE TILE BOUNDARY IN 25 OF 30 SAMPLED TILES = 83.3%.
    Median relief 188 m (13.5-797 m).  Flow leaves the 2.24 km window, so neither
    TWI nor HAND is computable from the current tile.  Not approximately — the
    quantities are defined by structure outside the box.
    Refinement that makes modest windows viable: the non-local error is largely
    COMMON-MODE within a tile (two cells on one hillslope both miss the same
    upstream inflow, so their DIFFERENCE survives).  It breaks specifically at
    CHANNEL cells.  Hence: a tile is at risk IFF it contains a channel, readable
    from MERIT upa inside the tile.

  * THE U-NET HAS NO HIGH-RESOLUTION SKIP PATH AT ALL.  Verified in source:
    _get_skip_connections returns anchor_l3/l6/l9 (model.py:530), all 14x14,
    F.interpolate'd up at model.py:258,261,264.  TerraMind is a ViT and never
    downsamples, so L3/L6/L9/L12 are ALL at 14x14.  A CNN U-Net's power comes from
    skips at 224/112/56; ours has none.  This is an architectural reason to drop
    the decoder INDEPENDENT of the supervision argument.

  * Landsat TIRS is 100 m.  §29.9's "5776 px vs 1" is the GRID, not the
    information — ~22x22 ~ 500 independent values per tile.  Still ~500x better
    than 1, but the honest number is 500.

THEORY — added, and it was missing entirely before

  TOPMODEL (Beven & Kirkby 1979): R*a = T0 exp(-fz) tan beta  =>
  z = (1/f)[ln(T0/R) - TWI].  DEPTH TO WATER TABLE IS LINEAR IN -TWI.  That is
  the entire theoretical content — TWI is a water-table-depth index, not a soil
  moisture index.
  HAND (Renno 2008, Nobre 2011) approximates the same thing more directly and is
  numerically more robust (a height difference; TWI's `a` is very sensitive to the
  flow algorithm).
  Capillary rise ~0.1-0.3 m sand, 1-2 m silt, 3-10 m clay — above a few metres
  HAND the top 10 cm is hydraulically DECOUPLED.  TxSON has 46-88 m relief.

  GRAYSON ET AL. (1997) + Tarrawarra (Western, Grayson & Bloschl): terrain control
  of SURFACE moisture is STATE-DEPENDENT.  Wet -> lateral flow organises the
  pattern, terrain explains much.  Dry -> soil and vegetation dominate, terrain
  explains ~nothing.

  ==> THIS IS WHY BLOCK 3 IS THE RIGHT ARCHITECTURE, and it is a stronger argument
      than anything in §30.  A state-dependent effect requires terrain INTERACTED
      WITH WETNESS STATE.  Cross-attention provides that; a uniform FiLM
      scale/shift structurally cannot express it.  It also means a STATIC terrain
      input is the wrong functional form — it would fit the average of two regimes
      and match neither.

  Consequence for the gate: the sufficiency test MUST be split wet/dry.  A pooled
  regression would average the two regimes into nothing.

NEXT, IN ORDER — NOTHING STARTED
  0. branch feat/per-location-processor from tag pre-s30-architecture (61c1773)
  1. download_merit_hydro_gee.py — needed in EVERY scenario, so start first.
     Template is download_soil_openlandmap.py (read_patch :171, process_station
     :207), NOT download_smap_gee.py — that samples POINTS via reduceRegions and
     has no retry, no resume, no checkpointing.  ~8-16 workers, NOT Pool(64):
     remote API, it will throttle.  (Deliberate departure from the standing
     Pool(64) rule, which is for local CPU scans.)
  2. PLOT THE upa DISTRIBUTION across 993 stations — the per-station go/no-go for
     self-computing terrain at all.  Report how many are tractable at 5/10/50 km.
  3. wide-DEM fetch at native 30 m.  CONFIRM FABDEM ACCESS FIRST — GLO-30 is a
     DSM, canopy dams streams, and this is the largest correctness risk in the
     whole derivation.  Fallback is aggressive breaching over filling.
  4. build_twi_hand.py + build_fine_stack.py.  None of pyflwdir / richdem /
     whitebox / pysheds is installed in either env.
  5. SUFFICIENCY GATE — 62 colocated pairs, dSM on dTWI/dHAND under station fixed
     effects, SPLIT WET/DRY.  Runs on MERIT 90 m in PARALLEL with 3-4.  The pair
     list was computed in-session in §26 and never persisted — write
     csvs/colocated_pairs.csv this time.
  6. dataset.py -> model.py -> regression gates -> smoke (20 stn, 3 ep) -> full
     run ~42 GPU-h.

RISK, STATED BEFORE ANY GPU
  TWI and HAND are saturation-excess concepts; our labels are the top 10 cm.  Same
  shape as §29, which tested evaporative cooling and measured -0.077.  No amount of
  DEM engineering addresses this, which is why the gate runs on 90 m data in
  parallel with the 30 m build rather than after it.
  BUT A TERRAIN FAILURE DOES NOT KILL THE PROJECT.  Ablation row 2 — Block 3 on,
  terrain off — isolates the architecture from the hydrology, because the
  per-location S2/S1 history columns do not depend on terrain at all.  Unlike §29,
  a negative result prunes ONE INPUT.

  Second risk, on the LST auxiliary: if the target is a fixed map per station and
  the model can identify the station — §24.12 found identity dominates at depth —
  it can MEMORISE 993 maps rather than learn structure.  Likely, not hypothetical.
  Test in the first smoke run: LST-head skill on validation vs training stations.
  Third: the 18.2% no-retrieval region CONTAINS THE WETTEST STATION, so the
  missingness correlates with the target.

  1. GEE IS DEAD.  The stored refresh token returns
     "invalid_grant: Token has been expired or revoked".  Every MERIT route is

  2. GLO-30 IS DIRECTLY READABLE FROM AWS AS A PUBLIC COG.

     no auth, no request signing.  The 993-job openEO/CDSE manager in

     download_dem_cdse.py is left untouched for the existing 10 m tiles.

  3. FABDEM ACCESS CONFIRMED — §31.4's "confirm FABDEM access first" DISCHARGED.
     zip+https://data.bris.ac.uk/datasets/s5hqmjcdj8yo2ibzi9b4ew3sn/
     N50E000-N60E010_FABDEM_V1-2.zip!N52E006_FABDEM_V1-2.tif opens in 0.4 s, a
     10 km window reads in 0.6 s, Accept-Ranges: bytes, HTTP 206, no auth.
     49 zips ~39 GB would mirror every station at 50 km half-width.
     NOT adopted — kept as the mitigation if the OSM stream check fails.

  4. TERRAMIND PRETRAINED ON GLO-30, AND GLO-30 IS A DSM.
     TerraMesh's DEM modality is Copernicus WorldDEM-30 ("produced using
     Copernicus WorldDEM-30 (c) DLR e.V."); the shipped tokenizer config carries
     domain="dem@264", data_path="./data/TerraMesh/train" at
     terratorch/.../terramind/tokenizer/tokenizer_register.py:240.
     => The DEM CHANNEL MUST STAY GLO-30 (no pretraining distribution shift).
     => But that says NOTHING about routing water through it.  The wide DEM is an
        INTERMEDIATE, not an input — nothing downstream sees those elevations,
        only TWI and HAND — so swapping only the derivation step to FABDEM costs
        no consistency.  This split is the useful result.

FOUR THINGS MEASURED

  - GLOBAL 30 m ROUTING SIZED AND REJECTED.  166e9 cells, 0.66 TB for the DEM
    array, ~4.6 TB working set against ~1 TB on a fat node; and conditioning is
    non-local so it cannot be naively tiled.  The download would be fine; the
    routing is not.  MERIT Hydro IS this problem solved at 90 m.

  - REGIONS BEAT PER-STATION WINDOWS.  Single-linkage clustering of the 993:
      25 km  -> 537 regions, 0.34e9 cells, largest  155 km
      50 km  -> 353 regions, 0.84e9 cells, largest  559 km   <- CHOSEN
     100 km  -> 202 regions, 2.76e9 cells, largest 1112 km
    50 km: every region ~10 GB working or less, Pool(16) on one ordinary node.
    Needs per-region Lambert Azimuthal Equal Area at 30 m — a UTM zone is only
    ~500 km wide and cannot carry a 559 km region.

  - COLOCATED PAIRS RECOMPUTED (§26's list was never persisted).  KD-tree on
    ECEF, 1120 m cutoff: 75 pairs across 84 distinct stations —
    15 at 0-50 m, 6 at 50-160 m, 25 at 160-500 m, 29 at 500-1120 m.
    §31.8 records 62 (13/4/16/29), so §26 applied a further filter, most likely
    overlapping observation periods.  RECONCILE when csvs/colocated_pairs.csv is
    finally written.
    ==> THE REASON THE SEQUENCE CHANGED: 21 of 75 pairs are under 160 m apart,
        i.e. under TWO MERIT cells, so dTWI is ~0 for them by construction, and
        the 25 at 160-500 m span 2-5 cells.  §31.8's gate on MERIT 90 m was
        really only powered on the 29 pairs beyond 500 m.  Our own 30 m terrain
        restores ~50 usable pairs.  A false negative there would have killed a
        valid arm of the architecture.

  - GLO-30 GRID TRAPS.  The N52 tile is 2400x3600 at (0.000417 lon, 0.000278
    lat) — longitude is DECIMATED POLEWARD, 1.5 arcsec in x above 50 deg, again
    at 60/70/80.  Cells are not square in metres anywhere and 56 STATIONS ARE
    ABOVE 60 N.  Flow routing and Horn slope assume square cells.  Also a 10 km
    window straddles 1-degree tile boundaries often — direct COG reads must
    mosaic up to four neighbours.

  - NO ROUTING LIBRARY IS INSTALLED, re-verified.  pyflwdir 0.5.12 needs numba,
    which exists ONLY in `terramind` (0.67.0; soilmoisture has none).  So the
    download scripts run in `soilmoisture`, build_twi_hand.py in `terramind`.

THE ONE DECISION THE USER MADE
  MERIT IS A NECESSARY CONDITION, not a deferred cross-check.  No station's
  TWI/HAND may be used by anything — not the sufficiency gate, not dataset.py —
  until its accumulation has been checked against MERIT `upa` per station.
  Failures are masked or fall back to MERIT, never silently used.  This makes
  the GEE re-auth blocking.

  TWO CORRECTIONS TO §32 AS FIRST WRITTEN, both from the probe:

  1. upa IS IN km2, NOT m2.  §32.6 originally said m2.  Measured, not assumed:
     upa/upg matches the 3-arcsec cell area in km2 to four decimals across
     30-52 N (0.005292 vs 0.005283 at 52 N; 0.007389 vs 0.007402 at 30 N), and
     the ratio TRACKING CELL AREA WITH LATITUDE is what makes it decisive.
     upg is the pixel count.  This is §32.4's cells-vs-area trap landing on the
     REFERENCE side — a factor of 1e6 inside a log, not 900.  download_merit
     now fetches upg alongside upa so the assertion is reproducible offline.

  2. THE GATE MUST NOT POINT-SAMPLE MERIT.  Four probe points placed on named
     large rivers all returned upg of 1-8, i.e. hillslope cells — the
     coordinates missed the channels by a couple of hundred metres.  At 90 m,
     two cells off a river drops upa by ORDERS OF MAGNITUDE.  Some ISMN stations
     sit near channels, so a point comparison would manufacture spurious
     failures out of georeferencing error.  §32.6 rewritten to compare over the
     2.24 km footprint instead: spatial correlation of ln(upa) plus magnitude at
     matched quantiles, with snap-to-nearest-stream and a reported snap distance
     where a station-level number is wanted.

HEADLINE
  Everything §32 asked for got built and validated.  Then the science gate said the
  input is not worth having, so it is pruned — which is exactly the outcome §31.10
  and §32.8 pre-committed to handling.  The architecture is untouched: ablation row
  2 (Block 3 on, terrain off) always isolated them.

WHAT RAN
  step 1  pyflwdir 0.5.12 + whitebox 2.3.6 into terramind (WBT binary v2.4.0)
  step 2  download_merit_hydro_gee.py     993/993 stations, 0 failures, ~5 min
  step 3  build_dem_regions.py            353 regions at 50 km, reproducing §32.3
                                          exactly; largest 420x562 km; 0.98e9 cells
          download_wide_dem.py            353/353 in 2 MIN 10 S, 0 failures, 2.0 GB
          download_wide_dem.py --source fabdem
                                          353/353 in 5 MIN 19 S, 0 failures
  step 4  test_terrain_tier1.py           ALL TIER-1 CHECKS PASS
          build_twi_hand.py               353/353 regions (17 min bulk + 54 min big)
                                          TIER 2 CLEAN: 0 mass-conservation failures,
                                          0 HAND<0, 0 a<res, 1 region with 175
                                          residual pits out of 18.7e6 cells
  step 7  csvs/colocated_pairs.csv        75 pairs / 84 stations (15/6/25/29)
  step 8  gate_sm_vs_terrain.py           THE GATE — see below

  THE RESULT THAT VINDICATES §32.3's REGION DESIGN:
    0 of 990 stations have a catchment touching its region edge.  The 50 km linkage
    plus 10 km buffer holds everywhere.

SIX CORRECTIONS TO §32 AS WRITTEN (all in runbook §32.9)
  1. MERIT's grid is CENTRE-registered.  crs_transform origin
     (-180.000416666667, 84.999583333333), so snapping a window to multiples of
     1/1200 deg — the obvious move — lands half a cell out.  46.6 m systematic
     transform error; snapping to the asset's own edges gives 0.34 m.
     upa in km2 confirmed across ALL 993 (median ratio 0.99974).
  2. §32.5's CONE ROW IS WRONG and would have failed correct code.  On a cone
     a = r/2 exactly (measured 18.3 cells), not "~1 cell".
  3. §32.5's PIT ROW IS VACUOUS through pyflwdir — from_dem fills internally.
  4. §32.4's HAND DEFINITION CONTRADICTS ITSELF: elevtn=dem_raw AND HAND>=0 cannot
     both hold, because breaching carves (65 m and 152 m measured; raw HAND hit
     -31.5 m and -84.9 m).  HAND now measured on the CONDITIONED surface.  Slope
     still from the RAW DEM.
  5. BREACH_DIST_CELLS 100 -> 20.  Cost ~O(dist^2) per pit.
  6. §32.5's TIER 3 CANNOT PASS AS SPECIFIED — see below.

TIER 3: TWO D8 IMPLEMENTATIONS DO NOT AGREE, AND THAT IS A PROPERTY OF D8
  Over 343 regions, pyflwdir vs WhiteboxTools D8: r(ln a) 0.774 median, 0.652 on
  hillslopes, and WORSE on channels (0.42; 0.16 above 100 ha) with stream networks
  overlapping only 45%.  Three hypotheses tested and killed:
    - conditioning flats — agreement IMPROVES with conditioning burden (r = +0.46),
      the opposite of what I claimed earlier in the session
    - orientation error — as-is 0.55, every flip/transpose ~0.000
    - pointer mis-decoding — irrelevant to a raster-vs-raster comparison
  Cause: D8 makes a discrete choice among 8 neighbours, so sub-metre elevation
  differences flip directions and accumulated areas diverge downstream.  On a tilted
  plane with 0.4 m roughness the two libraries correlate at 0.55 while their row
  means agree to 1%.  Both are correct; the test asks D8 for something it does not
  determine.

TIER 3b — STABILITY, THE TEST THAT REPLACED IT
  Perturb the DEM, redo the whole derivation, measure what moves.
      sigma      TWI    HAND   a(MFD)   a(D8)   pooled r(dTWI)   pooled r(dHAND)
      0.05 m   0.940   0.997    0.908   0.839            0.519             1.000
      0.2  m   0.836   0.991    0.744   0.608            0.195             0.987
      1.0  m   0.542   0.965    0.421   0.316            0.190             0.963
  dTWI IS UNUSABLE AS A GATE REGRESSOR: at 0.2 m the perturbation noise is 82% of
  the signal spread (1.53 vs 1.86), which attenuates any regression slope toward
  zero and manufactures a false negative.  dHAND is 2.6% (0.63 vs 25 m).
  Mechanism: `a` is a chaotic upslope INTEGRAL (near-tie routing decisions that
  compound downstream, and genuine near-discontinuity in space); HAND is a LOCAL
  elevation difference, so error enters once and stays bounded.  Then dTWI
  subtracts two knife-edge values while the true difference shrinks for nearby
  stations — noise up, signal down, simultaneously.

  FABDEM DOES NOT FIX IT.  Fetched all 353 regions (5 min 19 s, uniform 3600x3600 at
  every latitude, explicit nodata, no decimation seams — strictly better behaved than
  GLO-30).  But dTWI goes 0.195 -> 0.221, i.e. no change, and D8 gets WORSE
  (0.608 -> 0.382).  Reason: the instability is not canopy ERROR, it is the routing
  decision being a NEAR-TIE on smooth hillslopes, and removing canopy makes the
  surface smoother, so the choice is more marginal.  Canopy roughness was helping D8
  break ties.  FABDEM is kept anyway — correct routing surface, no seams, and the
  right basis for Tier 4's OSM check.

  ENSEMBLING DOES FIX THE FIELD.  Averaging over DEM-error realisations (not over
  space, so 30 m per-cell resolution is preserved) takes the TWI field from
  r = 0.75-0.84 to 0.96-0.98 between independent ensembles, losing only ~11% of
  spatial variance, and yields a free per-cell uncertainty map.  Not pursued further
  once the gate failed.

  NO GLOBAL 30 m TWI EXISTS OPEN-SOURCE — checked, not assumed: Geomorpho90m CTI
  92.8 m, MERIT Hydro 92.8 m, HydroSHEDS 92.8 m, ALOS CHILI 90 m, mTPI 270 m.  And
  90 m is already excluded by §32.1(c) since 21 of 75 pairs are under 160 m apart.
  A third-party 30 m TWI would inherit the same instability anyway.

§32.8 THE SUFFICIENCY GATE — TERRAIN FAILS  (runbook §32.10)
  890 stations with terrain and >= 180 observed days.  49 of 75 pairs survive the
  >= 120 common-observed-day rule — WHICH RECONCILES 75-vs-62: §26's extra filter was
  overlapping observation periods.
      dSM(0-10) ~ dHAND      r = -0.102  p = 0.480  n = 49   95% CI [-0.373, +0.184]
      dSM(0-10) ~ dTWI       r = +0.049  p = 0.737
      wet third ~ dHAND      r = -0.104      dry third ~ dHAND   r = -0.101
      Koppen C -0.112 (n=36)          Koppen D -0.137 (n=10)
  TWO THINGS MAKE IT A FAIL RATHER THAN AN UNDERPOWERED NULL:
    - the most diagnostic prediction is ABSENT.  Saturation excess is a wet-state
      mechanism and §31.5 built the wet/dry split to test exactly that.  Wet -0.104
      vs dry -0.101: no contrast at all.
    - it WEAKENS when restricted to pairs that have terrain contrast:
      -0.102 (all) -> -0.103 (>=2 m) -> -0.065 (>=5 m) -> +0.002 (>=10 m).
      A real effect strengthens under that restriction.
  AND THE RESPONSE IS CLEAN: paired stations differ by a median |dSM| of
  0.047 m3/m3, same sign on 95.8% of days.  Regressor has range, response is
  well determined, they are unrelated.
  HOBE did not generalise: its n=5 look gave -0.637 with the right sign, stronger
  when wet and stronger at 10-30 cm; Koppen D over 10 pairs gives -0.137.

§32.11 THE DYNAMICS HYPOTHESIS — TESTED AND CLOSED
  Alternative: HAND controls DYNAMICS not level.  Over the same 49 pairs:
      d tau vs dHAND    -0.173 (all) -> -0.255 (>=2 m) -> -0.398, p=0.046 (>=5 m)
      d memory          -0.234, p = 0.10
  STRENGTHENING with contrast where the level test evaporated, both with the
  predicted sign.  Pre-registered checking, criteria fixed BEFORE running:
    SPLIT-HALF PASS — halves give -0.388 and -0.378, and tau is a real station
      property: tau(h1) vs tau(h2) r = +0.663, p < 0.001, n = 97 station-halves.
      That retired the "tau is sensor noise" worry (its IQR is only 2.9-3.6 d).
    PERMUTATION FAIL — best |r| 0.426 vs a null 95th percentile of 0.527 over the
      same 6x3 grid, 10000 shuffles.  Corrected p = 0.196.
  So the limit was POWER, and network fixed effects supply it — pairing was only
  ever buying control of climate/protocol/sensor, which networks also buy, over 890
  stations instead of 49 pairs.  ONE pre-specified regression, no grid:
      tau(0-10) ~ HAND, within-network demeaned
      r = +0.0881, p = 0.0099, n = 877, 25 networks.   FAIL — WRONG SIGN, r^2 = 0.8%
  The 49-pair -0.398 reverses to +0.088 at n=877.  Textbook small-sample fluke; the
  pre-registration is what caught it.  Secondaries all null.

TOOLING TRAPS WORTH NOT REDISCOVERING (runbook §32.9.3)
  - GLO-30 ocean tiles 404 rather than returning zeros, and carry nodata=None, so
    sea level 0.0 is indistinguishable from no data BY VALUE.
  - WHITEBOXTOOLS' PYTHON WRAPPER CANNOT REPORT FAILURE: run_tool() returns 0 even
    on a Rust panic, discards stdout when verbose=False (where the panic text goes),
    and chdir()s the whole process.  terrain_ops.run_wbt() calls the binary directly.
  - FillDepressions panics intermittently (~1 call in 6) — now conditional on a
    measured pit count, with a bounded retry underneath.
  - WhiteboxTools rejects a GeoTIFF with no geokeys.
  - rasterio's Window.round_lengths() rounds to NEAREST, not up.
  - Killing a foreground python does NOT kill its whitebox subprocesses.
  - A whitebox call hung for a full hour on one node (tcn1176) holding 228 MB, then
    ran in 1.2 s on another.  Node flake; run_wbt now times out and retries.

DECISION
  Terrain is pruned.  §32.7 step 9 proceeds with terrain OFF: build_fine_stack.py
  drops terrain_lo/terrain_hi, Block 1's zero-init terrain path is not built, and
  the §32.6 MERIT gate is demoted to a validation artefact since nothing downstream
  consumes accumulation.
  STILL UNTESTED, honestly: DEPTH.  Only 4 of 49 pairs carry 10-30 cm and 3 carry
  30-100 cm.  If terrain reaches soil moisture it is below the surface layer, and
  our labels are overwhelmingly 0-10 cm.  Data limitation, not a result.

THE FINDING THAT OUTLIVES THE TERRAIN ARM
  Stations ~400 m apart differ by a median 0.047 m3/m3 with the same sign on 95.8%
  of days.  A large, reproducible, per-location signal that FiLM's uniform context
  vector provably cannot represent, and precisely what Block 3 exists to capture.
  It is real.  It is simply not terrain.

NEXT

     -> §31.8's regression gates -> smoke (20 stations, 3 epochs) -> ~42 GPU-h.
  2. Ablation row 2 (Block 3 on, terrain off) is now the ONLY terrain row needed.
  3. Latent, fix regardless: dataset.py:483-486 returns all-zero anchors with
