2026-08-24  SESSION 30 — §33 DESIGNED: SENTINEL-1 IN THE DECODER.
            No code, no GPU.  Everything below was measured, not assumed.
================================================================================

THE QUESTION SESSION 29 LEFT
  §32.10/§32.11 closed terrain, but the thing terrain was hired to explain is still
  there: stations ~400 m apart differ by a median 0.047 m3/m3 with the same sign on
  95.8% of days.  So: what varies at 400 m, changes with weather, and is measured
  everywhere?  This session answers "Sentinel-1", and specifies how.

THE MECHANISM, RESTATED SO IT IS ACTIONABLE
  TerraMind is a ViT: it patches ONCE and never downsamples, so L3/L6/L9/L12 are
  ALL 14x14 (model.py:530).  model.py:257-264 interpolates them up.  BETWEEN 160 m
  AND 20 m THE DECODER HOLDS NO MEASUREMENT AT ALL, and §23 measured what fills the
  vacuum: up to 0.19 m3/m3 of confident structure, worst at the FLATTEST site.
  §16.1 already localised the collapse to the decoder, not the encoder.

MEASUREMENTS MADE THIS SESSION (all new)
  S1 ACQUISITION COUNTS — §0.8 listed these as NEVER MEASURED.
    s1_asc 993 stations median 212 / s1_desc 910 median 238 / asc+desc median 440,
    p10 144, only 9 stations below 50.  Raw sigma0 VV+VH dB, (N,2,224,224) fp16, on
    PERMANENT storage at /projects/prjs1968/satellite_zarr/{station}.zarr/s1_{asc,desc}/

  TWO-WAY DECOMPOSITION OF sigma0.  d = T - r_p - c_k + mu, per station x relative
    orbit x polarisation.  Variance splits EXACTLY (verified to 2e-07 dB^2):
      CA-Cbo     n=116   season 19.6%  static 63.7%  INTERACTION 16.7%
      TxSON CR1000-1     season 37.7%  static 50.1%  INTERACTION 12.2%   <- low end
      SCAN Abrams        season 40.7%  static 21.7%  INTERACTION 37.7%   <- high end

  THE VARIANCE SHARE IS NOT THE EVIDENCE.  Double-centring PURE NOISE leaves 94-99%
    in the interaction term.  The evidence is that the pattern REPEATS BETWEEN
    PASSES: lag-1 ac +0.240 / +0.371 / +0.478 against location-shuffled -0.006 /
    -0.011 / -0.002.  Report the autocorrelation, never the share alone.

  MULTILOOK IN LINEAR POWER, NEVER IN dB.  Averaging dB gives the geometric mean of
    power.  Measured bias 0.53 dB mean, 7.5 dB max, r=0.952 — worst in heterogeneous
    cells, exactly where the signal is.  Pooling in dB also INFLATES the interaction
    share (20.2% vs 18.3%), so linear is both correct and more conservative.

  RELATIVE ORBIT = ord(date) mod 12, and the grouping validates itself:
      split-half r(c_k) WITHIN a group (the ceiling)  0.9979 0.9981 0.9982
      BETWEEN groups  0v1 0.9632  0v7 0.9552  <- different orbits, keep apart
                      1v7 0.9952  <- AT ceiling: S1A/S1B same orbit, MERGE
    Mixing orbits inflates the interaction from ~17% to 18.3% — geometry leaking in.

  c_k SAMPLE SIZE.  RMSE against the full-record c_k, as % of the c_k spread:
      n=5 30.1%   n=10 22.0%   n=20 14.5%   n=30 11.7%   n=50 7.6%   n=80 4.3%
    n>=50 comfortable, n>=20 floor.  Medians 212/238, so nearly every station passes.
    Seasonal balance 22-28% per season everywhere checked, so c_k is a genuine
    climatology and not summer-weighted.

  VEGETATION IS PRESENT BUT IS NOT WHAT MAKES IT COHERENT.  Cross-ratio VH-VV is the
    standard C-band vegetation descriptor (Vreugdenhil 2018; Copernicus SSM).
      r(d_VV, d_CR) = -0.423 / -0.579 / -0.404  -> veg explains 16-34%
      ACF decays to ~0 by 6 passes (~72 d, e-folding 24-36 d)  <- SM timescale
      per-location annual harmonic explains only 9.8-22.3%     <- 78-90% EPISODIC
      REMOVING VEG MAKES THE SIGNAL STRONGER:  lag-1 ac 0.240->0.328,
                                               0.371->0.428,  0.478->0.546
    If vegetation caused the coherence, stripping it would destroy it.  It sharpens
    it.  So the build carries d* = d_VV - beta*d_CR, beta accepted ONLY IF the
    residual's autocorrelation RISES; else shrink to zero.

  SIGNAL SURVIVES TO FINE SCALES.  Signal fraction = ac1(scale)/ac1(160 m):
      160 m 1.00 | 80 m 0.62-0.92 | 40 m 0.42-0.78 | 20 m 0.31-0.64 | 10 m 0.25-0.57
    ENL of the delivered RTC product measured at ~8-10 on the 10 m grid — it is
    already multilooked, which is why 20 m is not hopeless.

  COLOCATED PAIRS, split-consistent (all 126 share location_group_id AND split):
      <1.12 km  75 pairs   train 19  val 34  oos 22
      <2.24 km 126 pairs   train 32  val 45  oos 49
      <9.00 km 533 total, 393 same-split, train 174
    AND THE CORRECTION THAT MATTERS: of the 21 pairs inside one 160 m token, 15 are
    closer than one 20 m output cell and CANNOT test sub-token skill.  Only 6 remain
    — 2 train, 4 oos, ZERO val.  Those 15 do measure the irreducible
    REPRESENTATIVENESS FLOOR: two probes in one output cell, which no 20 m model can
    beat.  Worth reporting as a number.

  CLIMATE STRATIFICATION: humid (A+C+D) 764 stations, arid (B) 223.  Held-out humid
    245 (75 val + 170 oos).  TxSON's 40 stations are all Cfa.

  TERRAIN STABILITY JSONs read (csvs/terrain_stability_probe{,_fabdem}.json).
    HAND IS THE STABLE ONE: r >= 0.96 under 1 m DEM noise, dHAND across pairs
    0.97-1.0000.  TWI IS NOT: dTWI goes 0.339 -> -0.163 -> -0.106 at 0.05/0.2/1.0 m.
    FABDEM IS WORSE THAN GLO-30 (region 31 dHAND 0.9999/0.9982/0.9796 vs
    0.9161/0.4399/0.0976), so §32's pre-committed FABDEM swap is NOT indicated.
    Job 25995800 itself FAILED — BreachDepressionsLeastCost timeout at 3600 s on
    region 10; the JSONs came from a later run.
    NONE OF THIS RESCUES TERRAIN.  Stable is not the same as useful: §32.10/§32.11
    closed HAND on both level and dynamics.

THE DESIGN (§33)
  S1 goes IN, LST comes OUT.  Disjoint roles, so no leakage discipline is needed.
    sigma0(p,k) = c_k + (r_p - mu) + d(p,k)
                  ^permanent   ^tile-wide     ^this spot vs its own norm
                   texture      wetness
  Feed all three per decoder stage — 3 channels, lossless (they sum back to raw).
  The point of factoring: c_k CANNOT be derived from one sample, it needs the
  archive.  Raw sigma0 would force memorising 993 per-pixel means that do not
  transfer out of sample.  w_p = catchment wetness state must be fed back explicitly
  because double-centring deletes it, and it is what lets a conv express
  response x wetness rather than a flat offset.

    stage      grid      cell    channels
    bottleneck 14x14    160 m    c_14, d_14, w
    up1        28x28     80 m    c_28, d_28, w
    up2        56x56     40 m    c_56, d_56, w
    up3       112x112    20 m    c_112, d_112, w
  Each in_channels += 3 (~12k params), new weight slices ZERO-INIT so the forward
  pass stays bit-identical to best.pt and §26's provenance gate applies unchanged.
  up4 dropped: most expensive conv in the decoder and the only stage with no
  measured input.  Output 112x112 @ 20 m (2240/20=112, 112/14=8 exact).
  LST as TARGET at 101.8 m (22x22, the finest grid dividing the tile that stays
  coarser than TIRS 100 m).  Head on up1 -> F.interpolate(mode='area') to 22x22, so
  BOTH sides of the loss are aggregations and nothing is invented on either.

ROUTES CONSIDERED AND REJECTED (each for a stated reason)
  - Differentiable water balance (architecture_state_model.tex).  REJECTED by user:
    too many physical parameters, too attackable.  Its own §10.2/§10.5 concede
    equifinality and the wrong-physics failure mode.
  - Second transformer / per-location processor.  REJECTED: more parameters means
    more data, and the spatial dimension is where the data is thin.  Also
    unnecessary — the 196 spatial tokens ALREADY attend over all 365 ERA5 tokens
    (full bidirectional attention, model.py:407-421); spatial_ctx is computed at
    model.py:732 and then DISCARDED.
  - S1 as a dense TARGET with hidden-pass masking.  REJECTED: redundant with
    s1_pyr on the input side, and the entire masking discipline exists only because
    a quantity was on both sides.
  - Deleting the U-Net.  REJECTED: §16.4 already showed the decoder reproduces
    structure almost perfectly GIVEN dense input (norm-std 0.0061->0.2504, r=1.000).
    The problem was never the decoder, it was having nothing at fine scale.
  - Colocated-pair loss.  REJECTED: 19 train pairs is too few to supervise with, and
    keeping pairs OUT of the objective makes them an uncontaminated metric — any
    rise in between-station SD is then attributable to S1 alone.

CORRECTIONS TO CLAIMS MADE EARLIER IN THIS SESSION
  - "§23's effective resolution ~160 m is a ceiling" — WRONG.  64-83% of variance
    retained at 14x14 describes what the BROKEN U-Net produced.  It is a failure
    description, not a bound.  Do not quote it as one.
  - "Zero-init gives bit-identity" — true only for ADDITIVE stems.  Deleting the
    U-Net changes the output path and forfeits it.  The final design keeps the
    decoder, so bit-identity is restored.
  - "21 pairs give a clean sub-token test" — 15 of them are closer than one output
    cell.  The real number is 6, with none in val.
  - "TWI is protected by MFD, HAND is the exposure" — the stability probe says the
    reverse.

REFERENCE CHECKED
  arXiv:2505.00265v1 (Yu, Filippi & Bishop, "Field-scale soil moisture from S1").
  Trains and validates ENTIRELY at point sensors (OzNet 0-5 cm), RMSE 0.06-0.08,
  r up to 0.64, NO dense spatial validation of any kind, and the final product
  resolution is never stated.  It renders a map AT fine resolution and never shows
  the map is CORRECT at fine resolution.  Useful lesson: their fine structure comes
  from a per-pixel WCM inversion, so every variation traces to a measurement.  Ours
  today comes from nowhere.  Fine structure is defensible only where a measurement
  exists at that scale.

    logic and basics for computing c / d / w and where they concatenate.  NOTE the
    preamble uses fontspec, so it needs lualatex or xelatex, NOT pdflatex; and
    fancyvrb environments must be defined with \DefineVerbatimEnvironment, not
    \newenvironment, or the compile dies with "File ended while scanning use of

## 2026-08-25 — S1 decomposition and LST target: design session, nothing built

No code run beyond small numerical verifications.  §33.9 gate 1 is still unrun and is
still the only thing that can kill the design.  All of the below is written up as
§33.12 in the runbook and derived in full in text/s1processing.md.

WHAT WAS SETTLED

  Multilook / dB.  Nothing to change on the download side.  MPC sentinel-1-rtc comes
    from IW GRDH and is ALREADY multilooked 5x1 — ENL 4.4, ~20 m resolution posted on
    a 10 m grid, so per-pixel speckle is ~2.2 dB, not the 5.6 dB of single-look.  At
    TerraMind's 16x16 patch the effective look count is ~280 and residual speckle
    ~0.26 dB.  Storing dB costs ~-0.5 dB of log-averaging bias, which is static per
    station-orbit and therefore absorbed.  DO NOT re-download, DO NOT pre-average.
    §33.5(a)'s rule stands: never average dB, only linear power.

  Channels: 3 -> 7, and beta is out of the build.  c_VV, d_VV, d_CR, w_VV, w_CR, age,
    valid.  The vegetation correction d* = d_VV - beta*d_CR was a coefficient fitted
    per station x group x polarisation — ~4000 offline numbers, the same class of
    object the water-balance design was rejected for.  Feed d_CR and w_CR instead and
    let the conv weight them: one shared trained weight, not thousands of fitted ones.
    Beta survives as the DIAGNOSTIC that earned the design, not as code.
    Zero-init widens to weight[:, -7:].

  d_CR is free.  Double-centring is linear, so d_CR = d_VH - d_VV exactly.  Verified
    at all four levels, max error 0.  c_VH and mu_VH must still be stored to build
    d_VH, but c_VH is not fed.
  w_CR is NOT free.  Standardisation is affine with per-channel constants, so
    w_CR != w_VH - w_VV.  Difference the raw rho first, standardise second.

  w comes from the full-tile linear mean, not the row mean of T_l.  The row mean
    averages logs of block means so it drifts with l (Jensen), yet one w is broadcast
    to four stages.  Keep the row mean inside the centring — that is what makes the
    variance split exact — and build w from rho = 10log10(mean P over 224x224).

  LST aggregation was ragged.  F.interpolate(28->22, area) has ratio 1.27, so output
    cells span 2 OR 3 source cells and 20 of 28 source cells feed more than one
    output.  Overlapping blur, not a partition — "nothing is invented on either side"
    did not hold and the 484 residuals were correlated.  Fix: move the head to up3,
    crop 112->110, avg_pool2d(5,5) -> 22x22 at EXACTLY 100.0 m, disjoint, 25 cells
    each.  110 = 5*22.  Costs a 20 m ring at the tile edge.
    Target side: Landsat ST is only distributed at 30 m (TIRS acquires at 100 m; there
    is no 100 m download), so warp to the same grid with Resampling.average.

  LST loss.  Normalise d_LST by DIVISION ONLY — it is already double-centred so its
    mean is exactly zero, and subtracting again breaks gate 4's identities.  One
    global sigma, train split only; per-station sigma would amplify retrieval noise
    at low-contrast stations.  Centre the prediction over the VALID cells so the loss
    is invariant to an offset the target does not contain.  Zero-init the head's last
    1x1 so the gradient into the trunk is zero at step 0.  Pick lambda by matching
    GRADIENT norms into up3, not loss values — SM residuals are ~0.03 in m3/m3 so
    L_sm ~ 5e-4 against L_lst ~ 0.5, and value-matching would give lambda ~ 1e-3 and
    silently disable the term.  ~6% of samples carry an LST gradient (8-day revisit,
    half lost to cloud), so guard the empty-mask case and log the count.
    112 is even — there is no centre cell; the station falls between 55 and 56.

  The clean split: LST supervises the PATTERN (dense, 100 m, zero-sum, relative), SM
    supervises the LEVEL (one point, 20 m, absolute).  They do not compete for the
    same degree of freedom.

CORRECTION TO A CLAIM MADE EARLIER IN THIS SESSION

  "w carries 19.6-40.7% vegetation seasonality" — WRONG.  Those §33.5 season% figures
    are a variance partition and sum to 100 (Var(r), Var(c), Var(d) as shares of
    Var(T)).  They say how much of tile variance the pass-level term carries, not how
    much of that term is canopy.  Vegetation contamination of w is therefore PLAUSIBLE
    BUT UNMEASURED, unlike d where 16-34% is measured directly against d_CR.  w_CR
    rests on an argument, not a number.  Say so wherever it is discussed.

NEW GATE ADDED (§33.9 #6)

  d_LST must pass the same coherence test as d.  Double-centring pure noise leaves
    94-99% in the interaction term — that applies to the TARGET as much as the input.
    Landsat ST retrieval noise is ~1-2 K and d_LST may not be much larger.  Run the
    lag-1 ACF of d_LST between consecutive passes against a location-shuffled control
    BEFORE using it as a target, or risk training the decoder to fit 484 noise cells
    per sample.

OPEN, NOT RESOLVED — c_k AND TEMPORAL LOOK-AHEAD

  Raised at sign-off.  c_k for an evaluation station is computed from that station's
  S1 archive, spanning years the model never trained on.
    - It is NOT label leakage.  c_k is built from S1, the label is an in-situ probe,
      and splits are by station while references are per station, so nothing crosses
      the split.
    - It IS temporal look-ahead within a station: the input at t depends on
      acquisitions after t.  The arguments that it is mild (c_k estimates a
      time-invariant quantity; carries no information about the target date; the
      self-inclusion effect is 1/N) are arguments, not measurements.
    - The chronological split-half diagnostic settles it EMPIRICALLY: if c_k is
      temporally stable then causal and full-archive c_k must agree, and the
      convention stops mattering.  Run both and report the gap.
    - Provisional: full-archive c_k is fine for a held-out-station evaluation if the
      convention is stated; anything operational needs an expanding-window causal c_k.
    - REVISIT BEFORE PUBLISHING ANY NUMBER.

REFERENCE DISCIPLINE AGREED

  1. Global constants from the TRAIN SPLIT ONLY.  SIGMA_LST, and audit
     csvs/era5_stats.json — if it was built over all 993 stations that contamination
     is already in the baseline.
  2. Per-station references stay per-station.  True by construction; assert it in code.
  3. Freeze and version the references, or d for a given date depends on when the job
     ran and reported numbers stop being reproducible.
  4. Log n per group.  Self-inclusion bias is 1/N, worst exactly at the n>=20 floor.
  5. Chronological split-half of c_k as a new diagnostic — same code as the +-6 orbit
     merge, split at the record midpoint instead of at random.  Flag and exclude
     stations that fall below ceiling; do NOT auto-detect change points.
  6. Balance the averaging set rather than shortening it.  Merged S1A/S1B groups are
     ~2x denser before Dec 2021, so c_k is implicitly weighted to the pre-2021 scene.
     Weight to equalise per-year contribution.  Rolling window REJECTED — it would
     absorb genuine multi-year drying into the reference and delete it from d.

  No coordinates in the model, confirmed.  Checked dataset.py and model.py: no lat,
    lon or elevation scalar anywhere; ERA5_VARS is 19 pure meteorological channels.
    Recorded in §33.8 as a deliberate non-decision — splits are BY STATION, so
    coordinates would let the model interpolate a spatial climatology between training
    stations, inflating val and collapsing on the 219 oos stations.
    Caveat worth keeping straight: this does NOT mean the model cannot identify a
    station.  dem_pyr (4,768), lulc_pyr (4,768) and soil_patch (21,74,74) are
    per-station constants and are almost certainly a unique fingerprint across 993
    stations.  §33.4's argument never rested on the absence of an identifier — it rests
    on memorised c_k costing a 993-entry table AND being unable to transfer to a
    fingerprint never seen.

2026-08-25  SESSION 31 — §34 SPECIFIED: PATCHWISE TEMPORAL TRANSFORMER.
            §33.13 (MAX_AGE measured, not chosen).  open_items.md opened.

WHERE THE SESSION STARTED

  §33 fully designed, nothing built, gate 1 unrun.  Session opened on the seven
  decoder channels and ended on a defect one level upstream: the model has almost
  no per-patch information AT 160 m either, never mind below it.

§33.13 WRITTEN — MAX_AGE IS MEASURED, NOT CHOSEN

  `valid` is defined entirely by MAX_AGE (s1processing.md §9.9) and no document ever
  assigns it a number.  Do not fix that by taste.

  Do NOT bin samples by naturally-varying lag.  Long dt arises when passes are
  missing, and missingness is not random in time — merged groups run ~6-day revisit
  before Dec 2021 and ~12-day after, so dt correlates with era and the estimated
  decay would be part staleness and part 2021.

  CONSTRUCT the lag instead.  A label date has a LADDER of prior passes.  For
  k = 1..4 build d from the k-th most recent pass and run the same gate-1 regression
  at each, on IDENTICAL samples.  Stations, seasons, land cover and revisit era are
  held constant by construction; only age varies.  MAX_AGE = where the partial
  contribution over the persistence control stops being distinguishable from zero.
  Fit on TRAIN stations only and freeze — sweeping the cutoff then reporting the
  skill it maximises is selection on the evaluation set.

  Prior bound in hand: d's lag-1 ACF is 0.240 / 0.371 / 0.478 (0.328 / 0.428 / 0.546
  vegetation-corrected), so one to two revisits, not a month.  A bound, not the
  answer — the ACF measures how fast d changes, the lag curve how fast its RELEVANCE
  decays.

  THE FLAT CURVE IS A FAILURE SIGNAL, NOT A LICENCE.  If skill at k=1 is
  indistinguishable from k=4, d is a quasi-static map — residual texture that
  survived the centring — not a dynamic anomaly.  That is the §29 LST failure mode
  exactly.  Gate 1 must treat it as disqualifying.

CORRECTION I MADE TO MY OWN EARLIER CLAIM THIS SESSION

  I cited §29's within-station r = -0.077 as though it settled LST generally.  It
  does not.  That negative is scoped to DAYTIME INSTANTANEOUS Landsat.  §29.13 and
  §30.6 both say so: Phase B (ECOSTRESS night LST, diurnal range) is untouched and
  is the only arm that could still recover a direct LST<->SM link.  Thermal inertia
  is UNTESTED, not refuted.

  Also had Contextformer's ordering backwards — vision backbone runs FIRST, then the
  per-patch temporal stage.  Corrected in §34.2.

THERMAL TARGET — THE RANKING, AND WHY d_LST AND NOT LST

  Absolute LST normalised globally FAILS: a single global mean is a constant and the
  tile is ~30 K warmer in July than January, so season survives, ERA5 predicts it,
  and the decoder never has to produce spatial structure at all.  Normalising is not
  centring.

  Ranking: DTR > daytime d_LST > per-scene-centred LST > absolute LST.

  Per-scene-centred still contains the static map, which the decoder can reproduce
  from DEM/LULC/soil — so most of that loss is satisfiable WITHOUT S1.  d_LST has no
  static component by construction, so the only inputs that can produce it are
  time-varying and spatial, i.e. S1 d.  d_LST is what FORCES the model to use S1.

  Static vs dynamic is NOT either/or.  T = r_cell + c_pass + d, so use two loss terms
  with separate weights: lam_static on the static map (dense, EVERY sample, breaks the
  0.0065 collapse) and lam_dyn on d_LST (rare ~6%, gated by gate 6).  Strictly better
  than the current all-or-nothing wiring.  New gate proposed before adopting the
  static term: delta r_cell vs delta(mean SM) on colocated_pairs.csv — the SAME
  sufficiency gate that killed terrain.  Thermal texture only helps if it is MOISTURE
  texture.

ECOSTRESS / DTR — USER RAISED IT, IT IS THE UNTESTED ARM

  Landsat C2 L2 ST is daytime-only and can never give a diurnal range (§29.2).
  ECOSTRESS gives day and night.  DTR = LST_day - LST_night is the thermal-inertia
  proxy: wet soil high inertia, small swing.  It also cancels the static surface
  terms PHYSICALLY rather than statistically, and night LST has no slope/aspect
  illumination and no transpiration confound.

  SNR is much better: DTR runs 10-25 K against ~3 K paired retrieval noise, versus
  d_LST's small residual fighting 1-2 K directly.

  Costs: starts 2018-07 (zero of 2016-17), LP DAAC via earthaccess not MPC,
  downloader never built.  THE BINDING CONSTRAINT IS PAIRING — ISS precession means
  day and night passes are usually not on the same date, so paired clear day/night is
  much rarer than either alone.  The CMR census (metadata only, no credentials,
  minutes) must report PAIRED counts per station per year, not granule counts.  It is
  the go/no-go and it is still unrun (§29.3 ran only the Landsat half).

  Grid: with the SM head at up3, avg_pool2d(7,7) -> 16x16 @ EXACTLY 140 m (112 = 7x16,
  no crop), ECOSTRESS 70 m -> 2x2 -> 140 m.  Both sides partition exactly and the 2x2
  halves retrieval noise.  70 m is a GRID not a resolution — native footprint is
  ~38 x 69 m at nadir and grows off-nadir.
  TWO-CHANNEL HEAD (day, night) rather than one DTR channel, so unpaired passes still
  supervise.  Costs one output channel, recovers most of the density.

  Scale honesty: a 140 m cell is ~2 ha and straddles fields in fragmented landscapes.
  Mixing only ATTENUATES, never manufactures — so a gate 6 PASS at 140 m is strong,
  a FAIL is ambiguous between "no signal" and "wrong scale".  Stratify by parcel-size
  regime before believing a negative.

DECISIONS TAKEN

  S2 IS NOT USED (raw 10 m bands).  Therefore up4 has no measured time-varying input:
  the only 10 m sources left are static WorldCover and S1 gridded at 10 m (one look
  per cell, ENL ~4.4, ~2.7 dB speckle).  This independently RE-DERIVES §33.6's reason
  for dropping up4.  TerraMind cannot fill it — all four skips are 14x14, so skip_L3
  at 224 is a 16x interpolation carrying nothing new.  SM head at up3, 112x112 @ 20 m.

  FiLM STAYS for now.  Traced it: `context` (model.py:743) reaches the decoder ONLY
  through film_s9/s6/s3 (model.py:253-255).  Deleting FiLM would sever the direct path
  for the whole non-spatial branch.  It is a shortcut not the sole path — the 196
  spatial tokens already attended to those tokens — so "FiLM vs w" is a legitimate
  ablation, deferred until §33 has a verdict.

§34 SPECIFIED — THE DEFECT IS THE POOLING

  _cpu_pyramid_pool collapses the patch axis before the transformer sees anything:
  S2 (60,196,768) -> (60,4,768), DEM (196,768) -> (4,768), etc.  §27a.2 measured what
  survives: 1.5% of within-tile variance for DEM, 2.6% for LULC, 2-3% for satellite
  history.  Destruction, not compression.

  Consequence: after pooling the ONLY per-patch information anywhere in the model is
  the anchor — ONE acquisition.  A patch knows what it looked like on one date and
  knows the TILE's history.  It does not know its own.

  The cost is already being paid: the worker loads the full (60,196,768) tensor and
  THEN pools it away.  Un-pooling adds no disk I/O.

  Why pooling was there: 196 x 100 = 19,600 tokens in one global sequence vs ~990
  today, quadratic, ~380x.  RUNNING PATCHWISE REMOVES THE CONSTRAINT — the patch axis
  moves into the batch dimension, attention is over T only:
      (B,196,T,768) -> (B*196,T,768) -> (B,196,768)
  196 x 100^2 ~= 2.0 M pairs, and the global stage SHRINKS to ~580 tokens.  Total
  ~2.4x, not 380x.  Contextformer reports 16x memory saving from the same move.

  Statics go in as a PREFIX to each patch's sequence, not appended to the summary —
  as a prefix the temporal attention can condition drydown on cover and terrain
  ("this patch dries fast because it is sandy"); appended after, that interaction is
  unexpressible.  Soil stays tile-level: SoilGrids is 250 m native (~9x9 real cells
  per tile) and is itself predicted from covariates including DEM and land cover, so
  per-patch soil is largely redundant with the prefix.

  Residual formulation: spatial_tokens = anchor_l12 + delta, delta zero-init.  Step 0
  is BIT-IDENTICAL to the current model, §26's provenance gate holds, and zeroing
  delta at test time is a clean ablation of exactly this stage.  Same trick as
  Contextformer's "predict deviations from the last cloud-free observation" — our
  anchor IS that observation.

  THREE STEPS, EACH WITH ITS OWN GATE.

  STEP 1 — NO DECODER.  Token head on the 196 outputs, supervise at the station's
    token, output 14x14 @ 160 m.  It is a REGRESSION: one shared function maps
    (patch history, patch statics) -> SM, applied at all 196 positions; weight sharing
    is why supervising one token teaches every token.  Nothing upsampled, nothing
    invented, output resolution = input resolution — and §23 already measured 64-83%
    of the current 224^2 map's variance as sitting on the 14x14 grid anyway.
    VALIDATABLE: colocated pairs are 405-936 m = 2.5-6 cells.  Sub-token is not — 15
    of 21 pairs are closer than one 20 m cell, 6 usable, ZERO in val.
    Answers the premise everything else rests on: does per-patch history carry
    within-tile SM information at all?  §27b.8 measured 0-4.2% with POOLED history.
    Must beat §28.8's four numbers.  §28.5's position leakage is mandatory here —
    the supervised token is always index 105.

  STEP 2 — decoder, 160 m -> 20 m.  Only if step 1 passes AND gate 1 passes.  Above
    160 m everything is settled by step 1; below it the skips give nothing (all four
    are 14x14 stretched).  So the SEVEN S1 CHANNELS ARE THE ONLY INFORMATION doing the
    160->20 m work — which makes "zero the seven channels" a sharp ablation.
    Honesty condition: at 160 m the prediction is checkable, at 20 m it is not.

  STEP 3 — thermal, after gate 6 and the sufficiency gate.

CODE FINDINGS THIS SESSION

  model.py:267   conv4 takes NO skip — pure upsample+conv.  Literally §33.6's "the
                 only stage with no measured input".
  model.py:741   the masked mean EXCLUDES the 196 spatial tokens.  bottleneck is not
                 pooled; `context` is a separate summary of the complement.
  model.py:530   anchor_l3/l6/l9 already BYPASS the transformer as skips.  Only l12
                 takes the long route.  Adding l12 as a fourth skip is trivial.

     the only thing that can kill §33.
  2. §34 STEP 1 — patchwise temporal transformer + token head at 160 m, no decoder.
     Independent of gate 1; tests the premise §34 step 2 and §33 both rest on.
  3. ECOSTRESS CMR census — PAIRED clear day/night per station per year.  Metadata
     only, minutes, and it is the go/no-go for the whole DTR arm.
  4. Chronological split-half of c_k, to close the look-ahead question with a number.
  5. build_s1_interaction.py, then the thermal pull and gates 6 / sufficiency.


2026-08-26  SESSION 32 — PHASE 0 EXECUTED, STAGE 2a BUILT, THEN MADE
            PATCHWISE-ONLY.  Two GPU jobs, both smoke.  Nothing trained yet.
================================================================================

WHERE THE SESSION STARTED

  §34 specified, nothing built.  Opened on "where are we and how do we get rid of
  the Perceiver", ended with a patchwise-only codebase pushed for review.

PHASE 0 — THE TRAINING STORE WAS CORRUPT, AND THE SILENCE WAS THE BUG

  Age-based scratch purge (NOT quota — usage was 8.8% of 8 TiB) deleted .zarray
  headers everywhere and, for the small arrays, the chunks too.  Every large token
  array was META-ONLY, so the ~730 GB of L3/L6/L9/L12 chunks survived: headers were
  written once in June and never re-read, while chunk files had atime refreshed by
  training.

  zarr.open_consolidated then returned fill_value with NO exception — soil read as
  all zeros, and a run started that day would have trained on it and reported a
  plausible number.  Merge-restored (rsync -a, no --delete), 993/993 verified,
  backup chmod -R a-w.  slurm/train.sh now refuses a damaged store.

  Three bugs, all the same shape — SILENT SUCCESS.  The worst:
  RSYNC_OPTS=(-a --chmod=u+rwX) as a bash ARRAY.  Bash cannot export arrays and
  copy_one runs inside bash -c under xargs, so rsync ran with no flags at all.  The
  job looked healthy while doing nothing.

THE PERCEIVER IS DROPPED, AND WHAT REPLACED IT

  §G2/§G3's three resamplers (427 driver tokens -> 32 latents) were never coded and
  will not be.  The 59x that justified them is a RATIO; the absolute cost is
  ~0.05 s per tile-day, so it does not bind.  "Three separate resamplers for clean
  ablation" was false — masking a modality's inputs already gives exact ablation and
  dataset.py already does it on ~50% of samples.  And deferring to "stage 2b" was
  never free: a resampler is a different model, so 2b meant a full retrain.

  Replacement: the drivers become a READ-ONLY cross-attended memory.  Two
  transformers, not one.

    T1  weather encoder   431 tokens, 2 layers, runs ONCE per tile-day
    T2  patch decoder     105 tokens, 6 layers, runs K times

  Every patch reads the same weather; the weather never reads the patch.  Because
  the drivers are read-only, Kc/Vc are computed once per sample and reused — exact,
  not an approximation.

  THE COST ARGUMENT IS NOT THE REASON, and saying so cost two corrections.  Per
  layer the cost is 12.n.d^2 + 2.n^2.d and the LINEAR term is ~90% at n=537; the
  crossover where attention dominates is n = 6d = 4608 tokens, and we are at 537.
  So §34.7 and open_items §G2, which both counted attention pairs, are wrong by a
  large factor — and §35.15's own "12 min vs 3 min" was 2x low (MACs read as FLOPs).
  The 4.4x saving is mostly the FFN not running on 431 driver tokens 196 times.

  What survives scrutiny is an OPTIMISATION argument, not an expressiveness one.
  Neither design contains the other: concat has the (431,106) block, memory has
  independent softmax budgets and separate projections.  What differs is what the
  optimiser is asked to do — patch-specific share of the readout is 102/537 = 19%
  under concat against 102/105 = 97% under memory.  If concat returns a null,
  "un-pooling does not help" and "the optimiser never dug the signal out of an 81%
  constant background" are indistinguishable.  That confound is why memory is the
  default.  concat stays as a flag and is NOT run: under concat the driver tokens
  are updated by each patch from layer 1, so there is no cache to retrofit later.

  text/patchwise_math.md (NEW) — the whole derivation from first principles, every
  dimension written out, a worked numeric attention example at d=2, a numeric proof
  that one layer of concat destroys the cacheable redundancy, and a two-transformer
  flowchart.  Read this before touching the encoder.

REGISTER STANDARDISATION DROPPED, AND THE REASON IS A MEASUREMENT I HAD WRONG

  §35.7 called it "not optional".  Checking csvs/ says otherwise.  Two quantities
  had been conflated: the SINK (one token position dominating — DEM 13.0, LULC 9.1,
  s2/l12 7.2, S1 1.2) and the REGISTER (a few DIMENSIONS dominating — s2/l12 0.940,
  s1 0.911, dem 0.884).  The sink is a DEM/LULC/S2-L12 phenomenon and was already
  audited clear; the register is broad.

  But the number the whole argument rested on — post-LayerNorm cosine 0.783 -> 0.157
  — lives in csvs/register_dim_variance.json, which contains ONLY dem and lulc.  It
  is the DEM p14 row.  Post-LN collapse has never been measured for s2 or s1, and
  nobody has measured the quantity that actually decides it: cosine between the ~100
  history tokens of the same patch ACROSS TIME.

  Deferring is safe here in a way the driver-mode decision was not: §35.10 already
  mandates temporal attention entropy logging, which measures whether attention
  ACTUALLY collapsed rather than whether the keys look aligned, and a retrofit is
  only forced in a world where the run was already invalid.  Entropy logging is now
  load-bearing, not a nicety.

BUILT, SMOKED, THEN REBUILT PATCHWISE-ONLY

  Stage 2a first landed behind --arch patchwise with the unet path intact (job
  26070202: unet unmoved at 50,050,944 params, patchwise memory 75,526,208, concat
  61,342,784, all three clean on 2 GPUs).  Then, by decision, the live modules were
  stripped to ONE architecture and the baseline frozen as model_unet.py /
  dataset_unet.py / train_unet.py / ckpt_utils_unet.py.

  The justification is the session's own evidence: every silent-failure bug found
  while building 2a was the other path's assumption leaking.  ckpt_utils' strict=
  False turned a patchwise checkpoint into a randomly-initialised U-Net that ran and
  printed plausible numbers.  ablation.py's `if k in d` made --ablate sat a TOTAL
  NO-OP while still reporting a healthy donor fraction — which would have made every
  §35.9 arm return "no effect".  Two plot scripts assumed a 224x224 map.

  model.py 1210 -> 649 lines.  dataset.py 1135 -> 1023.  Deleted: UNetDecoder,
  FiLMLayer, _cpu_pyramid_pool, select_anchor_zarr, all four anchors, the .npy
  memmap machinery, total_variation_loss, STATION_ROW/COL, --arch, --use-memmap,
  --lambda-tv, --lambda-boundary.

  The loss got SIMPLER, not more complex.  sm_map[:, :, 112, 112] existed only
  because the U-Net emitted a map; the model emits (B, K, n_depths) where the value
  IS the prediction and the dataset already selected patch 105.  No index, no
  gather — an earlier draft of the plan said "gather over token_idx" and that was
  wrong.

  L12 ONLY.  L3/L6/L9 served the U-Net skips alone, so convert_l369_to_npy.py's
  ~7,800 .npy files are no longer needed for training.  That also caught a latent
  crash: train.py's _advise_l369_willneed still read ds._l369_cache after the cache
  was deleted, and would have raised at epoch 1.

  FiLM AND THE PATCH CLS ARE GONE — the depth heads predict directly.
    was   out_i = head_i(FiLM1d_i(h, depth_ctx[:, i, :]))
    now   out_i = head_i(depth_ctx[:, i, :])
  FiLM earned its place in the U-Net by broadcasting one context vector across a
  (B,C,H,W) map; here both operands are (N,768) vectors from the same transformer.
  It cost 3 x 1.18 M parameters and, identity-initialised, started all three depths
  reading the IDENTICAL vector — a soft echo of the §18.4 star residual the design
  had explicitly rejected.  105 tokens, 71,981,888 params.

THE MEASURED WIN — THE EPOCH IS NO LONGER DATALOADER-BOUND

  token_sel was consulted at exactly ONE place, after a full (196,768) slab had been
  read and 195/196 of it discarded.  The loader now reads tokens_z[i, sel, :] —
  1.5 KB, one memmap page, against 294 KB and ~72 pages.  Same smoke, before/after:

    before   data=4s   compute=1s     gpu_util=27%
    after    data=0s   compute=1-2s   gpu_util=93-95%

  Caveat: 3 stations and 20 batches does not extrapolate to 993.  But two things
  follow.  concat's 4.4x compute is now a REAL cost, where before it was nearly
  free.  And soil_patch is now the dominant payload — 460 KB of 635 KB, against
  ~153 KB for everything satellite.

VERIFICATION (job 26071036, ALL PASSED)

  Unit tests clean; the frozen snapshot still loads cls_depth_star_reg (epoch 16,
  50,348,544 params, decoder present); no unet remnants; both driver modes smoke on
  2 GPUs with no DDP unused-parameter error; no pooled or anchor keys emitted.

  Three of my own bugs were caught by the FIRST verification run, all silent-shaped:
  CONFIG["token_sel"] left at None after the arch-fixup block was removed (crashed
  both smokes); a stale comment tripping the remnant grep; and the dataset test
  still asserting the old _finalise_history contract.  Two more were caught in
  self-review: collect_entropy armed before validation and never disarmed (would
  have killed SDPA for all subsequent TRAINING), and the smoke script's rm using a
  relative path so a rerun would have silently RESUMED instead of starting clean.

CHECKPOINTS HAVE NO BACKUP — VERIFIED, AND IT IS NOT WHAT IT LOOKS LIKE

  /projects/prjs1968/checkpoints and /gpfs/work3/0/prjs1968/checkpoints are THE SAME
  DIRECTORY (device 49, inode 1561052160) — exactly the trap the zarr store had.
  best.pt and last.pt being byte-identical is two files in one directory on one
  filesystem, not redundancy.  cls_depth_star_reg's 604 MB of unique weights exist
  exactly ONCE.  The tag baseline-unet-temporal preserves the CODE, not the weights.
  §35.13 flagged this in words; it is still open.

DECISIONS TAKEN, RECORDED SO THEY ARE NOT RELITIGATED

  * Nowcast, not forecast.  The window is [t-364, t] INCLUSIVE — the model estimates
    SM on day t from data up to and including day t.  Confirmed deliberate.
  * n_layers 6 for T2, 2 for T1 (75.5 M -> 71.9 M after FiLM).  N=6 is not a law:
    it is Vaswani's empirical choice, and ViT-Base is 12.  Sweep it later.
  * skt KEPT.  It is an input DRIVER; ECOSTRESS LST is a supervision TARGET
    (§34.6 step 3).  Different roles, and the replacement has not passed its gate
    (§29: within-station r = -0.077).  Stage-2c ablation, not a refactor casualty.
  * Fully bidirectional, no causal mask.  Correct for a nowcast from a fixed window,
    and it does not leak because the window is strictly backward-looking.  The one
    open exception is §33.12's c_k look-ahead, untouched by this work.

SESSION 33 — 2026-08-26 — Two-round audit of dataset.py / model.py / train.py
================================================================================
Branch feat/patchwise-temporal.  Rollback tag: pre-s33-audit-fixes.
Runbook section: §35.24.  Maths doc revised in place (now derived at L=105).

WHAT HAPPENED
  Audited the three files against text/patchwise_math.md — four agents (one per
  file plus interfaces), then a second external review.  ~30 defects found and
  fixed.  NOT ONE OF THEM WOULD HAVE CRASHED.  The design survived intact;
  the implementation did not.

THE THREE CLASSES
  1. Fail-open data paths.  Missing cloud mask -> all 60 S2 acquisitions "clear".
     Missing S1 token_mask -> border/shadow patches valid.  Missing QC variable ->
     gap-filled climatology trained as observed truth.  Missing DEM nodata mask ->
     an all-zero token read as terrain.  All-NaN soil channel -> NaN through
     SoilEncoder into Kc/Vc and the whole cross-attention.  All now fail closed
     AND counted and printed — the counting is the point, after §32.
  2. Diagnostics that could not see what they were built for.  The §35.20 entropy
     detector head-averaged before computing entropy, spanned the 5 prefix
     columns, compared against a fixed log(100) although the median station-year
     has ~36 of 60 S2 slots (so a COLLAPSED row scored 4.0 and passed), and was
     overwritten every forward — one batch, rank 0.  The depth-collapse metric had
     been reading a getattr default since the U-Net strip and logged NOTHING.
     Both rebuilt as epoch-accumulated all_reduce(SUM) with a per-sample
     log(n_valid) reference: 1.0 now means collapsed.  SUPERSEDES the "log 100 =
     4.605" instruction at the end of §35.23.
  3. Scale.  nn.Embedding defaults to normal_(0,1) against an era5_mlp output of
     sigma 0.22 — the driver token was ~98% calendar, 2% weather.  All seven
     tables -> trunc_normal_(0.02), which only works alongside input LayerNorms on
     the frozen TerraMind features (otherwise the same problem moves to history).

HARDEST TO FIND
  ERA5 was the only modality taking staleness from its SLOT INDEX rather than its
  real date, in a window that load_era5_rolling COMPACTS.  Plus a year-granular
  admission guard: a station whose ERA5 ended 2023-03-15 still admitted an August
  target and put the March row at slot 364 as "today's weather".  circular_doy_pe
  carried the true DOY all along, so the two clocks disagreed and nothing said so.

ARCHITECTURE — THE FINDING THAT MATTERS
  Training K=1, inference K=196, and the 431 driver tokens are tile-constant by
  construction.  So the loss is fully minimised by a function that IGNORES
  dem_k/lulc_k/hist_k, and at K=196 that emits 196 identical values.
  §35.9's arms were always the test.  What was missing: TWO ORTHOGONAL COLLAPSE
  MODES, one detector.
      temporal collapse   -> entropy ratio -> 1.0
      spatial invariance  -> map SD -> 0
  §35.20 says map SD does not catch attention collapse — true, and the converse is
  equally true and was never written down.  Neither substitutes for the other.
  Both now run once per val epoch, plus a per-patch-vs-driver gradient-norm ratio.
  Also: the K=196 path had NEVER been executed end to end, and at inference the
  model is fed patches out of its training distribution (water, urban, swath edge).

TESTS
  The old suite set every validity tensor all-True and era5_doys never 0, so no
  test batch contained a single padded token: deleting a ~ anywhere in the masking
  chain passed everything, and so did permuting depth_heads.  Both chains were in
  fact correct; nothing defended them.  Rewritten as pytest, ~85 functions.

BLOCKING DISCOVERY
  /gpfs/work3/0/prjs1968/data/{cat}/{station}/ERA5Land NO LONGER EXISTS — 0 of 842
  sm_only station dirs have it, and /projects/prjs1968/data is the same inode.
  compute_era5_stats.py would have silently skipped every station.  Both stats
  scripts now read era5/values from the token zarr, which also fixes the old
  nc_files[0] partial read at the root.

STATE
  NOTHING WAS EXECUTED — every fix made by reading, per the no-login-node rule.
  All five modules parse.  "Carefully reviewed, unverified."

NEXT, IN THIS ORDER (driver_stats.json is now a hard fail-closed dependency)
  1. sbatch slurm/run_tests.sh        # must be green first
  2. sbatch slurm/driver_stats.sh     # writes csvs/era5_stats.json + driver_stats.json
  3. sbatch slurm/train.sh --driver-mode memory --driver-layers 2 --n-layers 6 \
       --run-name patchwise_2a
  4. Review the diff by eye at the two places a mistake is silent: model.py
     _forward_patchwise's kv/(D,P) view, and dataset.py _read_patch_tokens.
  5. Still deferred: back up cls_depth_star_reg (604 MB, exists once); per-patch
     soil (460 KB -> 16 KB); multi-station supervision via location_group_id.

OPEN DECISIONS LEFT TO THE USER
  - CM_MAX_BAD_FRAC = 0.01 over a 16x16 block admits at most TWO bad pixels
    (3 px = 1.17% and fails).  Left as zero-tolerance, now documented.
  - era5_require_full_window defaults False: the trailing-edge test is
    unconditional (that was the bug), but requiring full coverage would delete the
    first year of every station.  Count prints either way.
  - CLAUDE.md is untracked (.gitignore line 24 is *.md) — needs git add -f to be
    versioned or rolled back.

SESSION 33 addendum — §35.25 token scale MEASURED (2026-08-26)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  §35.24 added input LayerNorms on the frozen L12 features on an UNMEASURED
  argument.  Measured it (measure_token_scale.py, slurm/token_scale.sh, 120
  stations, ~25 s).  csvs/token_scale.json.

  modality  per-elem std  (no reg)   tag share  reg share  tile mag  mag-noreg
  s2               4.652     3.172       0.43%      79.0%     33.7%       2.7%
  s1_asc           4.210     2.765       0.48%      55.7%      1.2%       2.6%
  s1_desc          4.250     2.808       0.47%      52.8%      1.3%       2.7%
  dem              4.417     3.165       0.45%      83.4%     56.6%       2.9%
  lulc             3.998     3.600       0.50%      67.7%     34.0%       2.4%

  VERDICT: KEEP the LayerNorms.  The worry was that LN deletes token magnitude,
  which looked like a third of S2's within-tile variance.  Stripping the six
  register dims takes S2 33.7% -> 2.7% and DEM 56.6% -> 2.9%.  That magnitude
  variation was register, not content.  LN costs ~2.7% of within-tile content
  and gains ~4.6x on annotation visibility (0.43% -> 2.00%).  No code change.

  BUT: §35.24 claimed the tag would be worth 0.04%.  It is 0.43% — ten times
  better.  Quiet, not invisible.  The LayerNorm survives on a thinner margin
  than was argued for it.

  agree = 100% in every modality: all 120 stations share the same top-1 register
  coordinate.  §35.3 inferred this; now measured on current tokens.

  §27a.4's LayerNorm-compression worry is CLOSED for patchwise: it was about the
  POOLED path (pooled vector inherits sink/n_tokens).  Patchwise does not pool.
  Measured sigma inflation is only 1.47x.

  Says nothing about register standardisation (§35.20) — still deferred, still
  the entropy detector's job.

SESSION 33 addendum — §35.26 one scale cannot serve two streams (2026-08-26)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  SUPERSEDES PART OF §35.24.  That section set all seven nn.Embedding tables to
  trunc_normal_(0.02).  Half right, and the wrong half caused everything after it.

  THE BUG §35.24 HALF-FIXED
                        content std   annotation std   share
    drivers (era5_mlp)         0.22             1.0    ~450%  <- genuinely broken
    history (TerraMind)        4.65             1.0     ~21%  <- entirely fine

  rel_pos_emb is ONE SHARED TABLE annotating both streams, whose content differs
  in magnitude by ~21x.  One std cannot serve both.  Setting it to 0.02 fixed the
  drivers and dropped history staleness 21% -> 0.43%.  The input LayerNorms were
  then added to get history back to 2%, and a log|token| feature after that to
  hand back the magnitude the LayerNorm deleted.  Three changes, two undoing the
  first.

  FIX THE CAUSE: two tables.
    rel_pos_emb       drivers   EMB_INIT_STD      = 0.02   era5, sif, twsa
    rel_pos_emb_hist  history   HIST_EMB_INIT_STD = 1.0    s2, s1
    static/hist_modality_emb -> history scale; the rest -> driver scale
  280 K params, ~0.4%.  Input LayerNorm then unnecessary, scale feature gone,
  parity with the frozen pooled baseline restored.

  THE MEASUREMENT THAT DECIDED IT (added to measure_token_scale.py AFTER §35.25
  was committed, so it is recorded here and nowhere else):

    modality   tile mag  mag-noreg   TIME mag   noreg
    s2            29.4%       2.7%       9.6%    9.3%
    s1_asc         1.2%       2.6%       0.9%    2.5%
    s1_desc        1.4%       2.8%       0.9%    2.4%
    dem           56.6%       2.9%        n/a     n/a
    lulc          34.0%       2.4%        n/a     n/a

  SPATIALLY magnitude collapses to 2.7% once the 6 register dims are stripped —
  LayerNorm would delete sink, a gain.  TEMPORALLY it does NOT collapse
  (9.6 -> 9.3%).  That is real content on the axis where wetness lives.  So the
  norm comes out: use_input_norm flag, DEFAULT OFF, nn.Identity when off.
  Revisit when a decoder exists — a decoder broadcasts a token over a 16x16
  block, which is where the 97%-register spatial magnitude becomes artefacts.

  driver_norm NOW UNCONDITIONAL.  §35.24 tied it to the same conditional as
  driver_enc, so the two --driver-mode arms differed in contextualisation (the
  hypothesis) AND normalisation (an accident).  driver_enc stays memory-only
  (§4.3 holds).  Note what does NOT bite: the softmax budget is safe either way,
  because norm_self is per-token and normalises all 536 before Q/K/V.  The
  asymmetry was in the residual stream, not the attention.

  label_count now emitted by compute_driver_stats.py, so depth_weights is fixed
  before the first gradient step — epoch 1 no longer trains under uniform
  weights, i.e. under a different objective from every epoch after it.

  DRIVER ROW ORDER WAS DOCUMENTED WRONG.  Code appends soil FIRST:
      soil [0,4)  era5 [4,369)  sif [369,419)  twsa [419,431)
  Both model.py and patchwise_math.md §2.3 said era5-first.  Nothing crashes
  (mem_pad is built in the same order), but §35.9's ablation arms are next, and
  an arm masking "the ERA5 block" as m[:, 0:365] would ablate soil + era5[0:361]
  and report a number.  Fixed in both places, with offsets.

  TESTS: 117 passed / 1 skipped.  The one first-run failure was the right kind —
  test_concat_does_not_build_T1 asserted driver_norm is nn.Identity in concat,
  which is exactly what this section reverses; assertion updated, not the code.

  STILL UNVERIFIED — synthetic tensors only.  dataset.py's fail-closed paths, the
  day-granular ERA5 guard, /dev/shm preload, DDP, warmup-on-resume and every
  §35.24 diagnostic have NEVER run against real data.
      run_tests.sh -> driver_stats.sh -> train.sh --max-stations -> anything long

  DECLINED, with reasons (external critique):
    - Moran's I companion to patch_map_sd: user's call, spatial structure is
      taken to live in the TerraMind embedding.
    - soil_ch_valid emission: dead channels land on the corpus MEAN after
      z-scoring (imputation, not fabrication), >2 dead drops the station, and
      the DEM/LULC treatment would discard ~19 healthy channels to flag 1-2.
    - satellite-history dropout: 0.0 is deliberate at K=1, and cloud masking
      already leaves a median ~36 of 60 S2 slots valid.

SESSION 33 addendum — §35.27 the audit fix that cost 62% of the split (2026-08-26)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Ran slurm/driver_stats.sh for the first time.  219 / 587 stations contributing;
  362 dropped on `labels-qc-length-mismatch` — §35.24 audit item 9, which made a
  qc/sm length mismatch FATAL on the grounds that the old trailing-slice
  realignment was an unverifiable guess.  The audit note said the path was dead
  for current stores.  It fired for 62% of the train split.

  THE TRAILING SLICE WAS CORRECT, AND IT IS VERIFIABLE.
    station                     sm    qc  dates  diff
    Banizoumbou               1095  1825   1095   730
    COSMOS-UK_Sheepdrove      2557  3287   2557   730
    HOBE_1.07                 1144  1612   1144   468
  dates matches sm everywhere; only qc is long.  station_splits.csv closes it:
  Banizoumbou actual_start 2014-01-01, start 2016-01-01, end 2018-12-30 = 1825
  untrimmed / 1095 trimmed, diff 730.  trim_pre2016.py trims sm+dates from the
  FRONT and leaves qc alone.

  FIX: split the two directions.
    qc LONGER  -> front-trim, recoverable, now VERIFIED by requiring labels/dates
                  to be a gapless daily span (that is what makes "same end, earlier
                  start" imply trailing alignment).
    qc SHORTER -> no alignment exists, always fatal.

  LESSON, about this session's own method: converting "unverified" straight to
  "fatal" without measuring how often it fires is the same error as arguing from
  unmeasured init scales (§35.25, §35.26).  Verify first, then set severity.
  It surfaced in 30 s only because §35.24 also required every fail-closed path to
  be COUNTED AND PRINTED.  The audit caught its own regression.

  STATS, after the fix:
                        before      after
    ERA5 stations      225/587    587/587
    driver stations    219/587    577/587
    station-days       365,956  1,183,871
    label n (0-10)     302,020  1,049,213
  Remaining 10 = no-sample-producing-year-in-2016-2022 (SNOTEL alpine, Berlin

  engineering task: PRE-REGISTER THE §35.10 GATE.  The runbook mandates setting it
  before the result is in (§28.8 is the cautionary tale), and its stated threshold
  "log 100 = 4.605 nats" no longer refers to anything the code emits.  There are
  now THREE detectors, not one:
      entropy ratio    temporal collapse    1.0 = uniform = collapsed
      patch_map_sd     spatial invariance   ~0  = constant map
      grad_ratio       is the per-patch pathway read at all
  Entropy cannot see spatial invariance and map SD cannot see temporal collapse.
  Neither substitutes for the other.

SESSION 33 — §35.29 tile-sharing stations out of training (2026-08-26)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Each station has its OWN 2240 m tile centred on it, so "shares a tile" = B falls
  inside A's tile.  Two cases, different consequences:
    SAME PATCH (<160 m)      both in ONE token -> contradictory supervision, and
                             their disagreement IS the irreducible 160 m noise
                             floor (nothing else in the data gives that number)
    DIFF PATCH, SAME TILE    the ONLY direct test of §34: predict A's tile, read
    (160-1120 m)             the patch containing B, compare to B's observation.
                             patch_map_sd shows the map VARIES; this shows it is
                             CORRECT.  26 pairs, mostly TxSON+FMI, low power.

  FINDING: location_group_id does NOT capture tile-sharing.
                          via group    global sweep
    same-patch                   17              26
    tile-pair                    25              44
    train affected                5              17
  Missed Lamont-CF1, SMOSMANIA (Pezenas, Prades-le-Lez), Carpeneto STEMS pair,
  TonziRanch/US-Ton, +4 TxSON.  §35.19's deferred multi-station-supervision item
  reasons from location_group_id and is therefore working from an undercount.

  NO LEAKAGE: every pair <1120 m checked for a split mismatch — none.  Checked,
  not assumed.

  APPLIED (update_splits_tile_pairs.py, slurm/update_splits.sh, --apply to write):
    train 612 -> 595 (17 -> oos, 2.8%);  oos 202 -> 218;  val 76;  duplicate 1
    new cols: same_patch_pair, tile_pair_eval, duplicate_of
    backup:   csvs/station_splits.csv.pre_s3529
    VairaRanch (FLUXNET-AMERIFLUX mirror) vs US-Var (AmeriFlux), 6.1 m = SAME
    SITE ingested twice -> split="duplicate" (inert to every split_filter, row
    kept).  TonziRanch/US-Ton same pattern at 50-160 m, not auto-marked; both
    held out as same-patch anyway.  50 m threshold deliberate: Lamont-CF2/US-ARM
    at 152 m are genuinely different instruments at ARM SGP.

  STATS REFITTED on the new split: 563/573 (was 577/587).  Label means moved
  0.1720 -> 0.1715 at 0-10 — robust.  Both hashes changed:
    era5   7d72c8f82bc7d2f9 -> 572028af6cd66199
    driver 0eb7ade028686033 -> 34ade6b95d913b52
  So §35.28's provenance check now correctly flags smoke/smoke2 checkpoints as

SESSION 34 — §35.31 the preload read 196 patches to use 1 (2026-08-26)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Job 26076503 launched as the §35.30 stage-2a run and spent its whole life
  loading.  Killed at 50 min, zero optimiser steps.  §35.6 pre-flight passed
  clean (993/993, no damage) so this was NOT the purged store again.

    [SHM] Preload done in 2733.5s     <- 45 min 34 s
    647 stations, 1892 .bin files, 153.6 GB in /dev/shm

  TWO INDEPENDENT DEFECTS, only one of them about speed:

  1. STAGED 196 PATCHES TO READ 1.  L12 is (dates,196,768); 196 = the 14x14
     patch grid.  Training is token_sel="station" -> patch 105 alone, K=1, and
     train.py REFUSES to train on anything else.  Preloader did zg[...][:] and
     wrote full width.  Of 153.6 GB staged, ~0.8 GB (0.51%) is ever read.
     Not a new idea: dataset.py's own fallback has narrowed since §35.24 item
     12 and its docstring said outright that the shm side had not.  The
     l12_narrowed / _narrowed_for plumbing existed, never got a True.
     Not free either: tmpfs is RESIDENT ram.  The old "a memmap costs no
     resident RAM until touched" comment is true of a file-backed memmap and
     FALSE of /dev/shm.  That was 153 GB of the node's 720 G.

  2. RAN ON ONE CORE.  2733 s / 1892 arrays = 1.4 s each, 56 MB/s aggregate,
     process averaging 10.6% CPU -> blocked on GPFS, not computing.  No
     decompression to blame: 91.3 MB on disk for 99.3 MB raw (ratio 1.09).
     One serial stream against a 64-core allocation.

  THE KEY CONSTRAINT (why A and B are not substitutes): chunking is
  (32,196,768), so the token axis is ONE chunk.  Zarr's unit of IO and of
  decompression is the chunk, so patch 105 CANNOT be read without decompressing
  all 196.  Narrowing therefore saves nothing on the read — it only changes what
  is KEPT.  153.6 GB still moves; 0.8 GB stays.  Only parallelism touches the
  46 minutes.

  CHANGED:
    A  _preload_one_station()  new Pool worker; reads [:,tsl,:], stamps
                               "narrowed":true in .meta.json, blosc nthreads=1
                               (64 workers x 64 blosc threads = oversubscription)
    B  _preload_l12_to_shm()   selection stays SERIAL (per-split cap semantics
                               are load-bearing), read+write over Pool(64),
                               progress line every 100 stations
    C  _load_l12_shm()         returns narrowed per key + a width REFUSAL check
    D  train_one_epoch()       step-level W&B under the existing log_every

  THE WIDTH CHECK IS LOAD-BEARING, not padding.  train.py builds the patch-map
  diagnostic as dict(common_kwargs) with only token_sel="all" overridden, so it
  INHERITS the same shm_dir and would have opened K=1 memmaps expecting 196.
  numpy clips an over-wide basic slice SILENTLY — no exception.  Failure mode
  would have been diag/patch_map_sd_mean quietly computed over one patch, and
  that is a pre-registered §35.30 gate condition.  Stale full-width bins carry
  no "narrowed" key, default False, and still read correctly.

  Also caught: `import wandb` lives inside main()'s `if is_main` block, so a
  bare wandb.log() in train_one_epoch is a NameError on the first logged batch.
  Imported locally in the branch (sys.modules hit).

  EXPECTED:  preload 2733 s -> ~5 min;  /dev/shm 153.6 GB -> ~0.8 GB;
             boundary headroom vs 720 G  ~186 GB -> ~339 GB.  That headroom is
             what currently holds the loader at 12+4 workers, prefetch 4.
             OOM risk goes DOWN: ~153 GB freed against ~20 GB of transient
             fork/buffer cost, and the preload peak is disjoint in time from
             the val->train boundary peak.

  NOT FIXED, deliberately: the 196x read amplification is paid on EVERY launch
  and Pool(64) only hides it.  Real fix is to materialise the K=1 store once —
  ~1.2 GB for all 993, read in seconds forever, small enough for /projects where
  the age-based purge cannot reach it.  Deferred because it is a DERIVED
  artifact and §35.6 exists precisely because one of those silently disagreed
  with its source; it needs a provenance stamp + its own pre-flight, in the
  spirit of §35.28.  It would serve TRAINING ONLY — token_sel="all" still needs
  196 for patch_map_diag and the tile-pair sign test, so both stores coexist and
  "which store produced this number" has to be answerable.

  VERIFIED — job 26082396 (pw_stage2a), same node gcn91:
                          before      after
    preload             2733.5 s     55.5 s    49x
    /dev/shm            153.6 GB    758 MiB    1/196
    bins                    1892       1892    none lost (s2 647/s1_asc 645/s1_desc 600)
    narrowed=true              0       1892    only patch-axis width present is 1
  CAVEAT: same node had read these files 90 min earlier, so pagepool was WARM.
  49x is a best case; a cold node will be slower.  The 758 MiB is not cache-dependent.

  WIDTH CHECK FIRED IN PRODUCTION exactly as designed:
    [diag] patch-map loader ready: 1532 samples, token_sel=all
  It inherits training's shm_dir, found K=1 memmaps, REFUSED them, fell back to
  zarr at K=196 and built.  Without it numpy clips silently and the §35.30 gate
  condition diag/patch_map_sd_mean is computed over one patch.
  Zero fallbacks elsewhere: 0/158886 S2 and 0/290517 S1 acquisitions dropped, no
  "L12 cache: /dev/shm was partial" line.

  Epoch 001  train_loss=0.002224  val_loss=0.002500
             data=3s  compute=205s  gpu_util=98%  peak_vram=11.8GB/100GB

            (§35.30 threshold is >0.98 = collapsed, so this PASSES at epoch 1 —

  data=3s vs compute=205s: the input pipeline is no longer a factor at all
  (§35.23 measured 93-95% util; this is 98%).  Startup 13 min end to end:
  ~1 min pre-flight + 55 s preload + ~10 min dataset build + warm-up.

  §35.31b REPRIORITISED as a result.  Sharing the per-station statics/drivers
  instead of copying them per rank is the right end state and is the SAME work as
  the K=1 store — but memory is not the constraint (65.7/811 GB node, 758 MiB of
  a 378 GB tmpfs, 11.8/100 GB VRAM; a fully shared store would be ~2.6 GB), and
  with data=3s the steady-state loop gains nothing.  It is a ~10 min one-time cost
  on multi-day runs.  Worth doing when the store is rebuilt anyway, or if the
  workflow becomes many short runs; not on its own.

  §35.31c batch size: 11.8/100 GB VRAM means lots of headroom, but lr=2e-4 and
  warmup=1000 were tuned for 128 and §35.30 endpoint 2 compares against a baseline
  trained at 128.  Deliberate follow-up with lr rescaled, NOT a mid-flight knob.

  CAVEAT WITHDRAWN (2026-08-27, job 26083217).  The "49x is warm-cache best case"
  note above was REASONING, not measurement, and it was wrong.  pw_stage2a_L3
  landed on gcn117 -- a node that had never read this store -- and preloaded in
  47.4 s, FASTER than gcn91's warm 55.5 s, ladder matching within a second:
        gcn117 cold    gcn91 warm
    100      8.7 s        9.4 s
    400     29.4 s       31.4 s
    647     47.3 s       55.4 s
  So it is 58x, entirely from the 64-way fan-out.  The serial run's 10.6% CPU had
  already said this: blocked on PER-FILE LATENCY, which parallelism removes and a
  warm cache would only partly mask.  The caveat reached a commit message and this
  log before a free experiment refuted it.

  freed capacity for generalisable structure and val should have moved.  It did
  not.  So memorisation looks like a SIDE-EFFECT, not the cause, and the ceiling
  is set elsewhere.  Supporting: L3 reaches nearly L6's train loss (0.000477 vs

  not even better memorisation.  Underfitting ruled out on the same evidence.

  shrinking the transformer cannot remove it.  Consistent with
  R2_station_mean = -17.5 at 30-100 (ranks val stations worse than the global
  mean would).  ubRMSE is BLIND to this by construction -- it de-means per
  station -- so the selection metric cannot see the failure mode.

2026-09-15 (Session 35) -- §36 ECOSTRESS DTR census: specified, built, running
================================================================================

Planning only at the start; ended with a 44-task array running across all 918
in-range stations. No model code touched.

WHAT WAS DECIDED
  * Thermal arm = ECOSTRESS day/night DTR. FINAL, user decision, stated as not
    to be reopened. Not Landsat, not MODIS, no hybrid.
  * Target = raw DTR (LST_day - LST_night), ONE loss term. The first draft of
    §36.2 mandated subtracting the tile mean per scene; that deletes the
    tile-mean thermal inertia, which is exactly the LEVEL information §35.33.6
    says the model is missing (NSE negative everywhere, NSE-anomaly ~0).
  * Day/night by SOLAR ELEVATION alone (day >+10 deg, night <-5 deg, crossover
    discarded). Clock windows are gone.
  * Pairing by NOCTURNAL PERIOD: the night pass must follow the day pass and
    precede the NEXT day's solar noon. A 10:30 day pairs with 22:30-04:30 and
    never the following evening. Correct at any latitude and season.

MEASURED, AND EACH CONTRADICTS THE DOCUMENTATION
  * v003 has ZERO granules globally before late 2025 (L2T and L2G both), despite
    both catalogue pages advertising "2018-07-09 to Present". That string is the
    DECLARED temporal extent, not an inventory. Use v002. General rule: CMR
    collection metadata states intent; only granule queries report reality.
  * `ECO_L2T_CLOUD` does not exist. The `cloud` layer inside L2T LSTE is uint8
    0/1 -- BINARY, not a bitfield. The v001 "Final Cloud Plus Region-growing /
    bits 2,3,4" recipe decoded against it would read every cloudy pixel as clear.
  * QC bits 15&14 (LST accuracy) read 01 = "1.5-2 K marginal" for 100% of pixels
    at TxSON. The filter demanded >= 10 ("good"), rejecting EVERY valid pixel and
    producing clear_frac = 0.000 network-wide while looking like a cloud problem.
  * An all-zero QC word means UNPOPULATED, not worst-case. Granule 00375
    (2018-07-30, three weeks post-launch) returns a single value 0x0000 over the
    whole window.
  * `view_zenith` is all-NaN on 2 of 3 probed granules while QC/cloud/water/LST
    carry data at the same pixels. A grid check confirmed all five layers share
    one transform, so this is a data property, not a windowing bug. §36.15's VZA
    study may not be executable; the census reports availability.
  * Latitude limit is 54 deg (collection metadata), not the 52 deg in page prose.
    The prose figure was dropping 38 real stations, one a flux tower.
  * A station in an MGRS tile overlap receives each overpass TWICE. Banizoumbou
    straddles 31PDQ/31PDR: 60 granules for 38 passes. Dedup must key on
    (orbit, scene), not (tile, orbit). §29.3 warned about this for TxSON.

THE TWO BUGS THAT WOULD HAVE WASTED THE RUN
  * `end_date` in station_splits.csv is BASIC ISO (20181230). CMR needs extended
    and answers HTTP 400 otherwise, which is in _NO_RETRY_HTTP -- so every one of
    993 stations would have logged status=error, the run would have finished
    cleanly reporting ZERO granules, and --controls would have printed "CONTROL
    PASSED: zero granules above +/-52 deg", because zero is exactly what total
    failure produces. The bug validated itself through the test meant to catch
    it. The selftest passed throughout because it hardcodes a valid ISO literal
    and therefore exercised a different code path than production.
  * The clock-hour day/night windows discarded 271 of 440 TxSON overpasses,
    including the ONLY night granule at Banizoumbou (04:38 local, sun 19.8 deg
    below the horizon -- unambiguously night, and near the pre-dawn minimum that
    classical thermal-inertia work actually uses). Pairs 2 -> 62 after the fix.

SCIENCE CORRECTED AFTER ADVERSARIAL REVIEW (three agents + a live smoke test)
  * §36.1 argued a near-static target is memorisable and therefore harmful, and
    used that single claim to refuse the already-costed global Landsat pull.
    §29.15 line 6360 says the OPPOSITE -- "for LST-as-auxiliary-dense-target,
    stability is a virtue" -- and I had quoted that approvingly earlier in the
    same session before reversing without noticing. Withdrawn.
  * "§29 caveat iv" does not exist. Nor does §35.34 (it is §35.33.6).
  * The "10-25 K vs ~3 K noise" SNR is for ABSOLUTE DTR. The supervised quantity
    is within-tile: §29.13 measured 2.26 K against a ~2 K floor, i.e. SNR of
    order 1, not 3-8.
  * Confounds absent from the first draft and now named in §36.3a: vegetation
    fraction (largest, and worst exactly where the model is worst -- Forest
    0.0563), soil texture, latent/sensible partitioning, and solar-azimuth
    terrain shading, which would PASS the static-vs-dynamic kill test as pure
    nuisance (338 of 918 stations sit above 2 km).
  * §36.0a: the cheapest arm-killing test now runs FIRST, at one station, ahead
    of the census. It had been placed last, behind the census AND the Tier 2
    pull, while being labelled "cheapest test, run it first".

RUNNING AT SIGN-OFF
  Array job 26742514, 44 tasks x 21 stations, 12 concurrent. ~2.6 h/task
  expected. Per-task CSVs; merge with:
      bash slurm/ecostress_census_array.sh merge

  KNOWN BENIGN ERRORS: 16 of 918 stations (1.7%) have end_date before
  2018-07-09, so start>end inverts the temporal range and CMR returns 400. They
  have no possible ECOSTRESS data. Harmless but logged as "error", which is
  indistinguishable from a real failure -- skip them explicitly on the next run.

  DO NOT switch branches or edit census_ecostress.py until the array finishes:
  SLURM reads the script off disk at task start, and 32 tasks had not started.

GIT
  feat/patchwise-temporal: 9a0b208 (eval tooling), 780cfd9 (census), 9acc322
  (runbook). Pushed. Tag `pre-s1-decoder-aux` is the rollback point.
  Now on branch feat/s1-decoder-aux for the S1-in-UNet + spatial auxiliary
  supervision work -- not started.
  eval_output/pw_stage2a_L3/ (13 MB) left uncommitted: it is output, and its
  .csv contents are gitignored so it would commit as a misleading partial.

NEXT
  1. Merge the census, read pairs-per-station against §36.16's floors
     (>=20 pairs, >=150 stations, >=3 years).
  2. §36.0a STEP 0 at one station: is the within-tile DTR field dynamic, or
     ~0.967-coherent like the daytime field? This is a go/no-go on the ARM, not
     a sensor comparison.
  3. S1-derived bands in the U-Net + spatial auxiliary supervision.

2026-09-16  --  Session 36: drawing the ECOSTRESS filter chain (§36.22)
================================================================================

GOAL (user): understand the filtering process PICTORIALLY -- for one site, every
ECOSTRESS image, what each filter step does to it, "what does the solar elevation do
with every image", a flowchart, and a best case vs cases with no pairs.

BUILT
  plot_ecostress_census.py            ~1150 lines, reads only the 3 merged census CSVs
  slurm/ecostress_census_analyze.sh   the §36.17-specified job that had never been written
  fig/ecostress_filter_viz/           43 PNG + 43 vector PDF, 300 dpi, + viz_data.json (2.8 MB)

  Publication styling: scienceplots ["science", "no-latex"], serif, pdf.fonttype 42.
  Stations: BodieHills (best, 91 well-phased), Rothamsted (0 well-phased),
  PSA2Tiergarten (0 pairs at all), Banizoumbou (structurally unpairable).

THE FINDING -- pair yield is capped by ORBITAL GEOMETRY, before QC is ever consulted
  The census log invites reading `n_passed -> n_pairs`. That subtraction is meaningless.
  :956 builds `inwin` by PHASE alone; pair_station re-filters on phase (:605-606) and
  never reads passed_qc; `quality` is attached afterwards at :649 as a LABEL. The chain
  FORKS at stage 5 and rejoins at stage 12 -- n_passed and the pair count are on
  different branches. F2 is drawn fork-and-join for exactly this reason.

  BodieHills, true ordering:
      1850 in-window (931 day + 919 night)
        -> 216 candidate pairs   orbital coincidence  -88%   <- binding
        -> 193 both halves clear cloud                -11%
        ->  91 well-phased       phase geometry       -53%

  Cause: ISS overpass local time precesses through 24 h in ~60 days, so a solar date
  usually catches a station in only one half of the cycle.
      station          dates   BOTH day+night    %      pairs
      BodieHills        1083        184        17.0      216
      Rothamsted        1010         16         1.6       26
      PSA2Tiergarten     632         12         1.9       17
      Banizoumbou         12          0         0          0
  Even over BodieHills' 184 both-dates, sum(min(n_day,n_night)) = 207; 216 exceeds that
  only because 128 pairs take a night on the NEXT solar date. 715/931 day passes (77%)
  and 703 night passes find no partner -- and :625 records neither a row nor a counter.
  Corroboration: dt_hours is bimodally quantised with a HARD GAP (78 pairs at 6-9 h,
  138 at 15-18 h, zero elsewhere) -- two discrete orbital configurations.

  CONSEQUENCE FOR §36.16: raising CLEAR_FRAC_MIN or loosening the QC bits CANNOT
  materially raise pair yield. If the >=150-station floor is missed the levers are more
  stations or a looser PAIRING rule -- and DT_TOLERANCES_H / NEXT_DAY_MODES (:110-112)
  are DEAD CODE, the §36.14 sensitivity grid was never swept. That grid is the lever
  §36.16 would need and it does not exist.

CORRECTION MADE MID-SESSION
  I first attributed Rothamsted's zero well-phased pairs to cloud + phase. Wrong about
  the primary cause: only 16 of 1010 dates carry both halves (1.6% vs BodieHills' 17%),
  so orbital coincidence kills -99% there too. Cloud then cuts 26 -> 4, and the 4 clear
  pairs happen not to overlap the 3 well-phased ones. With n=26 that empty intersection
  is close to luck. I also wrongly said Rothamsted's day passes sit at TST 05-07 with
  the sun 10-14 deg up -- the figure shows they reach +60 deg and span TST 08-16; what
  is true is only that the pairs WHICH FORMED skew to early-morning day halves.

F3c -- UTC -> TST -> elevation, and why longitude decides if the conversion matters
  Rothamsted is at lon -0.378 (Greenwich), so UTC and solar time already agree there
  (median |TST-UTC| = 0.10 h, just the equation of time) and the conversion looks like a
  no-op -- which makes it the WORST station to demonstrate it. BodieHills at -119.126
  (median 8.01 h) is where it earns its keep:
      2020-09-27T23:31 UTC -> TST 15.76 -> elev +23.6 -> DAY
      2021-09-07T07:09 UTC -> TST 23.26 -> elev -45.1 -> NIGHT
  07:09 UTC is local midnight; 23:31 UTC is mid-afternoon. Classifying on the UTC hour
  would invert both. Concrete justification for §36.14 computing geometry on every granule.

ANTI-DRIFT IN THE SCRIPT
  - constants AST-parsed out of census_ecostress.py, never retyped, incl. the computed
    N_PX_EXPECTED (restricted evaluator resolves names/BinOp/int/round). No import, so no
    requests/rasterio dependency and no side effects.
  - every funnel reconciled against its own log row, 8 equalities, sys.exit on mismatch.
    All four reconcile EXACTLY.
  - well_phased re-derived and checked. The rule is abs(hfsn - 2.0) <= 2.5, i.e.
    hfsn in [-0.5, +4.5] -- SIGNED and asymmetric. abs(hfsn) first would wrongly admit
    88 morning pairs at BodieHills alone; the script prints that count.
  - assert n_pairs <= min(n_day, n_night), to catch a future QC-gated pairing refactor.

RECORD-KEEPING GAPS FOUND (fix before the remaining 754 stations run)
  1. dedupe drops (stages 3-4) leave no row -- only aggregate counts; the URs are gone.
  2. unpaired passes leave nothing at all (:625, bare continue) -- the LARGEST loss in
     the chain is entirely unrecorded and has to be derived.

BUGS FIXED WHILE DRAWING
  flowchart arrows pointed upward, against the flow; title/legend collision on F3;
  value/percent label collision on F7b; symlog x on F7b flattered the survivors (now
  linear); manual sharex was printing year labels on all three F3c panels.
