================================================================================
2026-09-16  S36 -- ECOSTRESS pair QC pass: the usable inventory, measured
================================================================================

RESULT. 41,348 usable day/night pairs across 824 stations (both halves
clear_frac >= 0.5). Sweep: 42,555 @0.3 | 41,348 @0.5 | 39,883 @0.7 |
30,177 @0.9 | 20,754 @1.0.

    1,687,708  CMR granules            (dry-run census, job 26790671)
    1,130,571  unique overpasses
       60,461  candidate pairs         (59,783 after dedupe + station join)
       57,424  both halves readable    96.1%; rest is MGRS tile-edge geometry
       41,348  both halves clear       83.3% scene clear rate

Cloud is far kinder than assumed: 83% of scenes clear at 0.5, mean cloud
fraction 0.127 over the 2.24 km box. Pair survival BEATS the independence
prediction at every threshold (41,348 vs 39,877) -- day and night passes hours
apart share weather, which is a small piece of evidence that the pairing is
physically sensible rather than coincidental.

NEW CODE. qc_wellphased_pairs.py reads the masks for exactly the granules named
in the pair inventory -- 119,566 reads instead of the 988,065 a full census
would do, because the census reads ~17x more granules than ever enter a pair.
Resume-safe, sharded, --report-only merges and prints. Driven by
slurm/ecostress_wp_qc_array.sh (8-node array) and slurm/ecostress_wp_report.sh.

BUG FOUND IN well_phased (census_ecostress.py). The night test was
`n_tst >= 2.0` -- one-sided on a quantity that WRAPS at 24. A 20:30 pass has
tst 20.5, which is >= 2.0, so it counted as "pre-dawn". 79% of flagged pairs
had an EVENING night half. Solar elevation cannot fix this: the sun is at -15
deg both at 20:30 and 04:30, while the surface is near its hottest at one and
its coldest at the other. Fixed with the signed offset from solar MIDNIGHT
(WELL_PHASED_NIGHT_MIN = 2.0); `dt_hours >= 12` is an equivalent operational
rule (99.98% agreement). The CSV still carries the OLD column -- use dt_hours.

DECISION: phase is a COVARIATE, not a filter. The thermal arm exists to give
the decoder SPATIAL structure inside the 2.24 km tile, not a temporal signal.
Both halves of a pair share acquisition times, so the DTR map is internally
consistent and phase mostly scales its amplitude. Filtering to true pre-dawn
leaves only ~4,100 pairs / 498 stations -- too sparse. So keep all 41,348 and
feed dt_hours / day_tst / night_offset as inputs; normalise the DTR map per
pair. Near-sunrise/sunset are already excluded by the crossover band.

THROUGHPUT, measured (jobs 26798022 / 26798662 / 26799329).
Every COG open costs ~2.1 s of LP DAAC redirect latency and ~90% of wall time
is opening, not reading -- the window is 32x32 px, about 4 KB.
  shared long-lived layer pool (vs one per granule)  +10% only
  dropping the 4th layer (READ_VZA=False)            no wall gain, -25% requests
  HTTP/2 + multiplex + 64 KB prefetch                WORSE, reverted
  sequential layer reads                             much worse
  SLURM array across 8 nodes                         ~38 reads/s, ~10x
The ceiling is PER CLIENT: 16 workers gave 2.8/s, 64 gave 4.0/s, with ZERO
errors. LP DAAC queues concurrent requests rather than rejecting them, so
latency inflates to absorb them. More threads does not help; more nodes does.
Also: --cpus-per-task=4 --mem=7G saves NOTHING on rome -- a node is shared by
at most 8 jobs, so 16 CPUs is the minimum billable slice. Ask for 16/28G.

MY OWN BUG, found and fixed mid-run. Shards sliced work as todo[shard::nshards]
AFTER reading the checkpoint, but each task starts at a different moment and so
sees a different-length todo list -- the slices then indexed different
partitions and 7,785 of 119,566 reads fell between them ("both halves read" was
83.6% instead of 96.1%). Now sharded on a stable MD5 of the task key, so every
task gives the same granule the same verdict regardless of start time.

NEXT. Optionally tighten the crossover band to -10/+20 (costs ~10% of night
halves, ~21% of day halves). Then the §36.21 signal tests on the 41,348 pairs.

================================================================================
2026-09-21  S37 -- the dt band decision, and TIER 2: the first ECOSTRESS LST ever read
================================================================================

BAND DECIDED: dt in [6,19) h. 39,223 pairs / 741 stations / 78,446 window reads.
Separations are bimodal with an EMPTY gap -- 3 pairs of 41,585 fall in 9-12 h --
so this is the union of the two orbital configurations, not a compromise.
489 stations clear both §36.16 floors (>=20 pairs AND >=3 years) against a
requirement of >=150, so §36.16 passes 3.3x. Pairs span 2018-2025, peak 2020.

WHY NOT THE EARLY BAND. dt 6-9 h retains 0 of 116 stations at |lat| >= 45. Zero.
The two orbital configurations sort by latitude, so picking the early one is a
latitude cut in disguise: +15.3 pp at |lat| 25-40, +6.7 pp Koppen B (arid), and
Europe plus the northern US deleted. dt >= 12 stays within 2.6 pp on every axis.
Also dt <= 12 is 93.8% EVENING night halves -- the surface is still shedding heat
and has not reached its diurnal minimum -- which is the same wrap-at-24 trap as
the well_phased bug wearing different clothes.

FILTERING BEFORE PAIRING BUYS +0.8%. Re-pairing with cloud filtered first gives
19,401 vs 19,250 post-hoc. The pairing is so starved by orbital coincidence that
a day pass rarely HAS an alternative clear night to swap to. The defect is real
and nearly costless.

NEW CODE: read_ecostress_lst.py + slurm/ecostress_lst_{smoke,array}.sh.
Array 26984229 launched, 8 tasks x 64 workers, ~30 min.

THREE DEFECTS THE SMOKE TEST CAUGHT -- by running, not by reading.

1. LST IS NOT uint16 x 0.02. §29.6 and §36.15c both call that "the only factor
   safe to hardcode". It is the spec for ECO2LSTE v001, the SWATH product. The
   TILED product we read, ECO_L2T_LSTE v002, delivers float32 Kelvin, NaN fill,
   scale 1.0. Applying 0.02 gave 5.9 K instead of 295 K. The Kelvin tripwire
   fired on the first run and refused to write the shard -- it existed precisely
   because the mask-only pass had no way to notice a silent 401, and it caught a
   different bug instead. The NASA catalogue page confirms the diagnosis by
   omission: it says cloud/water/view_zenith "are not documented in this product
   specification", because those layers exist only in the tiled product.

2. THE FIRST SMOKE SWEEP MEASURED ITS OWN CACHE. All six configs re-read the
   same 40 granules: 1.76 reads/s cold, then ~20 reads/s for every repeat, which
   is GDAL's VSI cache and the OS page cache, not concurrency. Each config now
   gets DISJOINT granules, and the sample is randomised across stations --
   sorted(tasks) is station-ordered, so the head of the list is two or three
   alphabetically-first stations, fine for latency and useless for availability.

3. clear_frac MUST be computed BEFORE LST validity is folded in. The first
   version ANDed lst_valid into the mask, turning a §36.23 clear_frac of 1.0
   into 0.0 and breaking reconciliation. Split into clear_frac (the §36.23 mask
   exactly) and usable_frac (that AND an LST retrieval exists). Reconciliation
   is now EXACT: 360/360, max|diff| = 0. That is the strongest evidence
   available that the window and the mask did not move between the two passes.

OPERATING POINT, measured cold on a random cross-station sample:
   16 workers -> 2.45 reads/s   32 -> 4.31   64 -> 5.60   (monotonic)
Different curve from §36.23.5, which flattened at 4.0, because this is a 4-open
read rather than 3. _water costs ~15% and is KEPT: it masks water pixels out of
DTR, not merely reconciles. 8 tasks x 64 workers.

THE FINDING THAT MAY MATTER MOST -- LST IS MISSING WHERE QC SAYS CLEAR.
Random 720-read sample:
   mean clear_frac   0.962     QC + cloud + water say clear
   mean usable_frac  0.504     ...and an LST retrieval actually exists
   mean lst_nan_frac 0.458
   162 of 360 reads (45%) are clear but have ZERO LST pixels
Structural cause: an L2T granule is a full MGRS tile but the ISS swath covers
only part of it. Off-swath, LST is NaN while QC reads all-zero -- and §36.0b
deliberately treats qc==0 as UNPOPULATED, do-not-veto, so those pixels pass as
clear. So §36.23's 41,348 "usable pairs" counts off-swath area as clear.
A pair needs both halves. If independent, 0.55^2 ~ 30% survival would take
39,223 pairs to roughly 12,000 -- but the halves are NOT independent in swath
coverage, so that estimate could be badly wrong in either direction. The full
run measures it at the pair level, which is why it was left to finish rather
than re-planned on an estimate. §36.16's 489 stations were computed on the
CLEAR count and will need re-checking against the USABLE count.

Also: ~0.5% of granules have a corrupt or missing layer ("not recognized as
being in a supported file format"), caught and logged as read_ok=0.

NEXT. Consolidate to per-station .npz, then G0: is the DTR field static like
§29's daytime LST (+0.967 coherent against its own annual mean)? That is the
go/no-go on the thermal arm and it runs the moment consolidation finishes.
consolidate_dtr.py and analyse_dtr_sm.py are not written yet.
The soil-moisture labels must come from /projects/prjs1968/zarr_tokens --
the scratch store at dataset.py:45 is GONE, 45,334 empty directories, 0 files.

--- 2026-09-21 sign-off: the pull is mid-flight, not finished -------------------

Array 26984229 was at 52,000 / 78,446 reads (66%), 260 MB, all 8 tasks RUNNING and
healthy when the session ended. It finishes on its own; SLURM mails on END/FAIL.
It is resume-safe -- `sbatch slurm/ecostress_lst_array.sh` picks up exactly where it
stopped, because the checkpoint globs every shard CSV and sharding is a stable MD5 of
the task key. DO NOT delete csvs/ecostress_lst_reads.dtr.s*.csv or the .bin blobs;
they ARE the checkpoint.

SWATH GEOMETRY, measured on shard 0 (3,839 reads) -- the LST loss is ALL-OR-NOTHING,
not gradual:

    46.2%  usable_frac == 0      station fully OFF-SWATH, zero LST pixels
    27.7%  0 < usable_frac < 1   station on the swath EDGE, partial cover
    26.1%  usable_frac >= 0.99   fully covered

An ECO_L2T granule is a whole MGRS tile, but the ISS swath crosses only part of it.
Off-swath the LST is NaN while QC reads all-zero, and §36.0b deliberately treats
qc==0 as UNPOPULATED / do-not-veto -- so those pixels passed as "clear" in §36.23 and
the 41,348-pair inventory counts empty sky as usable. Only ~26% of reads are chips we
can use unreservedly; another ~28% are partial and need a minimum-coverage rule.

FIRST THING NEXT SESSION, before anything else: compute PAIR-LEVEL survival (both
halves usable_frac > 0, and decide the floor for the edge cases), then re-check
§36.16's 489 stations against the USABLE count rather than the clear count. If it
falls below ~150 stations the availability gate no longer passes.

Kelvin then uint8[32,32] mask.

================================================================================
2026-09-21 (session 37) -- the pull finished, and the thermal arm answered
================================================================================

Started by checking array 26984229 from last session's sign-off. It had completed
cleanly, exit 0 on all eight tasks -- and lost 31,329 of 78,446 reads (39.9%).

Every failure was the same string: "not recognized as being in a supported file
format", which is GDAL's message for an EDL login page arriving where a TIFF
should be. Three things said contention, not data:
  * 8,777 granules both SUCCEEDED and FAILED, different stations, same URL
  * failure rate flat across every build (0710-0713: 0.39-0.44)
  * only 13 HTTP codes in the whole run (1x502, 12x503), so GDAL_HTTP_MAX_RETRY
    never fired -- it covers 429/5xx and this is neither

Cause: curl rewrites GDAL_HTTP_COOKIEJAR on every handle cleanup, and 64 outer
workers feeding a 192-thread layer pool all pointed at ONE file. Jar truncated
mid-read, session cookie lost, next redirect lands on the login page. This
corrects §36.23.5's "LP DAAC does not throttle" -- true, but at low thread counts.
The ceiling is the client's cookie jar, not the server.

Fix: one jar per thread via rasterio.Env (thread-local GDAL config), plus a
Python-level retry that deliberately does NOT retry geometric outcomes. And a
trap that would have wasted it: the checkpoint counted read_ok=0 as DONE, so a
plain resubmit says "nothing to do". --retry-failed counts only successes.

Validation 26987623: 600/600 ok, 0 errors, same 64 workers, on keys that had
failed. Then 26987690 cleared the rest: 30,729 reads, err=0, 5 minutes.
Throughput went UP -- the old 14.18 reads/s was mostly fast failures.

THE PULL IS COMPLETE.
   reads    78,446 / 78,446   err = 0
   pairs    39,223 / 39,223   over 741 stations
   blob     401,643,520 B = 78,446 records x 5120 B, exact

Pair-level survival, the number left hanging last session:
   both halves READ            39,223  (100%)   741 stations
   both halves USABLE (>0 px)  11,676  (29.8%)  594 stations
   grid-aligned of those       11,607  (29.6%)  594 stations
29.8% lands almost exactly on the naive 0.601^2, so the halves ARE near-
independent in swath coverage -- last session's worry did not bite.
§36.16 re-checked on the USABLE count: 206 stations clear >=20 pairs and >=3
years, versus 489 on the clear count. Above the ~150 floor, so the gate passes.

consolidate_dtr.py written and run: 741 stations in 56 s. Two gates enforced --
DTR only where day and night share tile AND window offsets (6.8% of pairs do
not; only 0.6% of USABLE pairs), and valid = both halves QC-clear AND both LST
finite, because §37.7 measured LST absent over ~45% of pixels QC calls clear.

Then looked at the field before testing it. RGB came free: the satellite_zarr at
/projects/prjs1968 holds raw S2 at 224x224 x 10 m = the same 2.24 km box. A water
body at LCRA-3 reads cold by day and warm by night -- textbook thermal inertia,
end to end from the blob with no tuning. Masking and sign convention are right.

--- G0, and a method error its own control caught --------------------------------

First run of the kill test returned day LST at +0.337. §29 requires the daytime
control near +0.95, so the STATISTIC was wrong, not the data. §29.15 correlated a
MONTHLY MEAN map against the ANNUAL MEAN map -- both sides averaged over 10-20
scenes, denoised by ~sqrt(n) BEFORE correlating, which is most of why it got
+0.967. Correlating one noisy date against a mean is a different, attenuated
quantity. My leave-one-out correction was right; dropping the pooling was not.

Replaced with split-half reliability (random halves, each averaged, correlate,
200 splits, Spearman-Brown to full n). Shuffled control then lands at -0.000.
LESSON: always carry a positive control whose answer is known. It is the only
reason this was caught rather than published.

G0 RESULT -- FAIL. 455 stations, median 17 dates:
   dtr_k        r_full +0.661   median +0.757
   day_lst_k    r_full +0.672   median +0.777   <- the control
   night_lst_k  r_full +0.636
   SHUFFLED     r_full -0.000
At >=30 dates both climb together (day +0.861, DTR +0.858), so the gap to
§29.15's +0.967 is sampling attenuation, not a difference.

The mechanism in one number: DTR pattern vs DAY pattern = +0.816 (median +0.870),
while DAY vs NIGHT = +0.059. Day and night share no static structure, so the
subtraction cancels nothing -- DTR inherits the daytime pattern §29 already
killed. The thermal-inertia premise fails empirically, not for lack of data.

--- §36.21(iii), DTR vs soil moisture -------------------------------------------

538 stations, 11,154 station-days, qc==0 only, labels from the zarr_tokens backup.
                     depth-avg SM    0-10 cm SM
   WITHIN-station       -0.073          -0.098
   BETWEEN-station      -0.105          -0.131
   POOLED               -0.076          -0.107
No Simpson reversal this time -- all three agree in sign. Sign is CORRECT and not
chance: 62.5% of 464 stations negative, median -0.103, against a 50% coin flip.
§29 could never test this (six stations, +/-0.7 CI). And it is ~1% of variance --
the SAME magnitude as §29.13's daytime -0.077.

Four follow-ups, each provoked by the last:

WEDGE. Equal-count SM bins, so narrowing cannot be sampling. Quantile slopes per
0.1 m3/m3: p5 -0.44, p50 -0.89, p90 -1.98, p95 -2.32. The ceiling falls 5x faster
than the floor. It is a one-sided constraint -- wet soil CANNOT swing, dry soil
may or may not -- i.e. the Ts-VI trapezoid, and why a Pearson r understates it.
Median DTR is also flat below SM ~0.11 then falls: a threshold, not a line.

dt BANDS. dt_hours is BIMODAL: 5,084 days at 6-9 h, 6,065 at >=15 h, FIVE between.
   dt 6-9 h    day_tst 14.1 h (right)   night_tst 20.5 h (wrong)
   dt >=15 h   day_tst  9.7 h (wrong)   night_tst  3.2 h (right)
ISS precession forbids both. So this DTR is never max-minus-min. The band with the
right DAYTIME endpoint wins 3x within-station (-0.174 vs -0.050).

WHICH HALF. within-station r:  DTR -0.098, day LST -0.156, night LST -0.117.
Day LST alone beats DTR in EVERY stratum. The night term SUBTRACTS signal.

SIX STATIONS. The CR200-18 tile, six probes in one 2.24 km window. Every
per-station r negative (-0.36..-0.48), pooled -0.246 -- 4x the global number, and
it is ONE observation: the six DTR series correlate at +0.9962. Between-station
on shared dates, DTR CV = 3.2% where SM CV = 32.8%. Station mean SM spans a factor
of 2.4; station mean DTR spans 3%, wrong sign. DTR cannot tell the six apart --
exactly the job §30.1 needs it for.

PER-STATION r + shuffled null (negative-DTR days dropped, which barely moved it:
median -0.131 -> -0.121, fraction negative 68% -> 72%):
   observed  median -0.121  SD 0.278  72% negative
   shuffled  median -0.001  SD 0.248  50% negative
Same box WIDTH -- only the shift is real. True between-station SD of r is ~0.13
against an apparent 0.28; the extremes all sit at low n. But the STRATA are real
(SE ~0.03): Koppen C -0.234 / D -0.116 / B -0.066; Grass-Crop -0.179 / Forest
-0.090; Low -0.251 / High -0.092. Canopy decouples the radiometric surface from
the soil; arid sites have no SM dynamic range. Best case -- temperate, low, open
vegetation -- is r ~ -0.25, 6% of variance, on 77 of 356 stations. The three
strata are NOT independent, so it may be one effect seen three ways.

DESEASONALISATION -- DECIDED AGAINST, same day. analyse_dtr_sm_deseason.py is
written but WILL NOT be run. Both variables are strongly seasonal and in most
climates seasonally anti-correlated for non-physical reasons, so part of the -0.1
may be climatology -- but no outcome changes a decision. The arm is closed by the
six-station contrast number (DTR 3.2% vs SM 32.8%), which is about CONTRAST, not
correlation magnitude, and holds at any r. Deseasonalising could move -0.1 either
way (in monsoon climates seasonality MASKS the physics rather than faking it) and
neither reading revives an arm that cannot resolve space. Consequence for writing
up: quote -0.098 as a RAW within-station correlation with a stated seasonal
confound of unmeasured sign, never as a clean thermal-inertia coupling estimate.
VERDICT ON THE ARM. Three independent lines agree, none a data-volume problem:
the DTR field is as static as the daytime field and IS the daytime field; day LST
alone beats DTR everywhere; and at sub-tile scale DTR has 3% contrast where SM has
33%. The best available version is daytime LST in the 6-9 h pairing at temperate,
low, open-vegetation stations, r ~ -0.20 to -0.25 on a one-sided constraint. Real,
correctly signed, and not enough to build on.

Written to the runbook as §38 (NOTE: commit messages say §37.8-§37.10; §37.8 was
already taken by §37's Files list -- §38 is authoritative).

Commits, branch feat/ecostress-dtr: 5001919 (cookie fix), 171476e (consolidate +
G0), 308a451 (DTR vs SM), c71d0ad (all stations), d10711e (pooled surface),
314c9af (envelope + dt bands), dbde7ff (six-station tile), 3d7e5f8 (per-station r).

--- 2026-09-21 continued: S1, and the decision to build on it ----------------------

With the thermal arm closed, ran the same battery on Sentinel-1. Tokens were already on
disk; §33.12's decoder design (text/s1processing.md) had never been started. Written up
as runbook §39.

d_VV is §33.12's double-centred anomaly -- each cell minus its own temporal norm, minus
the whole-tile level that day. That is the structural reason to expect it to behave
unlike DTR: the static landscape pattern is exactly what the temporal norm absorbs,
whereas DTR's pattern IS the static one.

THREE ERRORS MADE AND RECORDED so they are not repeated:
  * correlating d (zero temporal mean per cell BY CONSTRUCTION) against ABSOLUTE SM
    returns nothing whatever the sensor does. First run did this: r = -0.002.
    Both sides must be anomalies from their own location's norm.
  * location_group_id is NOT a co-located cluster -- the six TxSON probes inside one
    window carry ids 744/742/739/735/736/746. Grouping on it found 7 groups and missed
    every real cluster. Clusters must be built geographically.
  * §38.10's 3.2% CV was SCENE MEANS over overlapping windows, near-identical by
    construction. Everything in §39 is read at each station's OWN pixel, footprints
    matched at ~70 m.

Scale matters and 210 m is the optimum (70 m 54.9%, 210 m 60.9%, 450 m 53.8% on TxSON).
A scale optimum is itself evidence the signal is real -- noise would not peak in the
middle. No orbit confound: 182 of 184 ASC dates are on one 12-day cycle.

A. TEMPORAL, 878 stations, median 218 dates:
     d_VV  median r +0.067   58.4% POSITIVE   (chance 50%, SE 1.7% -> +4.9 SE)
     d_CR  median r -0.003   49.1%            (null, as it should be)
   Grass-Crop 61% beats Forest 57%; temperate 63%, arid 61%, continental 55%.

B. SPATIAL, geographic clusters: only 4 with >=4 members, 2 usable, BOTH TxSON.
     TxSON_6st  177 dates  mean r +0.192  66.7% positive  (+4.4 SE)
     TxSON_7st  184 dates  mean r +0.093  60.9% positive  (+2.9 SE)
   STRUCTURAL LIMIT, about the archive not the sensor: TxSON is the ONLY network with
   >=4 probes inside one 2.24 km tile. Within-tile spatial skill can be validated at
   exactly one place, for any sensor.

C. PAIR SIGN TEST, 91 pairs, 17,072 trials. Overall agreement 46.53% -- BELOW chance --
   while the mean r of the differences is +0.056, correct sign. Resolves on |dSM|:
     pairs >= 0.5 km apart: agreement RISES with the real moisture difference,
                            49.3 -> 53.1 -> 52.6 -> 52.9 -> 51.7 -> 57.0% (+4.3 SE)
     pairs <  0.5 km apart: FALLS to ~42%, and drags the total below chance.
   Sub-500 m probes are effectively co-located, so a large SM difference between them is
   an instrument/depth artefact, not a gradient S1 could see -- but why that gives
   systematic ANTI-agreement rather than chance is NOT EXPLAINED. One candidate tested
   and rejected: recentring on the pair's shared dates moved it 46.63 -> 46.53, i.e. not
   at all. Do NOT quote 46.5% against S1, and do NOT quote 57% without this.

CONCLUSION OF THE SESSION. Sensor choice is now eliminated as the explanation. DTR is
dead (three independent lines, complete data). S1 is real but weak -- the first signal in
this project with any significant SPATIAL discrimination inside a tile, at r ~ 0.07
temporal and ~0.14 spatial. The two things that actually bound the work are not sensors:
the archive gives one validatable dense site, and §30.1's architecture cannot express
spatial variation anyway (pooling destroys 97-98% of within-tile position variance; the
time-series path arrives as ONE spatially constant FiLM vector; §35's pw_stage2a_L3 came
back as memorisation).

DECISION, taken at sign-off: build S1 into the decoder. §33.12 / text/s1processing.md is
the design. Tagged `pre-s1-decoder` at this commit; per standing practice this is an
architecture change, so branch on day one and leave feat/ecostress-dtr frozen.

Commits: 2d229b7 (§39), eff178f (§38 + log), 2cfb9e7 (§38.12 closed), and the §38 chain
5001919 / 171476e / 308a451 / c71d0ad / d10711e / 314c9af / dbde7ff / 3d7e5f8.

================================================================================
2026-09-21 (session 38) -- two more sensors closed, and the S1 reference window
================================================================================

No code run. A decision session, written up as §40.

STARTED as a question about COSMOS-Europe (Bogena et al., ESSD 14, 1125-1151, 2022):
could a European CRNS network give spatial supervision? NO, and for a structural
reason rather than a practical one. A cosmic-ray neutron sensor integrates the neutron
field over R86 = 130-240 m and returns ONE number; that integration is exactly what
destroys within-footprint structure. Rovers and dense clusters -- the CRNS deployments
that would carry structure -- are explicitly excluded from the dataset. It changes the
SUPPORT of a label, not its dimensionality. Also D86 = 15-55 cm and moves with wetness,
so there is no fixed-depth CRNS value to put in the 0-10 / 10-30 / 30-100 bins.

Found along the way, and left OPEN: 42 stations in station_splits.csv already sit at
CRNS sites via ISMN -- COSMOS-UK 29 (20 train / 3 val / 6 oos), HOBE 5, TERENO 4,
FR_Aqui 3, COSMOS 1. process_station() bins by depth_to and averages all sensors in a
bin, so it is UNVERIFIED whether a footprint-average has been averaged into a point
label at those stations. Cheap to check, not blocking.

SMAPVEX also closed: PALS ~450 m (2016) against a 1600 m nominal, UAVSAR retrieved SM
at ~800 m (the 5 m layer is backscatter only), campaigns ~6 weeks over one or two
regions, none overlapping the station set. A validation snapshot, not a trainable
series.

So: thermal closed (§38), CRNS closed, SMAPVEX closed. S1 is the arm.

THEN MEASURED WHAT THE BUILD ACTUALLY FACES, and it reframes §39.4(2).

model.py HAS NO DECODER. It predicts directly at 160 m -- forward() returns
mu (B, K, n_depths), K=1 training / 196 full map (model.py:853); FiLM was removed from
this arm (model.py:576-583). UNetDecoder with up1-up4 lives only in model_unet.py, the
legacy arm behind baseline-unet-temporal. §34.5 STEP 2 is unwritten.

And that legacy decoder has NEVER had a fine input: skip_L3/L6/L9 are all 14x14,
bilinearly interpolated up to each stage -- 16x by up3 (model_unet.py:265-271). DEM and
LULC never reach it at all. So the worry is not that the architecture averages signal
away; it is that nothing finer than 160 m has ever entered it. S1 c/d/w would be first.

Which makes a coincidence worth noticing: §39's measured spatial optimum was 210 m,
beating 70 m and 450 m. That sits essentially at the patchwise model's native 160 m
output. Feeding c/d/w at l=14 into model.py would use the signal at the scale it was
measured, with no decoder built. Fork between that and the full §34.5 decoder left OPEN,
to be decided against the passes-per-group numbers.

DATA CHECK. The per-pass imagery is on disk permanently:
  /projects/prjs1968/satellite_zarr/{station}.zarr/s1_asc|s1_desc/data
  [N,2,224,224] float16 dB 10 m, VV+VH, NO block averaging on disk -> any l computable
  993 stations ASC (251,310 passes, mean 253), 910 DESC. ~70 GB.
  /projects/prjs1968 is the same inode as /gpfs/work3/0/prjs1968.
The source GeoTIFFs are PURGED -- the zarr is the only surviving copy. Same exposure
class as the checkpoint-backup gap.

THE STING: relative orbit number was never stored. metadata.json -> S1RTC is empty in
994 of 998 stores, and where populated holds only ascending/descending, never the track.
Time-of-day unrecoverable for 989/993. So the §7 mod-12 proxy is not a convenience, it
is the ONLY option, and the +/-6 split-half merge test is the sole guard against residue
collision. It must be RUN, not assumed.

DECISION TAKEN AT SIGN-OFF. c, mu, rho-bar and s are computed from AT LEAST TWO YEARS,
and NEVER from any pass inside OOT. OOT begins 2023 (train.py:280), so the window lies
inside 2016-01-01 -> 2022-12-31. Window is calendar-defined not split-defined (an OOS
station was never in training, so "its training dates" has no meaning). Same rule at
train and eval, or the feature distribution shifts between them and reads as poor
generalisation rather than as a bug. ONE fixed reference, computed once and reused --
preferred over leave-one-year-out because it reproduces deployment and keeps d
comparable across years and splits.

Not a new invention: §35.27 already regenerated era5_stats.json from whole-record to
train-years-only, recorded at train.py:1499 as "the OOT-leak fix". A full-archive c
would put the pipeline in contradiction with itself. And the scar attached to that fix
carries over -- overwriting era5_stats.json in place "silently invalidated every
checkpoint trained before it". So the S1 reference bundle gets SHA'd into CONFIG like
era5_stats/driver_stats (train.py:1505-1512) and is never regenerated in place.

Leakage is confined to c/mu/w by construction: T and r both come from p*, the most
recent valid pass AT OR BEFORE the label date. Also recorded honestly -- the per-pass
influence is exactly -1/N (0.5% at N=200), but a held-out span worth 20% of the archive
contributes 20% of c, so "bounded" was never an adequate defence on its own. Hence a
window rule rather than an argument.

STILL OPEN: two years or seven. The trade runs both ways -- longer is less noisy
(SE(c) = sigma_T/sqrt(N): 22% at N=20, 10% at 100, 5% at 400, against d_VV carrying
r~0.07), shorter is fresher (a 2016-2022 mean is centred ~2019 and four years stale at
OOT; 2021-2022 is one year stale). Speckle is already dead by N=20 -- the block
averaging does it -- so the 20-pass floor protects against the wrong thing. The binding
unknown is passes PER GROUP inside the window: mean 253 is across all tracks and the
whole archive, so 2016-2022 split across 2-6 residues may be 50-150 per group, and two
years 15-40. Dump that before fixing the window. Trap: Dec 2021 is a discontinuity
(S1B failed), so 2021-2022 straddles it and 2020-2021 does not.

CORRECTIONS MADE THIS SESSION:
  - T and T-r are NOT orbit-comparable. T carries the incidence-angle level; T-r removes
    the level but not the geometry-dependent spatial pattern. Only full d is poolable
    (§7.7). A cheap "just feed the image" stage must restrict to the dominant group.
  - §39.3's tile-pair sign test IS written and run (17,072 trials). Notes calling it
    unwritten are stale. Still unusable until the sub-500 m anti-agreement is explained.
  - Self-inclusion: with a fixed window, p* is outside it for OOT samples but inside for
    training samples, where d is shrunk by (1-1/N). Leave-one-out is free from the
    stored sum. Decide once N is known.
  - The §39.4(2) expressivity check was DROPPED BY DECISION, not by argument. Replaced
    by instrumentation in the build: log spatial std of the output per tile from epoch 1
    (§23 scale 0.09-0.52; the Tier-1 0.0065 is obsolete), and run the valid=0 control at
    eval -- that path already exists, so it costs no new code and gives expressivity plus
    attribution in one pass.

NEXT SESSION starts with the §40.6 job: passes per (station, mod-12 group) inside
candidate windows, the +/-6 merge test, split-half r(c) as the static-pattern premise,
the saturation curve over 1-7 year windows, and the drift rate against 2023. One
64-core rome job, read-only, no labels needed so all 993 stations qualify. Fold in the
ERA5 frozen/snow exclusion while the data is open.

================================================================================
2026-09-22  SESSION 39 -- LANDSAT LST AT 100 m IS THE SPATIAL SUPERVISION TARGET
================================================================================

DECISION TAKEN. The dense spatial supervision task is Landsat C2 L2 Surface
Temperature pooled to 100 m, as a SECOND HEAD beside the soil-moisture head on the
shared patchwise trunk. Not ECOSTRESS. Not MODIS. Written up as runbook 41.

This does not reopen 38. The thermal arm stays closed as a PREDICTOR of soil
moisture. What is adopted is a different role -- a dense TARGET whose job is to put
gradient on all 196 patches, which the single-point ISMN loss structurally cannot.

WHY LANDSAT, on resolution against the model's own 14x14 @ 160 m output:
  Landsat  30 m -> 22x22 @ exactly 100 m over the 2240 m tile   FINER than native
  ECOSTRESS 70 m -> 32x32                                        finer, but noisier
  MODIS     1 km -> 2x2   (2240/926.625 = 2.42 px per side)      DISQUALIFIED
MODIS's real advantages -- sun-synchronous 10:30, daily global -- solve problems
that only matter once the target can supervise something. Landsat is ALSO
sun-synchronous at ~10:30, so it carries none of the ECOSTRESS phase problem.

THE DENSITY ARGUMENT, which is the actual case for doing this at all:
  per station-year, patch-values receiving gradient
    soil moisture   ~365,  ALWAYS patch 105   <- this IS 34.4's position leakage
    Landsat ST      196 x ~30 clear scenes = ~5900, spread over ALL 196 positions

DESIGN, four conditions, none optional:
  1. target is the RESIDUAL Landsat_ST - ERA5_skt, not absolute ST. dataset.py:58-61
     already feeds t2m_mean/min/max and skt_mean/min/max, so absolute ST is nearly
     free and the model would just learn a season detector.
  2. lambda by matching GRADIENT NORMS into the trunk, not loss values (34.6's rule).
  3. head_lst DELETED at inference -- no Landsat needed to run the model.
  4. SM metrics reported at lambda=0 alongside, so interference is measured.

MEASURED THIS SESSION (all new):

  ECOSTRESS vs Landsat coherence, 857 date-pairs, 9 colocated grassland clusters:
    ECOSTRESS  day +0.231   night +0.219   DTR +0.223
    Landsat over the SAME windows/months/stations:  +0.19 to +0.93, median ~+0.75
  TWO CORRECTIONS FALL OUT.  The day-night subtraction costs NOTHING -- day, night
  and DTR are equally coherent, so the "DTR is a difference so its noise is sqrt(2)
  higher" argument is WRONG and must not be repeated. And the gap is ECOSTRESS vs
  LANDSAT, not DTR vs ST. Leading untested explanation: ISS pointing gives ~50 m
  geolocation error, comparable to one 70 m pixel; grid_aligned guarantees the same
  NOMINAL footprint between passes, not the same ground.

  PHASE, answering "how do you compute a DTR anomaly with variable overpass time":
  LEVEL is not the problem -- a 2.24 km tile is imaged in one instant, so time of day
  shifts the whole scene uniformly and the double-centring removes it exactly.
  AMPLITUDE varies (contrast is larger at 13 h than 9 h) but Pearson r is
  scale-invariant, so it only matters when d is used as a feature -- fix is to divide
  each scene's anomaly by its own spatial SD.  SHAPE is what bites:
    |dtst_day|  0-0.5h  0.5-1  1-2   2-4   4-24
    r_day        0.322  0.272  0.296 0.238 0.096     corr = -0.215
    r_night      0.138  0.166  0.209 0.215 0.293     (flat to OPPOSITE)
  Monotone in the DAY channel only -- exactly the physical prediction, since daytime
  is solar-geometry driven and night is radiative cooling. But partial: even at
  |dtst| < 0.5 h ECOSTRESS day is +0.32, still far below Landsat. So DO NOT FILTER ON
  PHASE -- carry day_tst as a covariate (well_phased had a wrap-at-24 bug) and treat
  the residual decorrelation as a sensor noise floor, not physics.

  REGISTRATION, verified not asserted. read_ecostress_lst.py:136-138 builds the window
  from the station-centred 2240 m box then .round_offsets() snaps it onto the ECOSTRESS
  grid, so ECOSTRESS and S2 cover the same ground to within HALF A 70 m PIXEL (<=35 m).
  Good enough to see what is where; not good enough to difference.

  COLOCATED GRASSLAND INVENTORY. 161 GRA stations have a usable ECOSTRESS bundle;
  clustering geographically at 1.12 km (connected components, NEVER location_group_id)
  gives 9 multi-station clusters -- two of 6 stations, seven of 2, 135 singles.
  Pair separability computed from PATCH INDICES, not distance: 30 of 46 GRA pairs are
  both on different 160 m patches AND >160 m apart. Two are grid artifacts --
  VairaRanch/US-Var at 10 m and the Northstar pair at 58 m land in different patches
  only because a grid line falls between them. Needed because two points in one 160 m
  patch can be up to 160*sqrt(2) = 226 m apart, and a 10 m pair can straddle a boundary.

  ARM (Lamont-CF1/CF2, 334 m apart): median between-station SD is 28% of the median
  within-station temporal SD. 34.4's ">35%" model target sits near the PHYSICAL
  CEILING for that pair.

  TxSON_6st_01, 6 stations, n=62, DTR vs SM:
    within-station  r = -0.437 [-0.577, -0.342]   correctly signed, CI excludes zero
    between-station r = +0.141 [-0.107, +0.358]   null
  Temporal relation real at one probe, spatial one absent. 38.10 at a single site.

LANDSAT RE-PULL. 29's rasters were on SCRATCH and are GONE -- tree survives with 0
.tif files, same exposure class as 40.3's purged S1 GeoTIFFs. Re-pulled for the 9
clusters to PERMANENT /gpfs/work3/0/prjs1968/data/landsat_st/: 4358 scenes, 136 MB,
~0.5 min per cluster. download_landsat_st_mpc.py gained --extent cluster and
--extent station; OUT_ROOT now permanent.

SELECTION RULES LEARNED:
  - "Clear" must be relative to EACH TILE'S OWN CEILING. An absolute 99% cut discarded
    both 6-station clusters, because TxSON's Landsat clear fraction ceilings at ~82%
    permanently -- 29 measured 18.2% of that tile with no ST retrieval at all.
  - Month coverage is limited by SWATH, not cloud. "07:n=5,best=0.00" means all five
    July pairs were entirely off-swath; no threshold recovers them. JJAS gives 2
    columns at most TxSON clusters, so the season used is MAY-SEP.
  - S2 scene choice must use the cloud mask np.isin(cm,(3,4,5)), never cm != 0, which
    counts water and snow and marks every lakeside or winter station permanently cloudy.

STANDING RISK, recorded rather than argued away. 29 measured the Landsat within-tile
pattern as STATIC (+0.967 across months) and 32.10's gate measured that the terrain /
landscape pattern does NOT control within-tile SM (29.13: pooled +0.167 vs
within-station -0.077). So this head supervises a largely static landscape pattern.
The hypothesis bought is that reproducing it yields per-patch features useful to the
SM head -- NOT that ST tracks SM. If the lambda=0 control shows no SM improvement,
that hypothesis is falsified and the head is dropped, not retuned.
Also: 18.2% of the TxSON tile has NO ST retrieval and that region holds the wettest
station, so the loss must be masked per pixel and the masked fraction reported.
And Landsat C2 L2 ST is DAYTIME ONLY -- no Landsat DTR exists and none is proposed.

FIGURES PRODUCED, fig/gra_thermal/, per colocated cluster (36 files):
  combined_{cid}.png   RGB | ECO day | ECO night | ECO DTR | DTR anom | Landsat ST |
                       ST anom, one row per growing-season month, clearest scenes only,
                       markers annotated with observed SM, SM record full width below
  ecostress_{cid}.png  sm_timeseries_{cid}.png  dtr_vs_sm_{cid}.png  landsat_{cid}.png

NEXT SESSION:
  1. Restart the 993-station Landsat pull (--extent station; cancelled mid-run here).
  2. consolidate_landsat_st.py -- area-pool 30 m ST to the 100 m grid per station,
     store [N,22,22] + per-pixel valid + dates, mirroring consolidate_dtr.py.
  3. dataset.py emits the ST target + mask; model.py gains head_lst; train.py gains
     L = L_sm + lambda*L_lst with lambda by gradient-norm matching.
  4. The lambda=0 control runs as part of the FIRST run, not as a follow-up.

================================================================================
2026-09-22  SESSION 40 -- THE 993-STATION LANDSAT ST PULL, BUILT AND LAUNCHED
================================================================================

Delivered §41.7 step 1.  One decision reversed the stored form mid-session and one of
my own diagnoses had to be retracted; both are recorded below because both changed the
design.

DECISION REVERSED: STORE RAW 30 m, NOT POOLED 100 m.
  §41 had the target as 22x22 @ 100 m.  Pooling -- and masking before pooling -- is
  IRREVERSIBLE with respect to the pixel mask: you cannot recover "mean of pixels with
  ST_QA <= 2" from "mean of pixels with ST_QA <= 3".  That forced the 100 m design to
  fix QC thresholds up front, carry a second "loose" pooling as insurance, and justify a
  cell valid-fraction cut.  Storing raw at native 30 m deletes all three.  NO QC IS
  APPLIED; every threshold is now a training-time sweep.
  Step 0 showed this matters more than expected -- see below.
  Cost: pooling still has to happen downstream (30 m is not reachable exactly from the
  model's 112x112 @ 20 m map, ratio 1.5), and TIRS acquires at ~100 m so the 30 m grid
  carries no finer thermal information.  It just happens where it can be changed.

TARGET IS RAW ABSOLUTE KELVIN.  §41.4's `ST - ERA5_skt` residual and §33.12(e)'s
  double-centring are both DROPPED as the stored form.  Nothing is lost -- the tile mean
  is computable and wrs_path/row is stored -- and it removes a real phase mismatch,
  since ERA5 skt is a DAILY aggregate while Landsat overpasses ~10:30 local.
  WATCH: LST spans 280.9-332.0 K while within-tile spatial spread is ~2.3 K (§29.13), so
  a raw-LST head can score well while learning no spatial structure.  Report its skill on
  the spatially-centred residual alongside the raw loss in the first run.

ERA5 RADIATION, decided but NOT built: add ssrd/strd (DOWNWARD, the atmospheric forcing),
  not ssr/str.  `str = strd - eps*sigma*T_skin^4` is a function of ERA5 skin temperature,
  so "remove skt, add net" puts skt back in disguised; `ssr = ssrd*(1-albedo)` embeds
  ERA5-Land's MONTHLY ALBEDO CLIMATOLOGY, a static spatial field -- precisely the leak
  §41.5 warns about.  download_era5land.py already fetches ssrd/strd into NetCDF, but the
  training path reads a zarr whose era5/values is exactly 19 columns, so this needs a zarr
  rebuild + ERA5_VARS 19->25 in BOTH dataset.py and compute_era5_stats.py + a stats re-run.
  Training is from scratch, so the input-width change breaks nothing.

STEP 0 -- QC YIELD CURVES (27016542, 22 s, 14,712 scenes, 42 stations)
  DFCB CONFIRMED: confidence level 2 occurs ONLY in cloud confidence (2,730,846 px);
    shadow/snow/cirrus are exactly zero.  So "<=1 vs <=2" is one field, worth +0.6%.
  MASK VARIANTS BARELY DIFFER: legacy 0.4791, bit6 dropped 0.4798, water kept 0.4849.
    Water is 0.52% of pixels -- keeping it was never about yield, it was about not
    carving a STATIC hole in a static-pattern target.  Bit 6 fires on 62.5% against a
    derived clear of 48%: looser AND redundant, so dropped.
  ST_QA IS A SCENE GATE, NOT A PIXEL MASK: within-tile std 0.362 K vs between-scene
    1.365 K, ratio 0.266.
  TxSON IS NOT REPRESENTATIVE.  Global median ST_QA is 3.120 K, not §29.13's 2.13 K:
        ST_QA <= 2 K  keeps 0.091      <- a 2 K cut would have been devastating
        ST_QA <= 3 K  keeps 0.481
        ST_QA <= 5 K  keeps 0.798
    Since ST_QA is scene-level that is half the SCENES.  Against §33.12(e)'s ~6% gradient
    density a 3 K cut gives ~3%.  All of this is now a consumer-side choice.
  Climate bias modest: <=3 K spans 0.426 (D) to 0.539 (C).
  §41.6's RELATIVE CLEAR RULE DOES NOT GENERALISE: ceilings are 1.000 at the median here
    (TxSON's ~82% were swath-limited), so `ceiling - 0.03` collapses to an absolute 0.97.
  ST_QA == -9999 and lwir11 == 0 agree to 1.00000.  One no-retrieval mask suffices.

THREE BUGS FIXED, ALL MEASURED
  1. assert_grid_invariants() raised SystemExit when a scene's CRS differed from the
     station's UTM zone, killing 8 of 51 stations in job 27015709 (430 wrong-zone scenes
     at CentraliaLake, 282 at Cascade#2, 254 at CamidelsNerets).  Landsat delivers scenes
     in the SCENE CENTRE's zone.  The smoke measured 654 of 2,161 scenes -- 30% -- out of
     zone, and at Cascade#2 EVERY scene.  Now recorded (native_epsg, reprojected), never
     fatal.  31 UTM zones are spanned across the 993.
  2. stackstac's default snap_bounds=True rounds bounds outward to multiples of the
     resolution ANCHORED AT ZERO.  The Landsat lattice is offset 15 m, so a 76*30 m
     request rendered 77x77 -- the true cause of the ragged 76/77 shapes on disk, which
     the old aoi.json had only described as a symptom.  snap_bounds=False.
  3. Positional slicing of a checkpoint-filtered station list does not tile the work when
     tasks start at different times (read_ecostress_lst.py:579 -- 7,785 of 119,566 reads
     unattempted on array 26800268).  Sharding is now md5(station_id), NSHARDS derived
     from SLURM_ARRAY_TASK_COUNT so it cannot disagree with --array.

RETRACTION -- I WAS WRONG ABOUT THE SAS TOKENS.
  I claimed job 27015709's failures were expired SAS tokens, citing "25x401, 28x403,
  25x429".  Those counts came from a bare grep -oE that matched the log's OWN PROGRESS
  COUNTERS, [401/528].  A correct grep finds ZERO genuine HTTP auth or throttle errors.
  §41.6's "one corrupt asset per cluster, server-side" was RIGHT.  The signature is a
  tile-level read failure -- TIFFFillTile: got 0 bytes, expected 101395 -> "not recognized
  as being in a supported file format" -- on ONE scene while others sharing the same SAS
  signature succeed.  4 of 2,161 scenes in the smoke (0.19%), 36 distinct in the old job.
  Such assets are now counted and SKIPPED, never retried: a dead tile is an answer, and
  retrying cost 110 s per scene.

SMOKE + ZONE REGRESSION (27016908): the same 8 stations the old assert killed, 6 UTM
  zones.  8/8 done, 0 problems, 3.1 min, 2,155 of 2,161 scenes kept.
VERIFY (27016976): 8/8 pass.  The FIRST verify run failed 6 of 8 on "LST min 150 K" --
  that was the CHECK being wrong, not the data.  The arrays are raw, so cloud tops are in
  them by design; the physical range only binds over pixels the decoder calls clear.
  Rewritten that way it measures ZERO clear pixels outside [220,340] K, which doubles as
  confirmation qa_decode() catches what it should.
PREFLIGHT (27017069): 993/993 station dirs already exist -> no orphans; category routing
  matches disk exactly (sm_only 842, sm_and_flux 48, flux_only 103); 0 grid failures.

MAPS + THE CROSS-ZONE WARP (27017096 / 27017111), fig/landsat_st30_check/
  Median LST, temporal SD and emis all mark the same features: at CentraliaLake a cool
  corridor (292 vs 304 K) that is LOW temporal variance (11.5 vs 14.5 K, high thermal
  inertia) and HIGH emissivity (0.99 vs 0.975).  Cloudiest scene reads 223-240 K raw with
  an all-black decoded mask -- raw storage behaving exactly as designed.
  SEASONALITY is textbook: ~280 K Jan -> ~315 K Jul -> ~280 K Dec, all 8 in phase.  A
  wrong scale or broken mask could not produce that.
  NO STATION HAS A PERMANENT NO-RETRIEVAL HOLE (hole_frac 0.000 at all 8), so §29.15's
  18.2% at TxSON looks SITE-SPECIFIC, not general.
  THE WARP COSTS NOTHING.  The naive test (median from reprojected vs from in-zone scenes)
  gave r=0.63..0.94 with up to 2.4 K offset and a SPATIALLY ORGANISED difference -- but it
  is CONFOUNDED: different scenes, different dates, and water has far lower seasonal
  amplitude than land, so a seasonal imbalance alone produces a difference shaped like the
  land-cover map.  Two clean tests settle it:
    A  round trip through the neighbouring zone (DOUBLE warp, no confound):
       r = 0.9965..0.9996, bias ~0.000 K, RMSE < 0.1 K, p95|d| = 0.000 K.
    B  149 SAME-DATE pairs at CamidelsNerets -- WRS sidelap from the same orbit, solar-time
       gap 0.007 h = 25 SECONDS, i.e. the same ground at the same instant read once
       natively and once through the warp: r = 0.99494, bias +0.006 K.
  Cross-zone scenes are usable without qualification.

§41.5 PREVIEW, VISIBLE BEFORE ANY TRAINING: corr(median LST map, emis map) is NEGATIVE at
  every station, -0.18 (Cascade#2) to -0.73 (CentraliaLake, Aniak).  Physically right --
  high emissivity radiates efficiently and runs cooler -- but up to ~50% of the static
  pattern's variance is shared with a STATIC emissivity field.  Run the falsification
  early, not after a training run.

LAUNCHED: array 27017190, all 993 stations, 0-49%12, md5 sharding, 8 workers.
  ~45 stations/min, 0 errors in the first ~100.  Verifier 27017275 and the map check
  27017276 are chained with --dependency=afterany, so they run themselves.

  smoke job was still running.  It recreated the file and appended 6 stale

ST_QA KEEPS RISING AS THE SAMPLE GROWS: 2.13 K at TxSON (§29.13) -> 3.120 K over 42
  stations (Step 0) -> 4.02 K over all 993.  A `ST_QA <= 3 K` cut would now discard WELL
  OVER HALF the archive.  Storing raw is what keeps that a decision instead of a fait
  accompli, and it retroactively justifies the reversal made earlier in the session.

THE FIRST VERIFY RUN FAILED 20 STATIONS.  Three causes, two of them mine:
  (a) REAL DESERTS, 12 stations.  Stovepipe Wells (Death Valley) reaches 354.5 K, Yuma
      345.4 K, Utah/Nevada/Texas sites 343-352 K.  Bare soil at midday genuinely exceeds
      70 C.  My 340 K ceiling was flagging real data as broken.  Raised to 360 K.
  (b) ST_CDIST > 200 km, 2 stations.  Documented range is 0-24000 DN x 0.01 = 0-240 km,
      so 211 and 220 km are legal.  The guard only has to exclude metres.  Raised to 250.
  (c) A GENUINE DATA FINDING, 3 stations.  Three SNOTEL sites reported a maximum of
      exactly 373.000 K.  149.0 + 65535*0.00341802 = 372.99994 -- the uint16 CEILING, to
      four decimals.  DN 65535 IS A SATURATION SENTINEL, NOT A TEMPERATURE, and consumers
      must drop it alongside DN 0.  The verifier now counts saturated pixels and excludes
      them BEFORE the range test, rather than lifting the ceiling to 373 K and going blind
      to real divergence.  Across all 993 the saturated fraction is below reporting
      precision, so this is a correctness note, not a yield problem.

STILL OPEN, unchanged from the list above: the §41.5 falsification across all 993
  (corr(median LST map, emis map) ran -0.18..-0.73 on the smoke 8 -- up to ~50% of the
  static pattern shared with a static emissivity field), gate 6, the ERA5 ssrd/strd zarr
  rebuild, then the two-head wiring with the lambda=0 control in the FIRST run.

--- 2026-09-22 SESSION 40, FINAL: THE QC DECIDED, AND THE MASK BUILT ---

Written up as §42 in the runbook.  The archive was already down and verified (993/993,
253,334 scenes, 4.68 GB).  This part is about what gets SUPERVISED out of it.

THE FILTER CHAIN, in order:

  STEP 0  scene selection at the STAC search       SERVER-SIDE, IRREVERSIBLE
          landsat-c2-l2 ; Landsat 8 and 9 only ; Tier 1 ; eo:cloud_cover < 80 ;
          all 5 assets present.
          Landsat 7 excluded: its Scan Line Corrector broke in 2003, so every scene since
          has diagonal gaps over ~22% of the image, and its thermal band is a different
          file (`lwir`, not `lwir11`).  Tier 1 is the USGS geolocation grade -- pixels
          placed within ~12 m, so the same pixel is the same ground across dates.
          -> 253,334 scenes

  STEP 1  at download, per scene                   IRREVERSIBLE
          product nodata -> NaN, scale/offset from each item's raster:bands, never
          hardcoded.  Corrupt server-side assets counted and skipped.  Per-station Kelvin
          tripwire.
          -> 253,334 scenes stored RAW, NO QC APPLIED

  STEP 2  the supervision mask                     REBUILDABLE
          2a  qa_decode(qa_pixel30): reject bits 0 Fill, 1 Dilated Cloud, 2 Cirrus,
              3 Cloud, 4 Shadow, 5 Snow; cloud/shadow/cirrus confidence <= low.
              Bit 6 NOT required (redundant: fires on 62.5% vs a derived clear of 48%).
              Bit 7 water KEPT and flagged.
          2b  isfinite(lst30)                      -> 180,832 scenes / 697,906,029 px
          2c  250 K < lst30 < 360 K                -> removes 26,776 px (0.005%)
          2d  cdist30 > 1.0 km                     -> 133,127 scenes / 522,675,116 px
          NO ST_QA GATE.

WHY NO ST_QA GATE -- measured over 697,906,029 clear pixels:
  ST_QA IS VERY NEARLY A READOUT OF DISTANCE TO CLOUD.  Pixel median by CDIST bin, over
  nine DISJOINT bins with barely overlapping distributions:
      0-0.09 km 5.875 K | 0.3-0.5 4.375 | 1-2 3.125 | 5-10 2.125 | >20 km 1.625 K
  so gating on both double-charges.  And:
    * the cost is STATIONS and the pixel count hides it: ST_QA <= 3 K looks survivable at
      64.1% of pixels but drops 107 OF 993 STATIONS below 20 scenes.  At 2 K it is 689.
    * there is no pathological tail: at 8 K only 5 SCENES IN THE WHOLE ARCHIVE are cut.
      p10 2.0, p47 3.0, p70 4.0, p96 6.0 K -- a smooth continuum.  The gate separates
      nothing; it shaves the humid end.
    * climate skew 2.98x at 3 K: B arid 0.725 vs A tropical 0.243, E polar 0.316.
  CDIST > 1 km keeps MORE data (74.9% vs 64.1%) at LOWER resulting uncertainty (2.49 K)
  for 7 stations instead of 107, and targets the cause rather than the symptom.
  0.5 km (85.9%, skew 1.28x) was considered and rejected for the stricter choice.  Skew
  rises LINEARLY with threshold -- there is no knee, so the data does not pick a value.

  CDIST IS PER PIXEL, from each pixel's own location -- a 30 m raster of distance to the
  nearest cloud, not a distance from the tile centre or the station.  Larger is further
  from cloud, so > 1 km is the STRICT direction.

  ACCEPTED COST: 28% of scenes are PARTIALLY masked (43.1% fully kept, 28.9% fully cut),
  the partial fraction spread thinly across 0-1.  I had claimed "nearly all-or-nothing"
  from 74.9% of pixels surviving against 73.7% of scenes; that match is coincidental and
  the direct measurement says otherwise.  It is accepted because the masked direction
  depends on which side the cloud was on and averages out over ~200 scenes, and where it
  does not the effect is on supervision COUNT per cell, not on target values.
  -> REPORT PER-CELL OBSERVATION COUNTS.

THE MASK IS BUILT AND STORED (build_landsat_mask.py, job 27022056):
  {folder}_st30mask_{start}_{end}.npz beside each bundle -- packbits `mask` and `water`,
  993 files, 22 MB TOTAL against a 4.68 GB archive.
  csvs/landsat_mask_index.csv, one row per (station, date), so sample selection never
  touches the archive.
  133,127 supervised scenes, 522,675,116 supervised pixels, 3,926 of 5,776 px per
  supervised scene (68%).  Per station min 2 / median 110 / max 579; 7 below 20 scenes.

THE 250-360 K GUARD, since it is easy to mistake for arbitrary: 360 not §29.5's 350
  because bare desert genuinely reads 354.5 K at Stovepipe Wells and 345.4 K at Yuma --
  350 flagged real data from 12 stations as broken.  360 still excludes DN 65535 ->
  372.99994 K, the uint16 SATURATION SENTINEL, which three SNOTEL stations reported as
  exactly 373.000 K.  Consumers must drop DN 65535 alongside DN 0.  After the cloud mask
  the guard removes almost nothing (0.005% of pixels), which is itself evidence the cloud
  mask works.

WHAT THE SURVIVING ARCHIVE LOOKS LIKE (fig/landsat_st30_check/inventory.png):
  per station min 2 / p10 42 / median 110 / p90 258 / max 579 supervised scenes.
  Koppen: D 41.5%, C 30.8%, B 27.0%, A 0.4%, E 0.3%.
  IGBP macro: Grass-Crop 43.7%, Forest 40.9%, Shrub-Savanna 9.9%, Other 5.4%.
  ENF alone is 36% of scenes by IGBP class.  Geography is US + Europe.

TWO DESIGN QUESTIONS CLOSED (§43, written to the runbook).

  ARCHITECTURE: the baseline-unet-temporal decoder, not the patchwise trunk.  Not
  because patchwise lost -- it has one run and that verdict was memorisation, a
  supervision-geometry failure both arms share -- but because the U-Net is the only
  arm that emits a map, and a dense target needs somewhere to land.  What it could
  not do was build that map from tile-pooled features (§27a.2: 97-98% of within-tile
  variance destroyed).  So the decoder is KEPT and its INPUTS are replaced.

  DECODER INPUTS: raw imagery, 19 ch at 112x112 @ 20 m, REPLACING the L9/L6/L3 skips
  (not appended -- 19 channels against a 1024-wide input is 1.8%, which is §35.18's
  concat objection in another form).  10 S2 bands + NDVI/NDWI/NBR computed at 10 m
  THEN pooled + s2_valid + raw VV/VH + s1_age + s1_valid + orbit.  No DEM (absolute
  elevation is a station fingerprint).  Both selections CAUSAL.  up4 dropped, so the
  supervised pixel moves 112,112 -> 56,56.  LST head at 22x22 @ 100 m.

  §34.5's premise -- "without raw S2 10 m bands the seven S1 channels are the ONLY
  information doing the 160->20 m work" -- is FALSE.  /projects/prjs1968/satellite_zarr
  survived the scratch purge: 998 stations, 247 GB, 12-band S2 + S1 + DEM + LULC at 10 m.

  LST STAYS AUXILIARY (considered and rejected as a product).  It would be a temporal
  gap-filling product, and claiming it means first beating ERA5_skt + a(t)*M_static on
  a split-half -- strong by construction, since §29 measured the pattern as +0.967
  static.  That baseline is step 1 if it is ever reopened.

TWO BLOCKERS FOUND, NEITHER NEW CODE.
  (a) dataset_unet.py:40 ZARR_ROOT points at purged scratch -- 24 MB, 0 .complete of
      993.  The dataset builds ZERO samples today.  zarr_tokens is 1.4 TB (1.2 TB
      sm_only) and is the ONLY copy, so REPOINT at /projects rather than re-stage;
      §43 makes that cheap by removing the l369 memmap reads entirely.
  (b) S2 +1000 DN harmonisation of satellite_zarr is UNDETERMINED.  The attrs string
      is hardcoded and proves nothing.  Settle by reading pixels in a batch job.

§43.12 ERA5 DRIVERS 19 -> 18, BUILT AND RUNNING.
  Rule: state variables get mean/min/max, accumulations get a daily SUM.
  skt OUT (a modelled diagnostic, and the ST target at 9 km), ssrd_sum/strd_sum IN.
  DOWNWARDS not net: str contains the surface's own upward emission, i.e. the Landsat
  target, and would reintroduce exactly the leakage dropping skt removed.
  Sum not mean/min/max because a 24 h total survives the UTC-day boundary and a daily
  max does not -- ERA5-Land days are UTC and cut the local diurnal cycle.

  Never fetched: download_era5land_gee.py (the path that produced all 993) has 7 bands
  and no radiation.  So: fetch TWO bands and splice, never re-fetch the other 16 --
  that would resample a different GEE snapshot and move the SM model's inputs for
  reasons unrelated to this change.  era5/values18 is written BESIDE era5/values.

  Blocked first on a dead Earth Engine refresh token (invalid_grant; earthengine
  authenticate needs --auth_mode=notebook, there is no gcloud on Snellius).

THE DEFECT THE PLOT CAUGHT AND THE RANGE CHECK DID NOT.
  Smoke 1 ran the three STRATEGY_BUFFER stations deliberately, as the riskiest path.
  check_era5_radiation.py PASSED them -- magnitude, seasonality, day counts, latitude
  ordering, all fine.  The plot showed a 16-day window in March where ssrd_sum halved,
  and a September step, AT THE SAME DOY IN ALL THREE STATIONS IN EVERY YEAR.  Sites at
  18N, 37N and 59N cannot share a discontinuity on one calendar day.

  MEASURED CAUSE: getRegion on a 25 km buffer returns one row per (pixel, hour) and
  WHICH PIXELS CARRY DATA CHANGES DAY TO DAY.  Combate's buffer holds 17 pixels, only 3
  valid year-round; Mar 5-20 another 4-6 blink on and they are systematically darker.
  Averaging "whatever is valid today" tracked the ERA5-Land mask, not the weather.
  NO HOURS WERE EVER MISSING -- every day returned all 24.

  FIX: pin each buffer station to the pixels valid in every hour of four seasonal probe
  months, persisted to csvs/era5_buffer_pixels.json.  Verified against the kept pre-fix
  files: already-correct days return BIT-IDENTICAL while the broken band is repaired
  (11.97,12.20,10.03 -> 22.32,23.34,24.23); month std 7.04 -> 1.70 MJ.
  Also min_count=24 so an incomplete day is NaN, not a quiet partial sum; at load it
  takes dataset.py's existing 15% ERA5-dropout path (value 0, doy 0 = padding).

  METHOD NOTE WORTH KEEPING: the artefact was narrow enough not to move the annual
  mean, so no bounds check could see it.  PLOT THE SMOKE.  slurm/plot_era5_radiation.sh
  is now step 2b of the run order and marked NOT OPTIONAL.

THREE STATIONS EXCLUDED, 993 -> 990.
  Combate (val), PortGraham (train), Cape-Charles-5-ENE (oos) sit on ocean-masked
  ERA5-Land cells.  The pinning works, but a pinned pixel is the nearest VALID LAND --
  Combate's is 13-24 km away, which is not the station's meteorology.
  splits 993->990 (train 665, val 90, oos 234, dup 1); excluded 35->38.
  The pinning CODE stays: correct, tested, and what any future buffer station needs.
  NOT YET CHASED: eval_predict.py EXPECTED_STATIONS and every doc stating 993.

CORRECTION MADE MID-SESSION, worth keeping because the first reading was wrong.
  The Ngari shortfall was first explained as "extreme cold and dry days".  The
  DAILY plot falsified that, and the elevation story too.  Two different things
  were being conflated:

    * the Brutsaert shortfall at Ali is SUMMER-CONCENTRATED, roughly May-Sept,

  in the same arid western basin.  Neither aridity-vs-Naqu nor thin-column
  altitude survives.  OPEN.  Needs Dilley-O'Brien or Brutsaert + Marks-Dozier
  before the 95.9% is quoted as clean.  It does NOT block the splice: the
  radiation series themselves show no artefact.

§45 WRITTEN TO THE RUNBOOK -- all-station driver QC, planning only, nothing built.
  Motivation: the ONLY test that has touched all 990 is a min/max range check, and
  that is the instrument that (a) produced both false alarms above and (b) let the
  buffer-pixel defect through in §43.12.  Everything plotted this session covered
  the 35 stations that tripped a threshold.  The other 955 have had no plot-level
  check.  And the 16 CARRIED driver columns predate §43.12, have never been
  QC-plotted, and already feed every training run to date.  SCOPE = ALL 19 COLUMNS.
  Method: do not plot 990 series -- build quantities whose correct value follows
  from first principles and put all 990 against them in one image.
  Centrepiece is TOA insolation H0 from latitude and DOY alone, giving three tests:
  Kt = ssrd/H0 <= 1 (hard), Kt sensible by Koppen, and polar night as an EXACT
  per-station equality count(H0==0) == count(ssrd==0) -- upgrading the 4-point
  latitude coincidence above into a 990-station identity.
  Highest-value single check is sp_mean vs elevation_m (barometric): it tests the
  RIGHT PIXEL, which is the buffer-pixel defect class and is untested by anything
  else in the pipeline.
  H0 does not exist in the repo (zero hits: extraterrestrial, solar_constant, 1361,
  insolation, daylength) but plot_ecostress_census.py:224 declination_deg and :229
  tst_at_elevation already give declination and the sunset hour angle -- ~6 lines.
  Gotcha measured: koppen_geiger has a CASE BUG, BSk(168) vs Bsk(11), BSh(6) vs
  Bsh(3); normalise before grouping.  station_name has embedded commas -- pandas
  only, never awk -F,.

SESSION 42 -- 2026-09-23 -- the in-situ target side (ISMN QC, inventory, Mead)
================================================================================

Scope was the soil moisture TARGETS, not the input arms.  Written up as runbook
§44 (§44.1-§44.8).  ERA5 radiation job 27030181 COMPLETED overnight (6h12m) and
is untouched -- its check -> PLOT -> splice -> verify -> stats chain is still the
next item on the other track.

WHAT THE ISMN QUALITY FILTERS ACTUALLY ARE (§44.1).
  Observation level: flag whitelist G or D*; average sensors by depth_to; drop
  all-NaN depths; mask days with <6 sub-daily obs; bin to 0-10/10-30/30-100 cm.
  Station level: longest run with gaps <=7 d bridged; trim to the surface window
  and drop depths under 95% coverage; duration >= 1095 d; climatological
  month-day gap-fill; skip if any NaN survives.

THE WHITELIST IS A WHITELIST (§44.2).
  There is no C filter.  C01-C03 and M are dropped by OMISSION.  Keeping D* is
  defensible -- D codes are ancillary-driven (GLDAS soil temp, gridded precip),
  so dropping them would delete frozen winters and, because filter 9 gap-fills,
  would substitute climatology for suspect-but-real data.
  THE DEFECT: str.startswith("D") is order-sensitive on comma-joined fields.
  "C01,D01" drops, "D01,C01" KEEPS -- same impossible value, decided by string
  order.  Unverifiable now (see below).  Fix is content-based rejection.
  NOT APPLIED -- awaiting the go-ahead.

THE RAW ARCHIVE IS GONE (§44.3).
  /home/khanalp no longer exists; zero .stm files anywhere on the filesystem.
  Level-1 survives in TWO genuine copies (raw_soil_moisture 1324, links=1;
  level1_organised 1048) plus level1_data.zip.  The ISMN flag code does NOT
  survive: the NetCDF carries only qc 0/1/2, so a G day and a D07 day are both 0
  forever.  Level-1 is now an irreplaceable INPUT, not an intermediate.
  audit_ismn_flags.py + slurm/audit_ismn_flags.sh written, ready for the day an
  archive exists, archive path as $1.

station_metadata.csv SURVIVED but is STALE (§44.4).
  /gpfs/work3/0/prjs1968/raw_soil_moisture/station_metadata.csv, 2393 rows,
  1310 saved / 1068 skipped.  Skip reasons: 525 "<1 year of valid daily data",
  432 no surface run, 109 NaNs after gap-fill, 3 no valid data.  That "<1 year"
  string is NOT in the current code (:228 says 3 years), which looked like short

  MEASURED, job 27044035 (audit_station_duration.py, reading n_days from each
  NetCDF, not from any CSV): 0 of 990 under 1095 days, MINIMUM EXACTLY 1095.
  So the 3-year rule IS enforced; metadata.csv describes an earlier 365-day run
  against a level-1 store that was later regenerated.  Use it for skip
  CATEGORIES only.  CLAUDE.md's "365 days" is stale and should read 1095.

    US-Ne1  36 SWC cols  2001-06-12..2025-12-31  8963 days
    US-Ne2  36 SWC cols  2001-06-20..2025-12-31  8963 days
    US-Ne3  55 SWC cols  2001-06-15..2025-12-31  8963 days
  3-4 profiles x 4-5 depths, 85-98% valid, hourly, ~24 years.  has_swc=False is
  an artifact of FLUXNET, not a fact about the site.  Ne3 downloaded fine under
  CCBY4.0, so data policy was NOT its exclusion reason.

  on 0-10/10-30/30-100 cm.

  DECISION: US-Ne1/Ne2/Ne3 are RESERVED FOR VALIDATION, not training.  Three
  co-located treatments (irrigated continuous, irrigated rotation, rainfed)
  within ~600 m sharing one satellite tile, differing only in water management,
  is a controlled test of whether the model resolves irrigation-driven
  within-tile differences -- the sub-tile claim §34/§41 exist to support.  They
  already sit in oos and fall in one location_group_id, so the group moves
  together, which is what this use needs.

  TWO FLAGS WERE MY BUGS, NOT THE DATA.  Recorded because the debugging is the lesson:
  1. "negative precipitation, 966 stations" -- PROBED the magnitude instead of
     trusting the count.  Most negative tp anywhere across 2.46 M station-days is
     -3.9e-8 m (39 NANOMETRES) against a typical 2.1 mm wet day; ZERO stations below
     -1 micron.  float32 IFS accumulation noise.  My -1e-9 threshold was meaningless.
  2. "polar-night mismatch, 24 stations" -- my FIRST FIX MADE IT WORSE.  I applied the
     -0.833 deg sunrise convention; at 67-69N that lets the midwinter sun clear the bar
     and collapses the count to 0.  GEOMETRIC horizon was right all along.  Real
     residual is -1.0 to -6.5 d/yr, mean -3.9, ALWAYS NEGATIVE across 24 stations --
     ERA5 twilight on boundary days.  Test is now two statements: observed zeros can
     never EXCEED geometric sunless days (hard, one-sided), and the deficit is bounded
     PER YEAR, not in absolute days (an absolute tolerance just penalises long records).

§45.12/45.13 ELEVATION vs MERIT DEM.  jobs 27047930, 27048477.  990/990 tiles sampled,
  0 tooling failures.  MERIT elv is BAND 4 of merit_hydro_25km.tif.

  True 669-879 m (Duero basin).  Three are in oos.
  ROOT CAUSE: enrich_station_inventory.py:192 gates its SRTM back-fill on .isna().
  -99.9 and 0.0 are NOT NaN, so the fill skips them and the sentinel flows through
  create_evaluation_splits.py:351-355 into elevation_band, where elev_band(-99.9)="Low".
  19 stations are in the WRONG elevation_band today (15 train, 4 oos).
  CONTROL PASSED: >3000 m SNOTEL agree with MERIT to -11.4..+0.2 m while disagreeing

  smoothing rather than flagging every mountain.
  171 stations match MERIT to <1 m = the SRTM back-filled subset, NOT independent
  confirmation (MERIT is itself SRTM3-derived).  Reported separately.

SESSION 42 addendum 3 -- the ZARR_ROOT problem, measured properly (2026-09-23)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Surfaced by compute_era5_stats.py refusing to write.  Investigated because "the
ZARR_ROOT repoint" has sat on the blocker list as a one-line fix, and it is not one.

THE SCRATCH STORE IS SKELETONS, AND EVERY STRUCTURAL CHECK PASSES.
  /gpfs/scratch1/shared/pkhanal/zarr -- purge timestamps Sep 20 02:15-05:11.
  All 993 station dirs EXIST.  era5/ exists.  era5/values/ exists.  s2/l3|l6|l9|l12
  exist.  Every one of them is EMPTY: no chunk files, no .zarray.
  MEASURED: 0 of 993 stations have an era5/values/0.0 chunk.
  `du` reports 51 GB of allocated DIRECTORY BLOCKS, so a size check does not reveal
  it either.  (An earlier note recorded 24 MB; both readings agree it is skeletons.)
  Live store /projects/prjs1968/zarr_tokens: 993/993 .complete, 1.4 TB.

  CONSEQUENCE, and this is the real problem: the .complete sentinel is not one guard
  among several, it is the ONLY thing between a training run and a zero-data store.
  It fails by returning None PER STATION (dataset.py:190, dataset_unet.py:134).
  Nothing raises.  A run does not crash -- it builds 0 SAMPLES AND PROCEEDS.
  compute_era5_stats.py survived only because it happens to carry an explicit
  end-of-run guard ("FATAL: no station produced usable ERA5 statistics -- refusing to
  overwrite era5_stats.json").  Without that it would have written a normalisation
  file computed from nothing and every downstream z-score would be silently wrong.

ZARR_ROOT IS ~40 HARDCODED COPIES, SPLIT 30 SCRATCH / 10 LIVE.  Not one constant.
  Plus a STALE reference: create_token_zarr.py:43 comments "matches dataset.py
  ZARR_ROOT_FAST" and no ZARR_ROOT_FAST exists anywhere in the repo.
  Analysis scripts already work around it by monkey-patching after import --
  plot_dtr_vs_sm.py:39, plot_txson_six.py:39, analyse_s1_spatial_cv.py:43,
  analyse_txson_six.py, plot_gra_thermal.py, and now compute_era5_stats.py.

WHY IT IS A DECISION, NOT A TYPO FIX.  Scratch was the fast SSD staging path.
  Steady state is ~31 MB/sample served from /dev/shm, so repointing at /projects
  moves training reads from SSD to GPFS -- a throughput question, not just a path.
  Three options, genuinely different trades:
    1. repoint to the live store        -- correct now, unknown throughput cost
    2. re-stage 1.4 TB back to scratch  -- restores SSD, but scratch purged once
                                           and will again
    3. env var, live store as DEFAULT   -- one source of truth, and a re-stage
                                           becomes SM_ZARR_ROOT=... not 40 edits

RECOMMENDED, NOT APPLIED (repointing the training loaders is the user's call):
    ZARR_ROOT = Path(os.environ.get("SM_ZARR_ROOT", "/projects/prjs1968/zarr_tokens"))
  plus FAIL LOUD AT THE ROOT -- check once at dataset construction that the root holds
  a non-zero count of .complete stations and raise if not.  Per-station None is correct
  for ONE incomplete station; it must never be how a wholly-missing store is discovered.
  This second half is worth doing regardless of which path wins, and it kills the whole
  silent-zero-samples class.

dataset.py and dataset_unet.py were NOT modified.

SESSION 43 -- 2026-09-23  (planning only; §46 written and approved, no code)
============================================================================

STARTED from the §43 plan and one question: the decoder is about to be fed raw S1/S2/DEM, and
  TerraMind normalisation constants already exist -- can we reuse them?

ANSWER: YES, and they were already in the repo.  precompute_terramind.py:76-87 holds
  _NORM_MEAN/_NORM_STD, lifted verbatim from terratorch's v1_pretraining_{mean,std}
  (terramind_register.py:199, :254) and applied as (x-mean)/std at :136-141.  The backbone does
  NOT normalise internally -- that is the whole contract.  S2 in DN, S1 in dB, DEM in metres:
  the SAME units as satellite_zarr, so no conversion.  External corpus, so no split leakage.
  And the replaced skips stay commensurable with the L12 bottleneck that produced them.
    S2L2A mean [1390.458 ... 1857.648], std [2106.761 ... 1334.311]   (12 bands, DN)
    S1RTC  mean [-10.93, -17.329]   std [4.391, 4.459]                (dB)
    DEM    mean [670.665]           std [951.272]                     (m)
    LULC   identity (v1_5 stats are mean 0 / std 1)

THE REAL GAP was not the constants.  §43 specifies the channels, their dtype and their pooling
  and specifies NO NORMALISATION FOR ANY OF THEM.  The stack mixes int16 DN, dB backscatter, a
  day count, 0/1 flags and raw metres into one shared Conv2d.  patchwise_math.md:294-310 already
  records this exact failure once (TWSA at +/- tens of cm and soil at ~1300 entering a stream
  whose ERA5 tokens were N(0,1)).

AND THE EXISTING BatchNorm DOES NOT COVER IT.  _ConvBlock (model_unet.py:173-186) is
  Conv3x3(bias=False) -> BatchNorm2d -> ReLU, and skip_proj (:214-218) is a bare 1x1 with no norm.
  BatchNorm normalises each OUTPUT channel AFTER the conv has summed across inputs, so a channel
  entering at std 50 beside one at 0.3 owns ~99.6% of the sum and rescaling preserves that ratio
  exactly.  BatchNorm fixes magnitude, never mixture.


§43.3(b) IS CLOSED -- THE +1000 DN HARMONISATION DID HAPPEN, and the answer was in the logs
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The user said "I think we already correct for that 1000 DN right?"  They were right.
  /gpfs/work3/0/prjs1968/data/logs/harmonize_s2_23183555_*:
    array 23183555, 1028 tasks, 2026-05-28 ~15:54
    978 tasks "Done."  -- 235,651 tiles rewritten, 20,394 already-done, 0 ERRORS
    50 tasks crashed, ValueError: not enough values to unpack (expected 3, got 2) at :129.
       COSMETIC: harmonize_station returns a 2-tuple on exactly its two no-op early exits
       (:52 no S2L2A dir, :57 no tile with stem < 20220125) and a 3-tuple after doing work.
       All 50 needed nothing.
    convert_satellite_to_zarr.py:42 reads that same directory; :95 copies verbatim, no offset.
    MEASURED: s2/data min = 1002 (AmeriFlux_CA-Cbo).
  So the store is baseline-4.0: 1000 DN = 0.0 reflectance, 11000 = 1.0, 0 = nodata.

  RESIDUAL RISK, kept as a free by-product of the fine_stats pixel pass: the idempotency guard
  skips any tile whose min non-zero DN is already >= 1000, so an unharmonised BRIGHT ARID tile
  could have been skipped.  Right test is per-band tile medians either side of 2022-01-25, not a
  per-scene minimum -- it also catches partial harmonisation.

  METHOD LESSON, and it cost a session of design: this sat on the blocker list as UNDETERMINED
  while the answer was in the SLURM logs the whole time.  Check whether the job logs still exist
  before designing a measurement.  Provenance beats inference -- again.


WHAT ELSE THE SESSION FOUND
---------------------------
LULC MAY BE REMAPPED TWICE.  download_s1_lulc_mpc.py:404 writes already-remapped TerraMind
  indices (its docstring says "uint8, TerraMind indices 0-9") and precompute_terramind.py:268 /
  retokenize_satellite_zarr.py:203 apply _LULC_REMAP again.  The map is NOT idempotent
  (4->3, 6->9, 3->9, 9->7), so a second pass relabels Crops->FloodedVeg and Rangeland->Snow/Ice.
  This is why §46 takes LULC from the RAW raster and one-hots it: one-hot is agnostic to
  labelling if it is consistent, where a learned embedding of scrambled classes is not.

THE FROZEN _unet ARM NORMALISES ONLY ERA5.  No driver_stats at all (0 hits), so SIF, TWSA
  (+/- tens of cm) and soil (bulk density ~1300, pH x10, clay %) go in unnormalised.  User chose
  to PORT it.  The lambda=0 control inherits the change automatically (same dataset file), so
  attribution to the aux head stays clean; what becomes a 3-variable diff is the comparison
  against the OLD baseline checkpoint.

STAR RESIDUAL AND use_cls_depth ARE THE SAME SWITCH in the baseline, default OFF.  With it on you
  get depth_tokens + per-depth FiLM + heads[1:] zero-init as offsets from heads[0].  There is also
  a live diagnostic (train_unet.py:1272-1291, §18.7/§19.4) watching whether the mechanism is INERT.


§46 WRITTEN AND APPROVED -- decisions, all the user's
-----------------------------------------------------
  1. RE-STAGE the 1.4 TB token store to scratch, not repoint.  + stage satellite_zarr (247 GB).
  2. SKIP the §41.5 emissivity falsification and the §35.33.6 statics ablation; build and train.
  3. NO NDVI/NDWI/NBR  -> and with them the whole (DN-1000)/10000 reflectance branch disappears.
  4. LULC = 10-ch ONE-HOT AREA FRACTION from the raw 10 m raster, NOT Conv2d(768,8,1) on tokens.
     (L12 tokens are consumed unscaled at per-element std 4.64 with 77% of squared magnitude in
      6 register dims -- 8 channels projected from them would arrive ~5x hot and mostly artefact.)
  5. RAW IMAGERY ONLY -- a scale-matched hybrid (L9 embedding at 14x14, zero-init, as a free
     ablation) was proposed and DECLINED.
  6. PORT driver_stats so SIF/TWSA/soil are z-scored.
  7. use_cls_depth ON, STAR RESIDUAL REMOVED -- three DISCONNECTED per-depth heads.
  => 27 channels, superseding both §43.4's "24" and §43.14's "28".

TWO THINGS THAT BECOME MANDATORY BECAUSE OF (7):
  * per-depth label_mean bias init.  Without the residual, the old zeros_ init means "predict
    ZERO moisture", not "same as surface".
  * log n_stations per depth.  The >=95% coverage filter drops depths per station, so disconnected
    heads for depths 2 and 3 train on strictly fewer stations and will be noisier.  That is the
    accepted cost of (7), and the per-depth curves are uninterpretable without the counts.

ON 20 m, STATED SO IT IS NOT OVERCLAIMED LATER: nothing in the loss supervises 20 m.  SM gives ONE
  labelled pixel of 12,544; the LST target is 100 m.  And 100 m is not tightenable -- Landsat st30
  is a 30 m deliverable but TIRS ACQUIRES at ~100 m, so dense thermal supervision is capped by the
  sensor (ECOSTRESS at 70 m was the only finer option and that arm closed on G0).  Claim 100 m;
  treat the 20 m stage as decoder capacity and the 20 m map as qualitative.
